L^{\prime}=L\cup L_{E_{{\rm new}}}
, where
L_{E_{{\rm new}}}=V(E_{{\rm new}})
so that the
C_{E_{{\rm new}}}
-vertices have themselves as landmarks,

•

({\cal N}^{\prime},{\cal R}^{\prime},\Pi_{{\cal R}^{\prime}\to\widetilde{G}})=({\cal N},{\cal R},\Pi_{{\cal R}\to G})
.

It can be easily checked that
(C^{\prime},L^{\prime},{\cal N}^{\prime},{\cal R}^{\prime},\Pi_{{\cal R}^{\prime}\to\widetilde{G}})
has the same quality parameters with those of
(C,L,{\cal N},{\cal R},\Pi_{{\cal R}\to G})
. Intuitively, the reason is that the new cut
C_{E_{{\rm new}}}
blocks all new edges (each
e\in E_{{\rm new}}
has
\ell_{\widetilde{G}-C^{\prime}}(e)=\ell_{\widetilde{G}}(e)+C^{\prime}(e)\geq h+1
, since the original edge length is at least
1
), so
{\cal N}
is still a valid distributed neighborhood cover in the graph
\widetilde{G}-C^{\prime}
.

Then we apply
Lemma

6.10
on
(\widetilde{G},A)
and
(C^{\prime},L^{\prime},{\cal N}^{\prime},{\cal R}^{\prime},\Pi_{{\cal R}^{\prime}\to\widetilde{G}})
with node-weighting increasing
A_{{\rm new}}=\deg_{E_{{\rm new}}}
. The output is exactly a certified-ED
(\widetilde{C},\widetilde{L},\widetilde{\cal N},\widetilde{\cal R},\Pi_{\widetilde{\cal R}\to\widetilde{G}})
of
\widetilde{A}=A+\deg_{E_{{\rm new}}}
with desired quality parameters. In particular,

\widetilde{C}=C^{\prime}+C^{\prime}_{{\rm new}}
with
|C^{\prime}_{{\rm new}}|\leq O(\kappa_{{\rm init},C}\cdot\phi\cdot h\cdot|A_{{\rm new}}|)
by
Lemma

6.10
, so we can rewrite
\widetilde{C}=C+C_{{\rm new}}
where

C_{{\rm new}}=C_{E_{{\rm new}}}+C^{\prime}_{{\rm new}}
with size
|C_{{\rm new}}|=O((\kappa_{{\rm init},C}\cdot\phi+1)\cdot h\cdot|E_{{\rm new}}|)
.
Similarly,
\widetilde{L}=L^{\prime}\cup L^{\prime}_{{\rm new}}
with
|L^{\prime}_{{\rm new}}|\leq O((\kappa_{\mathrm{CM},L}+\kappa_{{\rm init},L})\cdot\phi\cdot|A_{{\rm new}}|)
by
Lemma

6.10
, so
\widetilde{L}=L\cup L_{{\rm new}}
where

L_{{\rm new}}=L_{E_{{\rm new}}}\cup L^{\prime}_{{\rm new}}
with size
|L_{{\rm new}}|\leq O(((\kappa_{\mathrm{CM},L}+\kappa_{{\rm init},L})\cdot\phi+1)\cdot|A_{{\rm new}}|)
.

By
Lemma

6.10
, the recourse from
{\cal N}
to
\widetilde{\cal N}
is
O(|A_{{\rm new}}|\cdot(\omega+\kappa_{\mathrm{NC},\omega}))=O(|E_{{\rm new}}|\cdot(\omega+\kappa_{\mathrm{NC},\omega}))
. The running time is dominated by the subroutine
Lemma

6.10
, which takes time
|E_{{\rm new}}|\cdot\mathrm{poly}(h)\cdot n^{O(\epsilon)}\cdot(1/\phi+2^{O(f)}\cdot\omega)
.

∎

6.5
Proof of
Theorem

6.4

During the whole process, we will maintain a certified-ED
(C^{(i)},L^{(i)},{\cal N}^{(i)},{\cal R}^{(i)},\Pi_{{\cal R}^{(i)}\to G^{(i)}})
of node-weighting
\bar{A}^{(i)}
on
G^{(i)}
, where

\bar{A}^{(i)}=A^{(i)}+\deg_{G^{(i)}}.

Initialization.

We initialize
(C^{(0)},L^{(0)},{\cal N}^{(0)},{\cal R}^{(0)},\Pi_{{\cal R}^{(0)}\to G^{(0)}})
of
\bar{A}^{(0)}
on
G^{(0)}
by applying
Lemma

6.8
with parameters
\phi^{(0)}=\phi/\kappa_{{\rm init},L}
and
h^{(0)}=h\cdot\lambda_{\mathrm{dynED},h}
. The output satisfies that

•

|C^{(0)}|\leq O(\kappa_{{\rm init},C}\cdot\phi^{(0)}\cdot h^{(0)}\cdot|\bar{A}^{(0)}|)\leq O(|A^{(0)}|\cdot\phi\cdot\lambda_{\mathrm{dynED},h}\cdot h)
because
\kappa_{{\rm init},C}\leq\kappa_{{\rm init},L}
.

•

L^{(0)}
has distortion
\sigma^{(0)}=O(h^{(0)}/\kappa_{\sigma})=O(\lambda_{\mathrm{dynED},h}\cdot h/\kappa_{\sigma})
and

|L^{(0)}|\leq O(\kappa_{{\rm init},L}\cdot\phi^{(0)}\cdot|\bar{A}^{(0)}|)\leq O(|A^{(0)}|\cdot\phi).

•

{\cal N}^{(0)}
is a
b^{(0)}
-distributed
(h^{(0)}_{{\rm cov}},h^{(0)}_{\mathrm{sep}},\omega)
-pairwise cover of
\bar{A}^{(0)}
of
G^{(0)}-C^{(0)}
with

b^{(0)}=1,\ h^{(0)}_{{\rm cov}}=h^{(0)}_{\mathrm{sep}}=h^{(0)}=\lambda_{\mathrm{dynED},h}\cdot h,\text{ and }\omega=\kappa_{\mathrm{NC},\omega}.

•

Routers in
{\cal R}^{(0)}
are just initialized by
Theorem

5.1
, so they are under
f^{(0)}=0
batched update.

•

\Pi_{{\cal R}^{(0)}\to G^{(0)}}
is a
(h^{(0)}_{{\rm emb}},\gamma^{(0)}_{{\rm emb}})
-embedding with

\displaystyle h_{{\rm emb}}

\displaystyle=O(\lambda_{{\rm init},h}\cdot h^{(0)})=O(\lambda_{{\rm init},h}\cdot\lambda_{\mathrm{dynED},h}\cdot h),

\displaystyle\gamma_{{\rm emb}}

\displaystyle=O(\kappa_{{\rm init},\gamma}/\phi^{(0)})=O(\kappa_{{\rm init},\gamma}\cdot\kappa_{{\rm init},L}/\phi).

Batched Edge Insertions/Deletions and Node-Weighting Insertions.

For now we assume
\pi^{(i)}
represents edge insertions/deletions or node-weighting insertions (the case
\pi^{(i)}
represents isolated vertex insertions/deletions will be discussed later). We say
\pi^{(i)}
is
incremental
if it represents node-weighting increasing or edge insertions, and
\pi^{(i)}
is
decremental
if it represents edge deletions.

For each update
\pi^{(i)}
, we will apply
Lemma

6.10
,
Lemma

6.13
, or
Lemma

6.15
, depending on whether
\pi^{(i)}
represents node-weighting insertion, edge deletions or edge insertions respectively, on
(C^{(i-1)},L^{(i-1)},{\cal N}^{(i-1)},{\cal R}^{(i)},\Pi_{{\cal R}^{(i-1)}\to G^{(i-1)}})
with length parameter
h^{(i)}=h^{(i-1)}/3
and congestion parameter

\phi^{(i)}=\left\{\begin{aligned} &1/(\kappa_{\mathrm{CM},L}+\kappa_{{\rm init},L}),\text{ if $\pi^{(i)}$ is incremental}\\
&1/((\kappa_{\mathrm{CM},L}+\kappa_{{\rm init},L})\cdot\lambda_{\mathrm{rt},{\rm prune}}(f^{(i-1)})\cdot\gamma_{{\rm emb}}^{(i-1)}),\text{ if $\pi^{(i)}$ is decremental}\end{aligned}\right.

Directly, we have

•

C^{(i)}=C^{(i-1)}+C_{{\rm new}}^{(i)}
with

\displaystyle|C_{{\rm new}}^{(i)}|

\displaystyle\leq\left\{\begin{aligned} &O((\kappa_{{\rm init},C}\cdot\phi^{(i)}+1)\cdot h^{(i-1)}\cdot|\pi^{(i)}|),\text{ if $\pi^{(i)}$ is incremental}\\
&O(\kappa_{{\rm init},C}\cdot\phi^{(i)}\cdot h^{(i-1)}\cdot\lambda_{\mathrm{rt},{\rm prune}}(f^{(i-1)})\cdot\gamma^{(i-1)}_{{\rm emb}}\cdot|\pi^{(i)}|),\text{ if $\pi^{(i)}$ is decremental}\end{aligned}\right.

\displaystyle=O(h^{(i-1)}\cdot|\pi^{(i)}|)

\displaystyle\leq O(\lambda_{\mathrm{dynED},h}\cdot h\cdot|\pi^{(i)}|).

•

L^{(i)}=L^{(i-1)}\cup L_{{\rm new}}^{(i)}
with distortion
\sigma^{(i)}=\sigma^{(i-1)}+O(h^{(i-1)}/\kappa_{\sigma})
and

\displaystyle|L^{(i)}_{{\rm new}}|

\displaystyle\leq\left\{\begin{aligned} &O(((\kappa_{\mathrm{CM},L}+\kappa_{{\rm init},L})\cdot\phi^{(i)}+1)\cdot|\pi^{(i)}|),\text{ if $\pi^{(i)}$ is incremental}\\
&O((\kappa_{\mathrm{CM},L}+\kappa_{{\rm init},L})\cdot\phi^{(i)}\cdot\lambda_{\mathrm{rt},{\rm prune}}(f^{(i-1)})\cdot\gamma^{(i-1)}_{{\rm emb}}\cdot|\pi^{(i)}|),\text{ if $\pi^{(i)}$ is decremental}\end{aligned}\right.

\displaystyle=O(|\pi^{(i)}|).

•

{\cal N}
is a
b^{(i)}
-distributed
(h^{(i)}_{{\rm cov}},h^{(i)}_{\mathrm{sep}},\omega^{(i)})
-pairwise cover with

b^{(i)}=b^{(i-1)}+1,\ h^{(i)}_{{\rm cov}}=h^{(i)}_{\mathrm{sep}}=h^{(i)}=h^{(i-1)}/3,\ \omega^{(i)}=\omega^{(i-1)}+\kappa_{\mathrm{NC},\omega}.

•

Routers in
{\cal R}^{(i)}
are maintained under
f^{(i)}=f^{(i-1)}+O(1)
updates.

•

\Pi_{{\cal R}^{(i)}\to G^{(i)}}
has

\displaystyle h^{(i)}_{{\rm emb}}

\displaystyle=\max\{h^{(i-1)}_{{\rm emb}},\lambda_{{\rm init},h}\cdot h^{(i-1)}\}\text{ and }

\displaystyle\gamma^{(i)}_{{\rm emb}}

\displaystyle=\gamma^{(i-1)}_{{\rm emb}}+O((\omega^{(i-1)}\cdot\kappa_{\mathrm{CM},\gamma}+\kappa_{{\rm init},\gamma})/\phi^{(i)}).

The Quality of Certified-EDs.

Now we show that the certified-ED
(C^{(i)},L^{(i)},{\cal N}^{(i)},{\cal R}^{(i)},\Pi_{{\cal R}^{(i)}\to G^{(i)}})
meet the requirements. Some of them are easy to see (or they have already shown above), so we will omit some proofs.

•

The bounds of
|C_{{\rm new}}^{(i)}|
and
|L_{{\rm new}}^{(i)}|
have already shown above, and obviously we have
\omega^{(i)}\leq t\cdot\kappa_{\mathrm{NC},\omega}
,
f^{(i)}\leq O(t)
and
b^{(i)}\leq O(t)
.

•

The bounds of
h^{(i)}_{{\rm cov}},h^{(i)}_{\mathrm{sep}},h^{(i)}_{{\rm emb}}
follows the fact that, for
each
i
,
h^{(i)}\leq h^{(0)}=\lambda_{\mathrm{dynED},h}\cdot h
and
h^{(i)}\geq h^{(t)}=h^{(0)}/3^{t}\geq h
.

•

About the congestion of
\Pi_{{\cal R}^{(i)}\to G^{(i)}}
, we have

\displaystyle\gamma^{(i)}_{{\rm emb}}

\displaystyle=\gamma^{(i-1)}_{{\rm emb}}+O((\omega^{(i-1)}\cdot\kappa_{\mathrm{CM},\gamma}+\kappa_{{\rm init},\gamma})/\phi^{(i)})

\displaystyle\leq\gamma^{(i-1)}_{{\rm emb}}+O((\omega^{(i-1)}\cdot\kappa_{\mathrm{CM},\gamma}+\kappa_{{\rm init},\gamma})\cdot(\kappa_{\mathrm{CM},L}+\kappa_{{\rm init},L})\cdot\lambda_{\mathrm{rt},{\rm prune}}(f^{(i-1)})\cdot\gamma_{{\rm emb}}^{(i-1)})

\displaystyle\leq O((t\cdot\kappa_{\mathrm{NC},\omega}\cdot\kappa_{\mathrm{CM},\gamma}+\kappa_{{\rm init},\gamma})\cdot(\kappa_{\mathrm{CM},L}+\kappa_{{\rm init},L})\cdot\lambda_{\mathrm{rt},{\rm prune}}(t))\cdot\gamma^{(i-1)}_{{\rm emb}}.

Combining
\gamma^{(0)}_{{\rm emb}}=O(\kappa_{{\rm init},\gamma}\cdot\kappa_{{\rm init},L}/\phi)
, we get

\gamma^{(i)}_{{\rm emb}}=(\lambda_{\mathrm{rt},{\rm prune}}(t)\cdot(t\cdot\kappa_{\mathrm{NC},\omega}\cdot\kappa_{\mathrm{CM},\gamma}+\kappa_{{\rm init},\gamma})\cdot(\kappa_{\mathrm{CM},L}+\kappa_{{\rm init},L}))^{O(t)}/\phi.

Isolated Vertex Insertions/Deletions

It is easy to handle
\pi^{(i)}
if it represents isolated vertex insertions/deletions.

Suppose that
\pi^{(i)}
represents isolated vertex insertions
V_{{\rm new}}
with
A_{{\rm new}}
as their node-weighting (i.e. after this update,
A^{(i)}(v)=A_{{\rm new}}(v)
for each
v\in V_{{\rm new}}
). For each
v\in V_{{\rm new}}
, we create a cluster
S=A_{{\rm new}}(v)
collecting all virtual nodes of
v
, and add
S
into an arbitrary clustering
{\cal S}\in{\cal N}^{(i-1)}
. The router
R
corresponding to
S
can be initialized by
Theorem

5.1
.
R
can be embedded into
G
trivially (i.e. the embedding path of each router edge degenerates to the singleton vertex
v
), because each virtual node in
V(R)
are owned by the same vertex
v\in V(G)
. There is no change to
C
and
L
. Obviously the certified-ED after update is of
\bar{A}^{(i)}
on
G^{(i)}
with quality unchanged (note that for each
v\in V_{{\rm new}}
,
\bar{A}^{(i)}(v)=A^{(i)}(v)+\deg_{G^{(i)}}(v)=A_{{\rm new}}(v)
).

Suppose that
\pi^{(i)}
represents isolated vertex deletions
V_{\mathrm{del}}\subseteq V(G^{(i-1)})
. For each vertex
v\in V^{\mathrm{del}}
, we remove all clusters
S\in{\cal N}^{(i-1)}
s.t.
S\subseteq\bar{A}^{(i-1)}(v)
. In fact, we have
\bigcup_{S\subseteq\bar{A}^{(i-1)}(v)}=\bar{A}^{(i-1)}(v)
(since
v
is isolated), so this will remove all virtual nodes
\bar{A}^{(i-1)}(v)
from
{\cal N}^{(i-1)}
. Conceptually, for each cluster
S
that is removed, we will further remove its corresponding router
R
from
{\cal R}^{(i-1)}
and its embedding, but in the actual implementation, it is safe to leave them unchanged, because our algorithm will not access
R
and its embedding after the isolated vertex
v
and cluster
S
are removed. Note that because
R
is connected and some virtual nodes in
V(R)
are owned by the isolated vertex
v
, the embedding of
R
into
G^{(i-1)}
is trivial (i.e. the embedding path of each router edge degenerates to the singleton vertex
v
). Finally, we also need to remove
V_{\mathrm{del}}
from the landmark set
L
.

The Recourse from
{\cal N}^{(i-1)}
to
{\cal N}^{(i)}
.

When
\pi^{(i)}
represents isolated vertex insertions, the recourse is trivially at most
\sum_{v\in V_{{\rm new}}}A_{{\rm new}}(v)=|\pi^{(i)}|
. For isolated vertex deletions, the recourse is at most
\sum_{v\in V_{\mathrm{del}}}A^{(i-1)}(v)=|\pi^{(i)}|
.
Combining
Lemmas

6.10
,
6.13
and
6.15
, the recourse is

O(\lambda_{\mathrm{rt},{\rm prune}}(f^{(i-1)})\cdot\gamma^{(i-1)}_{{\rm emb}}\cdot(\omega^{(i-1)}+\kappa_{\mathrm{NC},\omega})\cdot|\pi^{(i)}|)\leq\lambda_{\mathrm{rt},{\rm prune}}(t)\cdot t\cdot\kappa_{\mathrm{NC},\omega}\cdot\kappa_{\mathrm{dynED},\gamma}\cdot|\pi^{(i)}|/\phi.

because the bottleneck is when
\pi^{(i)}
represents batched edge deletions.

The Running Time.

By
Lemma

6.8
, the initialization time is
(|G^{(0)}|+|\bar{A}^{(0)}|)\cdot\mathrm{poly}(h^{(0)})\cdot n^{O(\epsilon)}=(|G^{(0)}|+|A^{(0)}|)\cdot\mathrm{poly}(h)\cdot n^{O(\epsilon)}
because
h^{(0)}=h\cdot\lambda_{\mathrm{dynED},h}
. By
Lemmas

6.10
,
6.13
and
6.15
and the update algorithm for batched isolated vertex insertions/deletions, the bottleneck is batched edge deletions and the update time for batch
\pi^{(i)}
is

\displaystyle\lambda_{\mathrm{rt},{\rm prune}}(f^{(i-1)})\cdot\gamma^{(i-1)}_{{\rm emb}}\cdot|\pi^{(i)}|\cdot\mathrm{poly}(h^{(i)})\cdot n^{O(\epsilon)}\cdot(1/\phi^{(i)}+2^{O(f^{(i-1)})}\cdot\omega^{(i-1)})=|\pi^{(i)}|\cdot\mathrm{poly}(h)\cdot n^{O(\epsilon)}/\phi.

7
Dynamic Length-Constrained Expander Decomposition with Density

In this section, we will generalize the notion of certified-EDs in
Definition

6.1
to
\rho
-dense certified-EDs defined in
Definition

7.3
, and extend the online-batch dynamic certified-ED algorithm to
\rho
-dense certified-EDs. Roughly speaking, the
density

\rho
(defined in
Definition

7.1
) is essentially weight functions on node-weightings (in other words, the “weights of weights” of vertices). The motivation of defining this second level of weights is to reduce the recourse on the pairwise cover
{\cal N}
in the dynamic certified-ED algorithm in
Section

6
.

To be specific, the recourse on
{\cal N}
is roughly
1/\phi
by
Theorem

6.4
, and the bottleneck is the subroutine
Lemma

6.13
for handling batched edge deletions. However, to obtain the low-recourse dynamic vertex sparsifier in
Section

8
, we will require dynamic certified-ED algorithms with recourse on
{\cal N}
independent of
1/\phi
. To achieve this, the high level idea is to put
1/\phi

items
(see
Definition

7.1
) on each virtual node and define the routers on items instead of virtual nodes. Then, when handling batched edge deletions, roughly speaking, each unit of vertex pruning on routers now cause only amortized
\phi
(instead of
1
) unit of virtual node pruning, which reduces the recourse of
{\cal N}
by a factor
1/\phi
if we keep the congestion of the embedding unchanged. On the other hand, routing more things with the same congestion requires more routability of the certified-ED, so the size of the cut and landmark set will now depend on the total density instead of the number of virtual nodes.

7.1
\rho
-Dense Node-Weighting.

Given a graph
G
and a node-weighting
A
on
G
, the
density
of
A
is a function
\rho:A\to\mathbb{N}_{>0}
. The
density
of each virtual node
v\in A
is
\rho(v)
and let
\rho(A)=\sum_{v\in A}\rho(v)
. Equivalently, we say a virtual node
v
owns
\rho(v)
many
items
. Without ambiguity, we also use
\rho(v)
to denote the set of
items
owned by
v
, and use
\rho(A):=\bigcup_{v\in A}\rho(v)
to denote the whole set of items. A node-weighting
A
is unit-dense if each virtual node
v\in A
has
\rho(v)=1
.

7.2
\rho
-Dense Pairwise Cover.

Let
G
be a graph with
\rho
-dense node-weighting
A
. A pairwise cover
{\cal N}
of
A
on
G
is further
\rho
-dense, if each cluster
S\in{\cal N}
is assigned a density function (called the
S
-density
)
\rho_{S}:S\to\mathbb{N}_{>0}
s.t.
\rho_{S}\geq\rho_{\mid S}
.

Definition

7.3
generalizes the notion of certified-EDs in
Definition

6.1
, but with the following main differences.
(1) We only need
{\cal N}
to be a pairwise cover, which is weaker than the notion of
b
-distributed neighborhood cover in
Definition

6.1
, but
{\cal N}
should be
\rho
-dense.
(2) Each router
R
of
S\in{\cal N}
has vertices corresponding to items in
\rho_{S}(S)
instead of virtual nodes in
S
.

7.3
\rho
-Dense Certified-ED.

Let
G
be a graph with
\rho
-dense node-weighting
A
. A
\rho
-dense certified-ED
(C,L,{\cal N},{\cal R},\Pi_{{\cal R}\to G})
of
A
on
G
satisfies that

•

C
is an integral moving cut on
G
,

•

L
is a landmark set of
C
on
G
with distortion
\sigma
,

•

{\cal N}
denotes a
\rho
-dense
(h_{{\rm cov}},h_{\mathrm{sep}},\omega)
-pairwise cover of
A
on
G-C
with
h_{{\rm cov}}=h_{\mathrm{sep}}=h

•

{\cal R}=\{R^{S}\mid S\in{\cal N}\}
is a collection of routers s.t. for each cluster
S\in{\cal N}
,
R^{S}
is a router maintained by
Theorem

5.1
under at most
f
update batches s.t.
V(R^{S})\supseteq\rho_{S}(S)
. Precisely, each vertex
v\in V(R^{S})\cap\rho_{S}(S)
is corresponding to the item in
\rho_{S}(S)
, and each vertex
v\in V(R^{S})\setminus\rho_{S}(S)
is a
redundant
vertex corresponding to nothing.

This is equivalent to the following

density property
: for each virtual node
v\in S
, its
S
-density
\rho_{S}(v)
is the number of vertices in
R^{S}
corresponding to items of
v
.

•

\Pi_{{\cal R}\to G}
denotes an embedding of all routers into
G
with length
h_{{\rm emb}}
and congestion
\gamma_{{\rm emb}}
. In this embedding, each router vertex
v_{R}
is mapped to the vertex
v_{G}\in V(G)
which owns the corresponding item of
v_{R}
(if
v_{R}
is a redundant router vertex, then it can be mapped to an arbitrary vertex
v_{G}\in V(G)
).

The organization of this section is similar to
Section

6
. In
Section

7.1
, we show the initialization algorithm for
\rho
-dense certified-EDs. In
Sections

7.2
,
7.3
and
7.4
, we discuss how to maintain a
\rho
-dense certified-ED under batched update of node-weighting insertion, edge deletion and edge insertion respectively.

Although we can obtain an online-batch dynamic
\rho
-dense certified-ED algorithm (analogous to
Theorem

6.4
for certified-ED) combining subroutines in
Sections

7.1
,
7.2
,
7.3
and
7.4
, we did not formalize it here, because we need a specialized version of dynamic
\rho
-dense certified-EDs algorithm for our application in
Section

8
. Roughly speaking, in
Section

8
, we need to maintain a collection of dynamic
\rho
-dense certified-EDs of a same node-weighting
A
with different length scales simultaneously, and further enforce that the node-weighting
A
should include all landmark sets
L
of this collection. Therefore, in
Section

7.5
, we design an additional subroutine which can insert the newly generated landmarks to the node-weighting. This specialized version of dynamic
\rho
-dense certified-EDs algorithm is integrated in the proof of
Theorem

8.2
.

7.1
Initialization

7.4
.

Given a graph
G
with a
\rho
-dense positive node-weighting
A
and parameters
h
and
\phi
, there is an algorithm that computes a
\rho
-dense certified-ED
(C,L,{\cal N},{\cal R},\Pi_{{\cal R}\to G})
of
A
on
G
satisfying the following.

•

|C|\leq O(\kappa_{{\rm init},C}\cdot\phi\cdot h\cdot\rho(A))
.

•

L
has distortion
\sigma=h/\kappa_{\sigma}
and size
|L|=O(\kappa_{{\rm init},L}\cdot\phi\cdot\rho(A))
.

•

{\cal N}
is a
\rho
-dense
(h_{{\rm cov}},h_{\mathrm{sep}},\omega)
-pairwise cover of
A
on
G-C
with

b=1,\ h_{{\rm cov}}=h,\ h_{\mathrm{sep}}=h\text{ and }\omega=\kappa_{\mathrm{NC},\omega}.

•

Routers
{\cal R}
are initialized by
Theorem

5.1
undergoing
f=0
batched update.

•

Embedding
\Pi_{{\cal R}\to G}
has length
h_{{\rm emb}}=O(\lambda_{{\rm init},h}\cdot h)
and congestion
\gamma_{{\rm emb}}=O(\kappa_{{\rm init},\gamma}/\phi)
.

The running time is
(|G|+\rho(A))\cdot\mathrm{poly}(h)\cdot n^{O(\epsilon)}
.

Proof.

In fact, this is a simple corollary of
Lemma

6.8
. We define a node-weighting
A^{\prime}
s.t. for each vertex
v\in V(G)
,
A^{\prime}(v)=\rho(A(v))
be the number of items of virtual nodes of
v
. Note that items in
\rho(A)
one-one corresponding to virtual nodes in
A^{\prime}
. Then we invoke
Lemma

6.8
on graph
G
and node-weighting
A^{\prime}
with the same parameters. The output certified-ED
(C,L,{\cal N}^{\prime},{\cal R},\Pi_{{\cal R}\to G})
is almost what we desire.

We just need to modify
{\cal N}^{\prime}
to
{\cal N}
to make it fit with our definition of dense certified-ED. Precisely,
{\cal N}^{\prime}
can be viewed as a pairwise cover of items in
\rho(A)
. For each cluster
S^{\prime}\in{\cal N}^{\prime}
, we define a new cluster
S=\{v\in A\mid\text{some item of $v$ is included by $S^{\prime}$}\}
, and set the
S
-density function be
\rho_{S}=\rho_{\mid S}
. The density property of
(C,L,{\cal N},{\cal R},\Pi_{{\cal R}\to G})
holds because
\{v\in A\mid\text{some item of $v$ is included by $S^{\prime}$}\}=\{v\in A\mid\text{all items of $v$ are included by $S^{\prime}$}\}
for each
S^{\prime}\in{\cal N}^{\prime}
(this is from the guarantee of
Lemma

6.8
: if a cluster
S^{\prime}
includes a virtual node in
A^{\prime}
of some vertex
v\in V(G)
, then
S^{\prime}
includes all virtual nodes in
A^{\prime}
of
v
).
∎

7.2
Batched Node-Weighting Insertion

7.5
.

Let
G
be a graph with a
\rho
-dense node-weighting
A
and a

\rho
-dense certified-ED
(C,L,{\cal N},{\cal R},\Pi_{{\cal R}\to G})
of
A
on
G
in
Definition

7.3
. Given a
\rho_{{\rm new}}
-dense node-weighting
A_{{\rm new}}
s.t.
\widetilde{A}=A+A_{{\rm new}}\geq\deg_{G}+\mathds{1}(V(G))
, and parameter
\phi
, there is an algorithm that computes a
\widetilde{\rho}
-dense certified-ED
(\widetilde{C},\widetilde{L},\widetilde{\cal N},\widetilde{\cal R},\Pi_{\widetilde{\cal R}\to G})
of
\widetilde{A}
with density
\widetilde{\rho}=\rho\uplus\rho_{{\rm new}}
17
17

17

Recall the definition of operation
\uplus
from
Section

3.4
.
on
G
s.t.

•

\widetilde{C}=C+C_{{\rm new}}
with

|C_{{\rm new}}|\leq O(\kappa_{{\rm init},C}\cdot\omega\cdot\phi\cdot h\cdot\rho_{{\rm new}}(A_{{\rm new}}))

•

\widetilde{L}=L\cup L_{{\rm new}}
with distortion
\widetilde{\sigma}=\sigma+O(h/\kappa_{\sigma})
and

|L_{{\rm new}}|\leq O((\kappa_{\mathrm{CM},L}+\kappa_{{\rm init},L})\cdot\omega\cdot\phi\cdot\rho_{{\rm new}}(A_{{\rm new}})),

•

\widetilde{\cal N}
is a
\widetilde{\rho}
-dense
(\widetilde{h}_{{\rm cov}},\widetilde{h}_{\mathrm{sep}},\widetilde{\omega})
-pairwise cover with

\widetilde{h}_{{\rm cov}}=h/3,\ \widetilde{h}_{\mathrm{sep}}=h/3\text{ and }\widetilde{\omega}=\omega+\kappa_{\mathrm{NC},\omega}

•

Routers in
\widetilde{\cal R}
are maintained by
Theorem

5.1
under
\widetilde{f}=f+1
updates.

•

\Pi_{\widetilde{\cal R}\to G}
is a
(\widetilde{h}_{{\rm emb}},\widetilde{\gamma}_{{\rm emb}})
-embedding with

\displaystyle\widetilde{h}_{{\rm emb}}

\displaystyle=\max\{h_{{\rm emb}},O(\lambda_{{\rm init},h}\cdot h)\}

\displaystyle\widetilde{\gamma}_{{\rm emb}}

\displaystyle=\gamma_{{\rm emb}}+O((\omega\cdot\kappa_{\mathrm{CM},\gamma}+\kappa_{{\rm init},\gamma})/\phi)

Given a node-weighting
\widetilde{A}^{\star}\subseteq\widetilde{A}
, the recourse from
{\cal N}
to
\widetilde{\cal N}
restricted on
\widetilde{A}^{\star}
is

\mathrm{Rcs}_{\mid\widetilde{A}^{\star}}({\cal N}\to\widetilde{\cal N})=O(\rho_{{\rm new}}(A_{{\rm new}})\cdot\omega\cdot\kappa_{\mathrm{NC},\omega}/\min_{v\in\widetilde{A}^{\star}}\widetilde{\rho}(v)).

The running time is
\omega\cdot\rho_{{\rm new}}(A_{{\rm new}})\cdot\mathrm{poly}(h)\cdot n^{O(\epsilon)}\cdot(1/\phi+2^{O(f)}\cdot\omega)
.

Proof.

We first set up notations in this proof to avoid confusion. We will use subscripts
v_{\mathrm{vtx}}
to refer to a (real) vertex,
v
without subscript to refer to a virtual node, and
v_{\mathrm{item}}
to refer to an item. Also, for a set
\rho^{\prime}
of items, we use
\mathrm{supp}_{\mathrm{vtul}}(\rho^{\prime})
to denote the set of virtual nodes owning some items in
\rho^{\prime}
, and naturally,
\mathrm{supp}_{\mathrm{vtx}}(\rho^{\prime})=\mathrm{supp}(\mathrm{supp}_{\mathrm{vtul}}(\rho^{\prime}))
denote the set of vertices owning some items in
\rho^{\prime}
.

The first step is to apply the local cutmatch subroutine, i.e.
Theorem

4.4
. We define source density function
\rho^{\mathrm{src}}=\omega\cdot\rho_{{\rm new}}(A_{{\rm new}})
and sink density-function
\rho^{\mathrm{sink}}=\rho(A)
. To elaborate, for each virtual node
v\in A_{{\rm new}}
,
\rho^{\mathrm{src}}(v)=\omega\cdot\rho_{{\rm new}}(v)
is the total number of items owned by
v
scaled up by
\omega
, but for each virtual node

v\in A
,
\rho^{\mathrm{sink}}(v)=\rho(v)
is not scaled up. Furthermore, we can extend the domain of density functions
\rho^{\mathrm{src}}
and
\rho^{\mathrm{sink}}
to
vertices
(originally, the domain of a density function is a virtual node set) in a natural way, so they are valid inputs to a general form (see
Remark

4.5
) of
Theorem

4.4
. That is, for each vertex
v_{\mathrm{vtx}}\in V(G)
,
\rho^{\mathrm{src}}(v_{\mathrm{vtx}})=\sum_{\text{virtual node $v\in A_{{\rm new}}(v_{\mathrm{vtx}})$}}\rho^{\mathrm{src}}(v)
is the total number of items of all
A_{{\rm new}}
-virtual nodes at
v_{\mathrm{vtx}}
, scaled up by
\omega
at the end. Similarly,
\rho^{\mathrm{src}}(v_{\mathrm{vtx}})=\sum_{v\in A(v_{\mathrm{vtx}})}\rho(v)
is the total number of items of all
A
-virtual nodes at
v_{\mathrm{vtx}}
.

We apply
Theorem

4.4
on
G-C
with source density function
\rho^{\mathrm{src}}
, sink density function
\rho^{\mathrm{sink}}
, parameters
h_{\mathrm{CM}}=h/3
and
\phi_{\mathrm{CM}}=\phi
. The output is

•

a partition
\rho^{\mathrm{src}}_{M},\rho^{\mathrm{src}}_{U}
of
\rho^{\mathrm{src}}
,

•

a partition
\rho^{\mathrm{sink}}_{M},\rho^{\mathrm{sink}}_{U}
of
\rho^{\mathrm{sink}}
with
|\rho^{\mathrm{sink}}_{M}|=O(|\rho^{\mathrm{src}}_{M}|)
,

•

a matching
M_{\mathrm{CM}}
between
\rho^{\mathrm{src}}_{M}
and
\rho^{\mathrm{sink}}_{M}
with size
|M_{\mathrm{CM}}|=|\rho^{\mathrm{src}}_{M}|
, and an embedding
\Pi_{M_{\mathrm{CM}}\to G-C}
with length
h_{\mathrm{CM}}
and congestion
\gamma_{\mathrm{CM}}=O(\kappa_{\mathrm{CM},\gamma}/\phi_{\mathrm{CM}})

•

a moving cut
C_{\mathrm{CM}}
with size
|C_{\mathrm{CM}}|\leq\phi_{\mathrm{CM}}\cdot h_{\mathrm{CM}}\cdot|\rho^{\mathrm{src}}|
s.t.
\rho^{\mathrm{src}}_{U}
is
h_{\mathrm{CM}}
-separated from
\rho^{\mathrm{sink}}_{M}
in
G-C-C_{\mathrm{CM}}
.

•

a landmark set
L_{\mathrm{CM}}
of
C_{\mathrm{CM}}
on
G-C
with distortion
\sigma_{\mathrm{CM}}=O(h/\kappa_{\sigma})
and size
|L_{\mathrm{CM}}|=O(\kappa_{\mathrm{CM},L}\cdot\phi_{\mathrm{CM}}\cdot|\rho^{\mathrm{src}}|)
.

We define node-weightings
A_{1}
and
A_{2}
as follows. Let
\widetilde{A}=A+A_{{\rm new}}
be the total node-weighting with density
\widetilde{\rho}=\rho\uplus\rho_{{\rm new}}
. Then

A_{1}=A+\{v\in A_{{\rm new}}\mid\rho^{\mathrm{src}}(v)\subseteq\rho^{\mathrm{src}}_{M}\}\text{ with density }\rho_{1}=\widetilde{\rho}_{\mid A_{1}},

and

A_{2}=\widetilde{A}\setminus\widetilde{A}(\mathrm{supp}_{\mathrm{vtx}}(\rho^{\mathrm{sink}}_{U}))\text{ with density }\rho_{2}=\widetilde{\rho}_{\mid A_{2}}.

That is,
A_{2}
excludes virtual nodes of vertices in
\mathrm{supp}_{\mathrm{vtx}}(\rho^{\mathrm{sink}}_{U})
.

7.6
.

\rho_{2}(A_{2})\leq O(\omega\cdot\rho_{{\rm new}}(A_{{\rm new}}))
.

Proof.

By the definition of
A_{2}
, each virtual node
v\in A_{2}
has
\rho^{\mathrm{sink}}_{U}(v)=0
. If
v\in A_{{\rm new}}
is a new virtual node,
\rho_{2}(v)=\widetilde{\rho}(v)=\rho_{{\rm new}}(v)\leq\rho^{\mathrm{src}}(v)
. If
v\in A
is an old virtual node,
\rho_{2}(v)=\widetilde{\rho}(v)=\rho(v)=\rho^{\mathrm{sink}}(v)=\rho^{\mathrm{sink}}_{M}(v)+\rho^{\mathrm{sink}}_{U}(v)=\rho^{\mathrm{sink}}_{M}(v)
. Therefore, we have
\rho_{2}(A_{2})\leq|\rho^{\mathrm{src}}|+|\rho^{\mathrm{sink}}_{M}|
. Combining
|\rho^{\mathrm{sink}}_{M}|=O(|\rho^{\mathrm{src}}_{M}|)\leq O(|\rho^{\mathrm{src}}|)
, we can conclude that
\rho_{2}(A_{2})\leq O(|\rho^{\mathrm{src}}|)=O(\omega\cdot\rho_{{\rm new}}(A_{{\rm new}}))
.
∎

Obtain a Certified-ED of
A_{1}
by Update.

We will update
({\cal N},{\cal R},\Pi_{{\cal R}\to G})
to
({\cal N}_{1},{\cal R}_{1},\Pi_{{\cal R}_{1}\to G})
in what follows. For better understanding, with the
({\cal N}_{1},{\cal R}_{1},\Pi_{{\cal R}_{1}\to G})
constructed below,
(C+C_{\mathrm{CM}},L\cup L_{\mathrm{CM}},{\cal N}_{1},{\cal R}_{1},\Pi_{{\cal R}_{1}\to G})
is actually a
\rho_{1}
-dense certified-ED of
A_{1}
on
G
with slightly worse quality parameters (but we will not prove this explicitly).

First, for each cluster
S\in{\cal N}
and its router
R\in{\cal R}
, we will update them to be
S_{1}
and
R_{1}
. Concretely, for each virtual node
v\in S
, we first establish an injection from
v
’s items in
\rho^{\mathrm{sink}}
to
v
’s items in
R
, so that we can formally talk about adding matching edges in
M_{\mathrm{CM}}
to
R
later. Precisely,
v
has
\rho^{\mathrm{sink}}(v)
many items in
\rho^{\mathrm{sink}}
, while it has
\rho_{S}(v)\geq\rho(v)=\rho^{\mathrm{sink}}(v)
many items in
R
, so we just select arbitrary
\rho^{\mathrm{sink}}(v)
many
v
’s items in
R
and one-one correspond them with items in
\rho^{\mathrm{sink}}(v)
.

Next, we let

M^{\prime}_{S}=\{(u_{\mathrm{item}},v_{\mathrm{item}})\in M_{\mathrm{CM}}\mid\text{sink item $v_{\mathrm{item}}$ belongs to some virtual node in $S$}\}

be the sub-matching restricted on
S
. However, simply adding the whole
M^{\prime}_{S}
into
R
may violate the density property from
Definition

7.2
(it states that
\rho_{S}(u)\geq\widetilde{\rho}(u)
for all virtual nodes
u\in S
). We will explain soon why the violation might happen. But to fix this issue, we take

M_{S}=\{(u_{\mathrm{item}},v_{\mathrm{item}})\in M^{\prime}_{S}\mid\text{the virtual node $u$ owning $u_{\mathrm{item}}$ has at least $\rho_{{\rm new}}(u)$ source items in $M^{\prime}_{S}$}\}

We update
S
to
S_{1}=S\cup\mathrm{supp}_{\mathrm{vtul}}(M_{S}^{\mathrm{src}})
18
18

18

Recall that
M^{\mathrm{src}}_{S}
denote the set of source items of
M^{S}
, and for each virtual node
u\in\mathrm{supp}_{\mathrm{vtul}}(M_{S}^{\mathrm{src}})
,
M^{\mathrm{src}}_{S}(u)
denote the number of
u
’s items in
M^{\mathrm{src}}_{S}
.
and update
R
to
R_{1}=R\cup M_{S}
via
Theorem

5.1
. For each new virtual node
u\in\mathrm{supp}_{\mathrm{vtul}}(M_{S}^{\mathrm{src}})
, set its
S
-density
\rho_{S}(u)=M^{\mathrm{src}}_{S}(u)
. The new router
R_{1}
has embedding
\Pi_{R_{1}\to G}=\Pi_{R\to G}\cup\Pi_{M_{S}\to G}
, where
M_{S\to G}
can be easily obtained from
\Pi_{M_{\mathrm{CM}}\to G-C}
.

With this update to
S
, we can verify that, for each virtual node
u\in S
,
\rho_{S}(u)\geq\widetilde{\rho}(u)
. If
u\in A
is an old virtual node, then this condition clearly holds, i.e.,
\rho_{S}(u)\geq\rho(u)
even before update and
\rho_{S}(u)
does not change.
Otherwise, we have
u\in A_{\rm new}
. Then the condition
\rho_{S}(u)\geq\rho_{\rm new}(u)=\widetilde{\rho}_{u}
holds by the construction of
M_{S}
. Note that if we instead added
M^{\prime}_{S}
to
R
, this condition may not hold because the number of source items owned by
u
that is matched by
M^{\prime}_{S}
may be less than
\rho_{{\rm new}}(u)
.

Compute a Certified-ED of
A_{2}
from Scratch.

Next, we initialize a
\rho_{2}
-dense certified-ED of
A_{2}
on
(G-C-C_{\mathrm{CM}})[A_{2}]
using
Lemma

7.4
. The output is
(C_{\mathrm{ED}},L_{\mathrm{ED}},{\cal N}_{2},{\cal R}_{2},\Pi_{{\cal R}_{2}\to(G-C-C_{\mathrm{CM}})[A_{2}]})
satisfying that

•

|C_{\mathrm{ED}}|\leq O(\kappa_{{\rm init},C}\cdot\phi_{\mathrm{ED}}\cdot h_{\mathrm{ED}}\cdot\rho_{2}(A_{2}))
.

•

L_{\mathrm{ED}}
is a landmark set of
C_{\mathrm{ED}}
on
(G-C-C_{\mathrm{CM}})[A_{2}]
with distortion
\sigma_{\mathrm{ED}}=h_{\mathrm{ED}}/\kappa_{\sigma}
and size
|L_{\mathrm{ED}}|=O(\kappa_{{\rm init},L}\cdot\phi_{\mathrm{ED}}\cdot\rho_{2}(A_{2}))
.

•

{\cal N}_{2}
is a
\rho_{2}
-dense
(h_{{\rm cov},2},h_{\mathrm{sep},2},\omega_{2})
-pairwise cover of
A_{2}
on
G-C-C_{\mathrm{CM}}-C_{\mathrm{ED}}
with
h_{{\rm cov},2}=h_{\mathrm{ED}},h_{\mathrm{sep},2}=h_{\mathrm{ED}}
and
\omega_{2}=\kappa_{\mathrm{NC},\omega}
.

•

Routers
{\cal R}_{2}
are initialized by
Theorem

5.1
.

•

The embedding
\Pi_{{\cal R}_{2}\to(G-C-C_{\mathrm{CM}})[A_{2}]}
has congestion
\gamma_{{\rm emb},2}=O(\kappa_{{\rm init},\gamma}/\phi_{\mathrm{ED}})
and length
h_{{\rm emb},2}=O(\lambda_{{\rm init},h}\cdot h_{\mathrm{ED}})
.

The New Certified-ED and Its Quality.

The new certified-ED
(\widetilde{C},\widetilde{L},\widetilde{\cal N},\widetilde{\cal R},\Pi_{\widetilde{\cal R}\to G})
is given by

\displaystyle\widetilde{C}=C+C_{{\rm new}}\text{
where }C_{{\rm new}}=C_{\mathrm{CM}}+C_{\mathrm{ED}},

\displaystyle\widetilde{L}=L\cup L_{{\rm new}}\text{
where }L_{{\rm new}}=L_{\mathrm{CM}}\cup L_{\mathrm{ED}},

\displaystyle(\widetilde{\cal N},\widetilde{\cal R},\Pi_{\widetilde{\cal R}\to G})=({\cal N}_{1},{\cal R}_{1},\Pi_{{\cal R}_{1}\to G})\cup({\cal N}_{2},{\cal R}_{2},\Pi_{{\cal R}_{2}\to G-C-C_{\mathrm{CM}}}).

Towards its quality, the size of the new cut
C_{{\rm new}}
is given by

\displaystyle|C_{{\rm new}}|\leq O(\phi_{\mathrm{CM}}\cdot h_{\mathrm{CM}}\cdot|\rho^{\mathrm{src}}|)+O(\kappa_{{\rm init},C}\cdot\phi_{\mathrm{ED}}\cdot h_{\mathrm{ED}}\cdot\rho_{2}(A_{2}))\leq O(\omega\cdot\kappa_{{\rm init},C}\cdot\phi\cdot h\cdot\rho_{{\rm new}}(A_{{\rm new}})).

because
\rho_{2}(A_{2})\leq O(\omega\cdot\rho_{{\rm new}}(A_{{\rm new}}))
.
The distortion of
\widetilde{L}
is
\widetilde{\sigma}=\sigma+\sigma_{\mathrm{CM}}+\sigma_{\mathrm{ED}}=\sigma+O(h/\kappa_{\sigma})
by
Lemma

3.21
, and the new landmark set
L_{{\rm new}}
has size

\displaystyle|L_{{\rm new}}|\leq O(\kappa_{\mathrm{CM},L}\cdot\phi_{\mathrm{CM}}\cdot|\rho^{\mathrm{src}}|)+O(\kappa_{{\rm init},L}\cdot\phi_{\mathrm{ED}}\cdot\rho_{2}(A_{2}))\leq O((\kappa_{\mathrm{CM},L}+\kappa_{{\rm init},L})\cdot\omega\cdot\phi\cdot\rho_{{\rm new}}(A_{{\rm new}})).

The quality of
\widetilde{\cal N}
is given by
Lemma

7.7
. Each router
R_{1}\in{\cal R}_{1}
is updated from some
R\in{\cal R}
via one matching insertion, so
\widetilde{f}=f+1
. For embedding
\Pi_{\widetilde{\cal R}\to G}
, we have

\displaystyle\widetilde{h}_{{\rm emb}}

\displaystyle=\max\{h_{{\rm emb}},h_{\mathrm{CM}},h_{{\rm emb},2}\}=\max\{h_{{\rm emb}},O(\lambda_{{\rm init},h}\cdot h)\},

\displaystyle\widetilde{\gamma}_{{\rm emb}}

\displaystyle=\gamma_{{\rm emb}}+\omega\cdot\gamma_{\mathrm{CM}}+\gamma_{{\rm emb},2}=\gamma_{{\rm emb}}=O((\omega\cdot\kappa_{\mathrm{CM},\gamma}+\kappa_{{\rm init},\gamma})\cdot\phi),

where the
\omega
factor of
\gamma_{\mathrm{CM}}
is because each matching edge will be added into routers at most
\omega
times (at most one in each clustering of
{\cal N}
).

7.7
.

\widetilde{\cal N}
is a
\widetilde{\rho}
-dense pairwise cover of
\widetilde{A}
on
G-\widetilde{C}
with
\widetilde{h}_{{\rm cov}}=\widetilde{h}_{\mathrm{sep}}=h/3
and
\widetilde{\omega}=\omega+\kappa_{\mathrm{NC},\omega}
.

Proof.

The proof of the separation and the width is exactly the same with that in the proof of
Lemma

6.11
, so we omit it here.

{\widetilde{\cal N}}
is
\widetilde{\rho}
-dense by the following reasons. First, when computing
{\cal N}_{1}
, each time we add a new virtual node
v
to some original cluster
S
, we will assign
\rho_{S}(v)\geq\rho_{{\rm new}}(v)
, so each cluster
S_{1}\in{\cal S}_{1}
has
\rho_{S_{1}}\geq\widetilde{\rho}(S_{1})
. Also, clusters in
{\cal N}_{2}
respect the density function
\widetilde{\rho}
by construction, so we can conclude that
\widetilde{\cal N}
is
\widetilde{\rho}
-dense.

In the remaining proof, we will show that
\widetilde{\cal N}
has cover radius
\widetilde{h}_{{\rm cov}}
. It is equivalent to show for each pair of virtual nodes
u,v\in\widetilde{A}
s.t.
\mathrm{dist}_{G-\widetilde{C}}(u,v)\leq\widetilde{h}_{{\rm cov}}
, there is a cluster
S\in\widetilde{\cal N}
containing both
u
and
v
. We will study by cases. One can check that the five cases below will cover all pairs of virtual nodes in
\widetilde{A}
.

Case 1.
If
u,v\in A
, then because
\mathrm{dist}_{G-C}(u,v)\leq\mathrm{dist}_{G-\widetilde{C}}(u,v)\leq\widetilde{h}_{{\rm cov}}\leq h_{{\rm cov}}
, there is a cluster
S\in{\cal N}
containing both
u,v
. By the construction of
{\cal N}_{1}
, the cluster
S_{1}
originated from
S
will have
u,v\in S_{1}
.

Case 2.
Suppose
u\in A_{{\rm new}}\cap A_{1}
and
v\in A
. For each item
u_{\mathrm{item}}\in\omega\cdot\rho_{{\rm new}}(u)
of
u
, it is matched to an item of some virtual node
u^{\prime}
via
M_{\mathrm{CM}}
. Observe that

\displaystyle\mathrm{dist}_{G-C}(u^{\prime},v)

\displaystyle\leq\mathrm{dist}_{G-C}(u^{\prime},u)+\mathrm{dist}_{G-C}(u,v)

\displaystyle\leq\mathrm{dist}_{G-C}(u^{\prime},u)+\mathrm{dist}_{G-\widetilde{C}}(u,v)

\displaystyle\leq h_{\mathrm{CM}}+\widetilde{h}_{{\rm cov}}\leq h_{{\rm cov}},

which means each
u
’s item
u_{\mathrm{item}}
is matched to some cluster containing
v
. Note that there are at most
\omega
clusters containing
v
in
{\cal N}
, and there are totally
\rho^{\mathrm{src}}(u)=\omega\cdot\rho_{{\rm new}}(u)
items of
u
in the matching
M_{\mathrm{CM}}
by the definition of
A_{1}
. By averaging, there must be a cluster
S\in{\cal N}
with
v\in S
that receives at least
\rho_{{\rm new}}(u)
items of
u
. Therefore, by the construction algorithm,
u
will be added to the cluster
S_{1}\in{\cal N}_{1}
originated from
S
.

Case 3.
Suppose both virtual nodes
u,v\in A_{{\rm new}}\cap A_{1}
. For each pair of units
u_{\mathrm{item}}\in\omega\cdot\rho_{{\rm new}}(u)
of
u
and
v_{\mathrm{item}}\in\omega\cdot\rho_{{\rm new}}(v)
of
v
,
M_{\mathrm{CM}}
will match
u_{\mathrm{item}}
and
v_{\mathrm{item}}
to items of some virtual nodes
u^{\prime}
and
v^{\prime}
respectively. Similarly,

\displaystyle\mathrm{dist}_{G-C}(u^{\prime},v^{\prime})

\displaystyle\leq\mathrm{dist}_{G-C}(u^{\prime},u)+\mathrm{dist}_{G-C}(u,v)+\mathrm{dist}_{G-C}(v^{\prime},v)

\displaystyle\leq\mathrm{dist}_{G-C}(u^{\prime},u)+\mathrm{dist}_{G-\widetilde{C}}(u,v)+\mathrm{dist}_{G-C}(v^{\prime},v)

\displaystyle\leq h_{\mathrm{CM}}+\widetilde{h}_{{\rm cov}}+h_{\mathrm{CM}}

\displaystyle\leq h_{{\rm cov}},

which means for each item pair
u_{\mathrm{item}}
and
v_{\mathrm{item}}
of
u
and
v
, there is at least a cluster that both
u_{\mathrm{item}}
and
v_{\mathrm{item}}
are matched to. Note that in each clustering of
{\cal N}
, there is at most one cluster that can receive items of
u
via
M_{\mathrm{CM}}
, because
{\cal N}
has separation
h_{\mathrm{sep}}>2h_{\mathrm{CM}}
. Hence the number of clusters that can receive both items of
u
and
v
via
M_{\mathrm{CM}}
is at most
\omega
(at most one from each clustering). The total number of such pairs of
x
and
y
is
\omega^{2}\rho_{{\rm new}}(u)\rho_{{\rm new}}(v)
. By averaging, there exists a cluster that receives at least
\omega\rho_{{\rm new}}(u)\rho_{{\rm new}}(v)
pairs, which means it receives at least
\rho_{{\rm new}}(u)
items of
u
and at least
\rho_{{\rm new}}(v)
items of
v
, because a cluster will receive at most
A^{\mathrm{src}}(u)=\omega\cdot\rho_{{\rm new}}(u)
and
A^{\mathrm{src}}(v)=\omega\cdot\rho_{{\rm new}}(v)
units of
u
and
v
respectively. Therefore, by the construction, both
u
and
v
will be added to this cluster.

Case 4.
Suppose
u\in A_{{\rm new}}\setminus A_{1}
and
v\in A_{2}
. By definition, the virtual node
u
has at least one unit in
A^{\mathrm{src}}_{U}
, so the vertex
u
is
h_{\mathrm{CM}}
-separated from vertices
\mathrm{supp}(A^{\mathrm{sink}}_{U})
on
G-\widetilde{C}
. This means
\mathrm{dist}_{(G-\widetilde{C})[A_{2}]}(u,v)=\mathrm{dist}_{G-\widetilde{C}}(u,v)
because the shortest
u
-
v
path on
G-\widetilde{C}
cannot go through any vertex in
\mathrm{supp}(A^{\mathrm{sink}}_{U})=\mathrm{supp}(\widetilde{A}\setminus A_{2})
. Therefore, a cluster from
{\cal N}_{2}
will contain both
u
and
v
.

Case 5.
Suppose
u\in A_{{\rm new}}\setminus A_{1}
and
v\in A\setminus A_{2}
. This case is impossible because we have argued that
u
is
h_{\mathrm{CM}}
-separated from the vertex
v\in\mathrm{supp}(A\setminus A_{2})=\mathrm{supp}(A^{\mathrm{src}}_{U})
on
G-\widetilde{C}
, contradicting that
\mathrm{dist}_{G-\widetilde{C}}(u,v)\leq h/3=h_{\mathrm{CM}}
.

∎

The Recourse from
{\cal N}
to
\widetilde{\cal N}
Restricted on
\widetilde{A}^{\star}
.

From
{\cal N}
to
{\cal N}_{1}
, each virtual node in
A_{{\rm new}}
will be added into
{\cal N}
at most
\omega
times (at most one for each clustering), so

\mathrm{Rcs}_{\mid\widetilde{A}^{\star}}({\cal N}\to{\cal N}_{1})\leq\omega\cdot|A_{{\rm new}}\cap\widetilde{A}^{\star}|\leq\omega\cdot\rho_{{\rm new}}(A_{{\rm new}})/\min_{v\in\widetilde{A}^{\star}}\widetilde{\rho}(v).

Because
\widetilde{\cal N}
is the union of
{\cal N}_{1}
and
{\cal N}_{2}
, trivially
\mathrm{Rcs}_{\mid\widetilde{A}^{\star}}({\cal N}_{1}\to\widetilde{\cal N})
is at most the size of
{\cal N}_{2}
restricted on
\widetilde{A}^{\star}
, i.e.
\mathrm{size}({\cal N}_{2,\mid\widetilde{A}^{\star}})
. Recall that
{\cal N}_{2}
is a pairwise cover of
A_{2}
with width
\kappa_{\mathrm{NC},\omega}
, so
\mathrm{size}({\cal N}_{2,\mid\widetilde{A}^{\star}})\leq\kappa_{\mathrm{NC},\omega}\cdot|A_{2}\cap\widetilde{A}^{\star}|
, so.

\displaystyle\mathrm{Rcs}_{\mid\widetilde{A}^{\star}}({\cal N}_{1}\to\widetilde{\cal N})

\displaystyle\leq\kappa_{\mathrm{NC},\omega}\cdot|A_{2}\cap\widetilde{A}^{\star}|\leq\kappa_{\mathrm{NC},\omega}\cdot\widetilde{\rho}(A_{2})/\min_{v\in\widetilde{A}^{\star}}\widetilde{\rho}(v).

Recall that we have shown
\widetilde{\rho}(A_{2})=\rho_{2}(A_{2})\leq O(\omega\cdot\rho_{{\rm new}}(A_{{\rm new}}))
, so

\displaystyle\mathrm{Rcs}_{\mid\widetilde{A}^{\star}}({\cal N}\to\widetilde{\cal N})

\displaystyle\leq\mathrm{Rcs}_{\mid\widetilde{A}^{\star}}({\cal N}\to{\cal N}_{1})+\mathrm{Rcs}_{\mid\widetilde{A}^{\star}}({\cal N}_{1}\to\widetilde{\cal N})\leq O(\kappa_{\mathrm{NC},\omega}\cdot\omega\cdot\rho_{{\rm new}}(A_{{\rm new}})/\min_{v\in\widetilde{A}^{\star}}\widetilde{\rho}(v)).

The Running Time.

The running time of the cutmatch subroutines is
|\rho^{\mathrm{src}}|\cdot\mathrm{poly}(h_{\mathrm{CM}},\log n)/\phi_{\mathrm{CM}}
.
When we update
({\cal N},{\cal R},\Pi_{{\cal R}\to G})
to
({\cal N}_{1},{\cal R}_{1},\Pi_{{\cal R}_{1}\to G})
, the running time is dominated by the update to routers. Because
\sum_{S\in{\cal S}}|M_{S}|\leq\omega\cdot|M_{\mathrm{CM}}|\leq O(\omega^{2}\cdot\rho_{{\rm new}}(A_{{\rm new}}))
, the total running time for this step is
2^{O(f)}\cdot\omega^{2}\cdot\rho_{{\rm new}}(A_{{\rm new}})\cdot 2^{\mathrm{poly}(1/\epsilon)}

by
Theorem

5.1
. The construction time of
(C_{\mathrm{ED}},L_{\mathrm{ED}},{\cal N}_{2},{\cal R}_{2},\Pi_{{\cal R}_{2}\to(G-C-C_{\mathrm{CM}})[A_{2}]})
is

\omega\cdot\rho_{{\rm new}}(A_{{\rm new}})\cdot\mathrm{poly}(h_{\mathrm{ED}})\cdot n^{O(\epsilon)}
,
because the size of subgraph
(G-C-C_{\mathrm{CM}})[A_{2}]
is bounded by
\deg_{G}(\mathrm{supp}(A_{2}))\leq\rho_{2}(A_{2})\leq O(\omega\cdot\rho_{{\rm new}}(A_{{\rm new}}))
, where the first inequality is because, for each vertex
v_{\mathrm{vtx}}\in\mathrm{supp}(A_{2})
, we have
\widetilde{A}(v_{\mathrm{vtx}})\subseteq A_{2}
and
\deg_{G}(v_{\mathrm{vtx}})\leq\widetilde{A}(v_{\mathrm{vtx}})\leq\sum_{v\in\widetilde{A}(v_{\mathrm{vtx}})}1\leq\sum_{v\in\widetilde{A}(v_{\mathrm{vtx}})}\rho_{2}(v)
.
In conclusion, the total running time is

\omega\cdot\rho_{{\rm new}}(A_{{\rm new}})\cdot\mathrm{poly}(h)\cdot n^{O(\epsilon)}\cdot(1/\phi+2^{O(f)}\cdot\omega).

∎

7.3
Batched Edge Deletions

When we apply
Theorem

7.8
in the proof of

Theorem

8.2
(i.e.
Section

8.2
), we will set the parameter
\mu=2t
, where
t=\Theta(1/\epsilon)
is the global time parameter.

7.8
.

Let
G
be a graph with a
\rho
-dense node-weighting
A\geq\deg_{G}+\mathds{1}(V(G))
and a
\rho
-dense certified-ED
(C,{\cal N},{\cal R},\Pi_{{\cal R}\to G})
of
A
on
G
in
Definition

7.3
. Given a set of edge deletions
F\subseteq E(G)
with parameters
\phi
and
\mu
, there is an algorithm that computes a
\tilde{\rho}
-dense certified ED
(C,L,{\cal N},{\cal R},\Pi_{{\cal R}\to G})
of
A
on
\widetilde{G}=G\setminus F
with
\widetilde{\rho}=\lceil(1-1/\mu)\rho\rceil
s.t.

•

\widetilde{C}=C_{\mid E(\widetilde{G})}+C_{{\rm new}}
with

|C_{{\rm new}}|\leq O(\kappa_{{\rm init},C}\cdot\omega\cdot\phi\cdot\mu\cdot\lambda_{\mathrm{rt},{\rm prune}}(f)\cdot\gamma_{{\rm emb}}\cdot|F|\cdot h)
.

•

\widetilde{L}=L\cup L_{{\rm new}}
with distortion distortion
\widetilde{\sigma}=\sigma+O(h/\kappa_{\sigma})
and

|L_{{\rm new}}|\leq O((\kappa_{\mathrm{CM},L}+\kappa_{{\rm init},L})\cdot\omega\cdot\phi\cdot\mu\cdot\lambda_{\mathrm{rt},{\rm prune}}(f)\cdot\gamma_{{\rm emb}}\cdot|F|)

•

{\cal N}
is a
\widetilde{\rho}
-dense
(\widetilde{h}_{{\rm cov}},\widetilde{h}_{\mathrm{sep}},\widetilde{\omega})
-pairwise cover with

\widetilde{h}_{{\rm cov}}=\widetilde{h}_{\mathrm{sep}}=h/3,\text{ and }\widetilde{\omega}=\omega+\kappa_{\mathrm{NC},\omega}.

•

Each router
\widetilde{R}\in\widetilde{\cal R}
is maintained by
Theorem

5.1
under
\widetilde{f}=f+O(1)
updates.

•

\Pi_{\widetilde{\cal R}\to G}
is a
(\widetilde{h}_{{\rm emb}},\widetilde{\gamma}_{{\rm emb}})
-embedding with

\displaystyle\widetilde{h}_{{\rm emb}}

\displaystyle=\max\{h_{{\rm emb}},\lambda_{{\rm init},h}\cdot h\}

\displaystyle\widetilde{\gamma}_{{\rm emb}}

\displaystyle=\gamma_{{\rm emb}}+O((\omega\cdot\kappa_{\mathrm{CM},\gamma}+\kappa_{{\rm init},\gamma})/\phi)

Given a node-weighting
\widetilde{A}^{\star}\subseteq\widetilde{A}
, the recourse from
{\cal N}
to
\widetilde{\cal N}
restricted on
\widetilde{A}^{\star}
is

\mathrm{Rcs}_{\mid\widetilde{A}^{\star}}({\cal N}\to\widetilde{\cal N})=O(\omega\cdot\kappa_{\mathrm{NC},\omega}\cdot\mu\cdot\lambda_{\mathrm{rt},{\rm prune}}(f)\cdot\gamma_{{\rm emb}}\cdot|F|/\min_{v\in\widetilde{A}^{\star}}\rho(v)).

The running time is
\omega\cdot\mu\cdot\lambda_{\mathrm{rt},{\rm prune}}(f)\cdot\gamma_{{\rm emb}}\cdot|F|\cdot\mathrm{poly}(h)\cdot n^{O(\epsilon)}\cdot(1/\phi+2^{O(f)}\cdot\omega)
.

Proof.

We first compute the set
{\cal R}_{\mathrm{edge},F}=\Pi^{-1}_{{\cal R}\to G}(F)
which collects all edges in routers with underlying embedding paths going through some deleted edges in
F
. For each router
R\in{\cal R}
corresponding to a cluster
S\in{\cal N}
, let
R_{\mathrm{edge},F}={\cal R}_{\mathrm{edge},F}\cap E(R)
. We apply an edge-deletions update to
R
with
R_{\mathrm{edge},F}
as inputs by
Theorem

5.1
, which returns a set
R_{{\rm prune}}\subseteq V(R)
of pruned vertices. Recall that each router vertex in
R_{{\rm prune}}
is either a redundant vertex or corresponding to an item in
\rho_{S}(S)
. For each virtual node
v\in S
, let
R_{{\rm prune}}(v)
denote the number of
v
’s item in
R_{{\rm prune}}
. We define the pruned virtual nodes of
S
to be

S_{{\rm prune}}=\{v\in S\mid R_{{\rm prune}}(v)\geq\lceil\rho(v)/\mu\rceil\}.

Finally, let
A_{{\rm prune}}=\bigcup_{S\in{\cal N}}S_{{\rm prune}}
collect all pruned virtual nodes.

Now we partition
A
into
A_{{\rm prune}}
and
A^{\prime}=A\setminus A_{{\rm prune}}
, and let the density of
A_{{\rm prune}}
be
\rho_{{\rm prune}}=\rho_{\mid A_{{\rm prune}}}
and the density of
A^{\prime}
be
\rho^{\prime}=\lceil(1-1/\mu)\rho_{\mid A^{\prime}}\rceil
. Note that we can obtain a
\rho^{\prime}
-dense certified-ED
(C^{\prime},L^{\prime},{\cal N}^{\prime},{\cal R}^{\prime},\Pi_{{\cal R}^{\prime}\to\widetilde{G}})
of
A^{\prime}
on
\widetilde{G}
by simply removing
A_{{\rm prune}}
from
(C,L,{\cal N},{\cal R},\Pi_{{\cal R}\to G})
. Concretely, for each cluster
S\in{\cal N}
and its router
R
, we do the following steps.

1.

Remove virtual nodes
A_{{\rm prune}}\cap S
from
S
. Note that the new
R
after the pruning from
Theorem

5.1
may still have vertices corresponding to items of some virtual node in
A_{{\rm prune}}\cap S
and we will mark all such router vertices redundant.

2.

For each remaining virtual node
v
in
S
, subtract its
S
-density
\rho_{S}(v)
by
R_{{\rm prune}}(v)
, because these many items of
v
were pruned from
R
via
Theorem

5.1
before.

3.

For each edge removed from
R
due to the previous update via
Theorem

5.1
, remove its embedding path from
\Pi_{{\cal R}\to G}
.

Lastly, we take
C^{\prime}=C_{\mid E(\widetilde{G})}
by removing cut values on deleted edges. Also, let
L^{\prime}=L\cup V(F)
.

We observe that the
\rho^{\prime}
-dense certified-ED
(C^{\prime},L^{\prime},{\cal N}^{\prime},{\cal R}^{\prime},\Pi_{{\cal R}^{\prime}\to\widetilde{G}})
of
A^{\prime}
on
\widetilde{G}
has the same quality parameters as those of the original
\rho
-dense certified-ED
(C,L,{\cal N},{\cal R},\Pi_{{\cal R}\to G})
of
A
on
G
, except that each router in
{\cal R^{\prime}}
suffers one more batched update. In particular,
\Pi_{{\cal R}^{\prime}\to\widetilde{G}}
is a valid embedding on
\widetilde{G}
because we remove all router edges with embedding paths going through deleted edges by
Theorem

5.1
, and these embedding paths are removed in step (3). Also, the new cluster
{\cal N}^{\prime}
is
\rho^{\prime}
-dense because for each cluster
S^{\prime}\in{\cal N^{\prime}}
(updated from
S\in{\cal N}
) and virtual node
v\in S^{\prime}
, we have

\rho_{S^{\prime}}(v)=\rho_{S}(v)-R_{{\rm prune}}(v)\geq\rho(v)-R_{{\rm prune}}(v)\geq\lceil(1-1/\mu)\cdot\rho(v)\rceil=\rho^{\prime}(v)

because
R_{{\rm prune}}(v)\leq\lfloor\rho(v)/\mu\rfloor
(otherwise
v
will be selected into
S_{{\rm prune}}\subseteq A_{{\rm prune}}
and it will not belong to
S^{\prime}
).

We can obtain a
\widetilde{\rho}
-dense certified-ED
(\widetilde{C},\widetilde{L},\widetilde{\cal N},\widetilde{\cal R},\Pi_{\widetilde{\cal R}\to G})
of
A
on
\widetilde{G}
by applying
Theorem

7.5
on
(C^{\prime},L^{\prime},{\cal N}^{\prime},{\cal R}^{\prime},\Pi_{{\cal R}^{\prime}\to\widetilde{G}})
with the new
\rho_{{\rm prune}}
-dense node weighting
A_{{\rm prune}}
. The output has the desired quality by plugging the bound of
\rho_{{\rm prune}}(A_{{\rm prune}})
(see
7.9
) into the quality bounds of
Theorem

7.5
.

7.9
.

\rho_{{\rm prune}}(A_{{\rm prune}})=\rho(A_{{\rm prune}})\leq\mu\cdot\lambda_{\mathrm{rt},{\rm prune}}(f)\cdot\gamma_{{\rm emb}}\cdot|F|

Proof.

First, the number of router edges with embedding paths going through deleted edges, i.e.
|{\cal R}_{\mathrm{edge},F}|
, is at most
|F|\cdot\gamma_{{\rm emb}}
. By definition,
\sum_{R\in{\cal R}}|R_{\mathrm{edge},F}|=|{\cal R}_{\mathrm{edge},F}|
. By
Theorem

5.1
, each router
R\in{\cal R}
has
|R_{{\rm prune}}|\leq\lambda_{\mathrm{rt},{\rm prune}}(f)\cdot|R_{\mathrm{edge},F}|
. Therefore, we have

\displaystyle\rho_{{\rm prune}}(A_{{\rm prune}})

\displaystyle=\rho(A_{{\rm prune}})=\sum_{v\in A_{{\rm prune}}}\rho(v)\leq\sum_{S\in{\cal N}}\sum_{v\in S_{{\rm prune}}}\rho(v)\leq\sum_{S\in{\cal N}}\sum_{v\in S_{{\rm prune}}}\mu\cdot R_{{\rm prune}}(v)

\displaystyle\leq\mu\cdot\sum_{S\in{\cal N}}|R_{{\rm prune}}|\leq\mu\cdot\lambda_{\mathrm{rt},{\rm prune}}(f)\cdot\sum_{S\in{\cal N}}|R_{\mathrm{edge},F}|=\mu\cdot\lambda_{\mathrm{rt},{\rm prune}}(f)\cdot|{\cal R}_{\mathrm{edge},F}|

\displaystyle\leq\mu\cdot\lambda_{\mathrm{rt},{\rm prune}}(f)\cdot\gamma_{{\rm emb}}\cdot|F|.

∎

The Recourse from
{\cal N}
to
\widetilde{\cal N}
Restricted on
\widetilde{A}^{\star}
.

Because
{\cal N}^{\prime}
is obtained from
{\cal N}
by removing virtual nodes in
A_{{\rm prune}}
and the width is
\omega
, we have

\mathrm{Rcs}_{\mid\widetilde{A}^{\star}}({\cal N}\to{\cal N}^{\prime})\leq\omega\cdot|A_{{\rm prune}}\cap\widetilde{A}^{\star}|\leq\rho(A_{{\rm prune}})/\min_{v\in\widetilde{A}^{\star}}\rho(v).

From
{\cal N^{\prime}}
to
\widetilde{\cal N}
, by
Theorem

7.5
, we have

\mathrm{Rcs}_{\mid\widetilde{A}^{\star}}({\cal N}^{\prime}\to\widetilde{\cal N})\leq O(\rho_{{\rm prune}}(A_{{\rm prune}})\cdot\omega\cdot\kappa_{\mathrm{NC},\omega}/\min_{v\in\widetilde{A}^{\star}}\rho(v)).

Therefore, by
7.9
, the total recourse is

\displaystyle\mathrm{Rcs}_{\mid\widetilde{A}^{\star}}({\cal N}\to\widetilde{\cal N})

\displaystyle\leq O(\rho(A_{{\rm prune}})\cdot\omega\cdot\kappa_{\mathrm{NC},\omega}/\min_{v\in\widetilde{A}^{\star}}\rho(v))

\displaystyle\leq O(\omega\cdot\kappa_{\mathrm{NC},\omega}\cdot\mu\cdot\lambda_{\mathrm{rt},{\rm prune}}(f)\cdot\gamma_{{\rm emb}}\cdot|F|/\min_{v\in\widetilde{A}^{\star}}\rho(v)).

The Running Time.

The running time to update
(C,L,{\cal N},{\cal R},\Pi_{{\cal R}\to G})
to
(C^{\prime},L^{\prime},{\cal N}^{\prime},{\cal R}^{\prime},\Pi_{{\cal R}^{\prime}\to\widetilde{G}})
is dominated the edge deletion updates to the routers, which take totally

2^{O(f)}\cdot|{\cal R}_{\mathrm{edge},F}|\cdot 2^{\mathrm{poly}(1/\epsilon)}\leq 2^{O(f)}\cdot|F|\cdot\gamma_{{\rm emb}}\cdot 2^{\mathrm{poly}(1/\epsilon)}

time. From
(C^{\prime},L^{\prime},{\cal N}^{\prime},{\cal R}^{\prime},\Pi_{{\cal R}^{\prime}\to\widetilde{G}})
to
(\widetilde{C},\widetilde{L},\widetilde{\cal N},\widetilde{\cal R},\Pi_{\widetilde{\cal R}\to\widetilde{G}})
, it takes

\omega\cdot\rho_{{\rm prune}}(A_{{\rm prune}})\cdot\mathrm{poly}(h)\cdot n^{O(\epsilon)}\cdot(1/\phi+2^{O(f+1)}\cdot\omega)

time. Therefore, the total running time is

\omega\cdot\mu\cdot\lambda_{\mathrm{rt},{\rm prune}}(f)\cdot\gamma_{{\rm emb}}\cdot|F|\cdot\mathrm{poly}(h)\cdot n^{O(\epsilon)}\cdot(1/\phi+2^{O(f)}\cdot\omega).

∎

7.4
Batched Edge Insertions

7.10
.

Let
G
be a graph with a
\rho
-dense node-weighting
A\geq\deg_{G}+\mathds{1}(V(G))
and a
\rho
-dense certified-ED
(C,L,{\cal N},{\cal R},\Pi_{{\cal R}\to G})
of
A
on
G
in
Definition

7.3
. Given a batch of edge insertions
E_{{\rm new}}
with parameter
\phi
, there is an algorithm that computes a certified-ED
(\widetilde{C},\widetilde{L},\widetilde{{\cal N}},\widetilde{{\cal R}},\Pi_{\widetilde{\cal R}\to\widetilde{G}})
of a
\widetilde{\rho}
-dense
\widetilde{A}
on
\widetilde{G}=G\cup E_{{\rm new}}
satisfying that

•

\widetilde{A}=A+A_{{\rm new}}
and
\widetilde{\rho}=\rho\uplus\rho_{{\rm new}}
, where
A_{{\rm new}}=\deg_{E_{{\rm new}}}
with unit density
\rho_{{\rm new}}
.

•

\widetilde{C}=C+C_{{\rm new}}
with
|C_{{\rm new}}|\leq O((\omega\cdot\kappa_{{\rm init},C}\cdot\phi+1)\cdot h\cdot|E_{{\rm new}}|)
.

•

\widetilde{L}=L\cup L_{{\rm new}}
with distortion
\widetilde{\sigma}=\sigma+O(h/\kappa_{\sigma})
and

|L_{{\rm new}}|\leq O((\omega\cdot(\kappa_{\mathrm{CM},L}+\kappa_{{\rm init},L})\cdot\phi+1)\cdot|E_{{\rm new}}|);

•

\widetilde{N}
is a
\widetilde{\rho}
-dense
(\widetilde{h}_{{\rm cov}},\widetilde{h}_{\mathrm{sep}},\widetilde{\omega})
-pairwise cover with

\widetilde{h}_{{\rm cov}}=h/3,\ \widetilde{h}_{\mathrm{sep}}=h/3\text{ and }\widetilde{\omega}=\omega+\kappa_{\mathrm{NC},\omega}

•

Routers in
\widetilde{\cal R}
are maintained by
Theorem

5.1
under
f+1
updates.

•

\Pi_{\widetilde{\cal R}\to G}
is a
(\widetilde{h}_{{\rm emb}},\widetilde{\gamma}_{{\rm emb}})
-embedding with

\displaystyle\widetilde{h}_{{\rm emb}}

\displaystyle=\max\{h_{{\rm emb}},O(\lambda_{{\rm init},h}\cdot h)\}

\displaystyle\widetilde{\gamma}_{{\rm emb}}

\displaystyle=\gamma_{{\rm emb}}+O((\omega\cdot\kappa_{\mathrm{CM},\gamma}+\kappa_{{\rm init},\gamma})/\phi)

The recourse from
{\cal N}
to
\widetilde{\cal N}
is
\mathrm{Rcs}({\cal N}\to\widetilde{\cal N})=O(|E_{{\rm new}}|\cdot\omega\cdot\kappa_{\mathrm{NC},\omega})
.
The running time is
|E_{{\rm new}}|\cdot\mathrm{poly}(h)\cdot n^{O(\epsilon)}\cdot(1/\phi+2^{O(f)}\cdot\omega)
.

Proof.

We first update
(C,L,{\cal N},{\cal R},\Pi_{{\cal R}\to G})
to
(C^{\prime},L^{\prime},{\cal N},{\cal R},\Pi_{{\cal R}\to\widetilde{G}})
as follows.

1.

Let
C^{\prime}=C+C_{E_{{\rm new}}}
, where
C_{E_{{\rm new}}}
assigns
C_{E_{{\rm new}}}(e)=h
to each
e\in E_{{\rm new}}
.

2.

Let
L^{\prime}=L\cup L_{E_{{\rm new}}}
, where
L_{E_{{\rm new}}}=V(E_{{\rm new}})
includes all endpoints of new edges, so the
C_{E_{{\rm new}}}
-vertices have themselves as landmarks.

3.

Keep
\Pi_{{\cal R}\to\widetilde{G}}=\Pi_{{\cal R}\to G}
unchanged.

Obviously,
(C^{\prime},L^{\prime},{\cal N},{\cal R},\Pi_{{\cal R}\to\widetilde{G}})
is a
\rho
-dense certified-ED of
A
on
\widetilde{G}
with quality parameters the same as those of
(C,L,{\cal N},{\cal R},\Pi_{{\cal R}\to G})
.

Next, let
A_{{\rm new}}=\deg_{E_{{\rm new}}}
with density
\rho_{{\rm new}}(v)=1
for each virtual node
v\in A_{{\rm new}}
. Then apply
Theorem

7.5
on the
\rho
-dense certified-ED
(C^{\prime},L^{\prime},{\cal N},{\cal R},\Pi_{{\cal R}\to\widetilde{G}})
of
A
on
\widetilde{G}
with the new
\rho_{{\rm new}}
-dense node-weighing
A_{{\rm new}}
. The output
(\widetilde{C},\widetilde{L},\widetilde{\cal N},\widetilde{\cal R},\Pi_{\widetilde{\cal R}\to\widetilde{G}})
is exactly a
\widetilde{\rho}
-dense certified-ED of
\widetilde{A}=A+A_{{\rm new}}
on
\widetilde{G}
with density
\widetilde{\rho}=\rho\uplus\rho_{{\rm new}}
and the desired quality parameters.

By
Theorem

7.5
, the recourse from
{\cal N}
to
\widetilde{\cal N}
is at most

O(\rho_{{\rm new}}(A_{{\rm new}})\cdot\omega\cdot\kappa_{\mathrm{NC},\omega})\leq O(|E_{{\rm new}}|\cdot\omega\cdot\kappa_{\mathrm{NC},\omega})
, and the running time is
|E_{{\rm new}}|\cdot\mathrm{poly}(h)\cdot n^{O(\epsilon)}\cdot(1/\phi+2^{O(f)}\cdot\omega)
.

∎

7.5
Batched Landmark Insertions into Node-Weighting

7.11
.

Let
G
be a graph with node-weighting
A\geq\deg_{G}
and parameter
\phi
. For each
1\leq j\leq\bar{j}
, suppose we are given a
\rho
-dense certified-ED
(C_{j},L_{j},{\cal N}_{j},{\cal R}_{j},\Pi_{{\cal R}_{j}\to G})
of
A
on
G
s.t.

•

L_{j}
has distortion
\sigma_{j}
and a subset
L^{\star}_{j}\subseteq L_{j}
specified, and let
\tau=\sum_{1\leq j\leq\bar{j}}|L^{\star}_{j}|
;

•

{\cal N}_{j}
is a
(h_{{\rm cov},j},h_{\mathrm{sep},j},\omega_{j})
-pairwise cover of
A
on
G
with
h_{{\rm cov},j}=h_{\mathrm{sep},j}=h_{j}
;

•

{\cal R}_{j}
are maintained by
Theorem

5.1
under
f_{j}
updates;

•

\Pi_{{\cal R}_{j}\to G}
is a
(h_{{\rm emb},j},\gamma_{{\rm emb},j})
-embedding.

We define global parameters
\kappa_{\mathrm{insLM},\gamma}=n^{O(\epsilon^{4})}
and
\lambda_{\mathrm{insLM},t}=\log_{\kappa_{\mathrm{insLM},\gamma}}n=O(1/\epsilon^{4})
. There is an algorithm that computes, for each
1\leq j\leq\bar{j}
, a
\widetilde{\rho}
-dense certified-ED
(\widetilde{C}_{j},\widetilde{L}_{j},\widetilde{\cal N}_{j},\widetilde{\cal R}_{j},\Pi_{\widetilde{\cal R}_{j}\to G})
of
\widetilde{A}
on
G
s.t.

•

\widetilde{C}_{j}=C_{j}+C_{{\rm new},j}
with
|C_{{\rm new},j}|=O(h_{j}\cdot\tau)

•

\widetilde{L}_{j}=L_{j}\cup L_{{\rm new},j}
with distortion
\widetilde{\sigma}_{j}=\sigma_{j}+O(h_{j}/\kappa_{\sigma})
and
|L_{{\rm new},j}|=O(\tau)
.

•

\widetilde{A}=A+A_{{\rm new}}
with density
\widetilde{\rho}=\rho\uplus\rho_{{\rm new}}
, where

A_{{\rm new}}=\mathds{1}(\bigcup_{j}L^{\star}_{j})+\mathds{1}(\bigcup_{j}L_{{\rm new},j}\setminus\bigcup_{j}L_{j}),

and
A_{{\rm new}}
has
1/\phi
-uniform density
\rho_{{\rm new}}
.

•

\widetilde{N}_{j}
is a
(\widetilde{h}_{{\rm cov},j},\widetilde{h}_{\mathrm{sep},j},\widetilde{\omega}_{j})
-pairwise cover of
\widetilde{A}
on
G
with

\widetilde{h}_{{\rm cov},j}=\widetilde{h}_{\mathrm{sep},j}=h_{j}/2^{O(\lambda_{\mathrm{insLM},t})}\text{ and }\widetilde{\omega}_{j}=\omega_{j}+\lambda_{\mathrm{insLM},t}\cdot\kappa_{\mathrm{NC},\omega}.

•

Routers in
\widetilde{\cal R}_{j}
are maintained by
Theorem

5.1
under at most
\widetilde{f}_{j}=f_{j}+O(\lambda_{\mathrm{insLM},t})
update batches.

•

\Pi_{\widetilde{\cal R}_{j}\to G}
is a
(\widetilde{h}_{{\rm emb},j},\widetilde{\gamma}_{{\rm emb},j})
-embedding with

\displaystyle\widetilde{h}_{{\rm emb},j}

\displaystyle=\max\{h_{{\rm emb},j},O(\lambda_{{\rm init},h}\cdot h_{j})\},

\displaystyle\widetilde{\gamma}_{{\rm emb},j}

\displaystyle=\gamma_{{\rm emb},j}+O(\widetilde{\omega}_{j}\cdot(\kappa_{\mathrm{CM},L}+\kappa_{{\rm init},L})\cdot(\widetilde{\omega}_{j}\cdot\kappa_{\mathrm{CM},\gamma}+\kappa_{{\rm init},\gamma})\cdot\kappa_{\mathrm{insLM},\gamma}\cdot\lambda_{\mathrm{insLM},t}/\phi)

Given an arbitrary set
\widetilde{A}^{\star}
of virtual nodes, the recourse from
{\cal N}_{j}
to
\widetilde{\cal N}_{j}
restricted on
\widetilde{A}^{\star}
is

\mathrm{Rcs}_{\mid\widetilde{A}^{\star}}({\cal N}_{j}\to\widetilde{\cal N}_{j})=O(\tau\cdot\widetilde{\omega}_{j}\cdot\kappa_{\mathrm{NC},\omega}/(\phi\cdot\min_{v\in\widetilde{A}^{\star}\cap\widetilde{A}}\widetilde{\rho}(v))).

The running time is
(\tau/\phi)\cdot n^{O(\epsilon)}\cdot\sum_{j}(\widetilde{\omega}_{j}^{2}\cdot\mathrm{poly}(h_{j})\cdot(1/\phi+2^{O(\widetilde{f}_{j})}))
.

Proof.

Roughly speaking, this subroutine wants to add the landmarks
\bigcup_{j}L^{\star}_{j}
into the node-weighting. This can be done by calling the dynamic ED subroutine, at a cost of creating more landmarks. Hence our algorithm is iterative. Let
L^{\star,(i)}_{j}
be the new landmarks created in iteration
i
of the level-
j
certified-ED (initially
L^{\star,(0)}_{j}=L^{\star}_{j}
). Then at each iteration
i
, we add
\bigcup_{j}L^{\star,(i-1)}_{j}
(excluding repetitive landmarks) into the node-weighting of all certified-EDs, which will create new landmarks
L^{\star,(i)}_{j}
for the next iteration. We will set proper parameters to make sure the number of new landmarks drops by a factor roughly
\kappa_{\mathrm{insLM},L}
in each iteration, so the number of iteration is bounded by
\lambda_{\mathrm{insLM},t}
.

Formally, let
(C_{j}^{(0)},L_{j}^{(0)},{\cal N}_{j}^{(0)},{\cal R}_{j}^{(0)},\Pi_{{\cal R}_{j}^{(0)}\to G})
be the initial certified-ED for each
j
. In each iteration
i\geq 1
, we let

T_{{\rm new}}^{(i)}=\bigcup_{j}L^{\star,(i-1)}_{j}\setminus\bigcup_{j}L^{(i-2)}_{j}\text{ (initially $T^{(1)}_{{\rm new}}=\bigcup_{j}L^{\star,(0)}_{j}$)}

If
T^{(i)}_{{\rm new}}
is empty, we terminate the algorithm.

Otherwise, let
A_{{\rm new}}^{(i)}=\mathds{1}(T^{(i)}_{{\rm new}})
be the unit node-weighting over
T^{(i)}_{{\rm new}}
with
1/\phi
-uniform density
\rho^{(i)}_{{\rm new}}
. Then we call
Theorem

7.5
on
(C_{j}^{(i-1)},L_{j}^{(i-1)},{\cal N}_{j}^{(i-1)},{\cal R}_{j}^{(i-1)},\Pi_{{\cal R}_{j}^{(i-1)}\to G})
with the
\rho^{(i)}_{{\rm new}}
-dense node-weighting
A^{(i)}_{{\rm new}}
and parameter

\phi_{j}^{(i)}=\phi/(\kappa_{\mathrm{insLM},\gamma}\cdot\omega^{(i-1)}_{j}\cdot(\kappa_{\mathrm{CM},L}+\kappa_{{\rm init},L})).

Let
h^{(i-1)}_{j}
refer to
h^{(i-1)}_{{\rm cov},j}
. The output is a
\rho^{(i)}
-dense certified-ED
(C_{j}^{(i)},L_{j}^{(i)},{\cal N}_{j}^{(i)},{\cal R}_{j}^{(i)},\Pi_{{\cal R}_{j}^{(i)}\to G})
of
A^{(i)}=A^{(i-1)}+A^{(i)}_{{\rm new}}
on
G
with density
\rho^{(i)}=\rho^{(i-1)}\uplus\rho^{(i)}_{{\rm new}}
. It further satisfies that

•

C_{j}^{(i)}=C_{j}^{(i-1)}+C^{(i)}_{{\rm new},j}
with

\displaystyle|C^{(i)}_{{\rm new},j}|\leq O(\kappa_{{\rm init},C}\cdot\omega_{j}^{(i-1)}\cdot\phi_{j}^{(i)}\cdot h^{(i-1)}_{j}\cdot\rho^{(i)}_{{\rm new}}(A^{(i)}_{{\rm new}}))\leq O(h^{(i-1)}_{j}\cdot|A^{(i)}_{{\rm new}}|/\kappa_{\mathrm{insLM},\gamma}),

where the second inequality is by
\rho^{(i)}_{{\rm new}}(A^{(i)}_{{\rm new}})=|A^{(i)}_{{\rm new}}|/\phi
and
\kappa_{\mathrm{CM},L}+\kappa_{{\rm init},L}\geq\kappa_{{\rm init},C}
.

•

L_{j}^{(i)}=L_{j}^{(i-1)}\cup L^{\star,(i)}_{j}
has distortion
\sigma^{(i)}_{j}=\sigma^{(i-1)}_{j}+O(h^{(i-1)}_{j}/\kappa_{\sigma})
and

|L^{\star,(i)}_{j}|\leq O((\kappa_{\mathrm{CM},L}+\kappa_{{\rm init},L})\cdot\omega^{(i-1)}_{j}\cdot\phi_{j}^{(i)}\cdot\rho^{(i)}_{{\rm new}}(A^{(i)}_{{\rm new}}))\leq O(|A^{(i)}_{{\rm new}}|/\kappa_{\mathrm{insLM},\gamma}).

•

{\cal N}^{(i)}_{j}
has quality parameters

h^{(i)}_{{\rm cov},j}=h^{(i)}_{\mathrm{sep},j}=h^{(i-1)}_{{\rm cov},j}/3,\text{ and }\omega^{(i)}_{j}=\omega^{(i-1)}_{j}+\kappa_{\mathrm{NC},\omega}.

•

Routers in
{\cal R}^{(i)}_{j}
are maintained by
Theorem

5.1
under
f^{(i)}_{j}=f^{(i-1)}_{j}+O(1)
batched updates.

•

The embedding
\Pi_{{\cal R}^{(i)}_{j}\to G}
has

\displaystyle h^{(i)}_{{\rm emb},j}

\displaystyle=\max\{h^{(i-1)}_{{\rm emb},j},O(\lambda_{{\rm init},h}\cdot h^{(i)}_{j})\}

\displaystyle\gamma^{(i)}_{{\rm emb},j}

\displaystyle=\gamma^{(i-1)}_{{\rm emb},j}+O((\omega^{(i-1)}_{j}\cdot\kappa_{\mathrm{CM},\gamma}+\kappa_{{\rm init},\gamma})/\phi_{j}^{(i)})

\displaystyle=\gamma^{(i-1)}_{{\rm emb},j}+O(\omega^{(i-1)}_{j}\cdot(\kappa_{\mathrm{CM},L}+\kappa_{{\rm init},L})\cdot(\omega^{(i-1)}_{j}\cdot\kappa_{\mathrm{CM},\gamma}+\kappa_{{\rm init},\gamma})\cdot\kappa_{\mathrm{insLM},\gamma}/\phi)

7.12
.

The number of iteration is at most
\lambda_{\mathrm{insLM},t}
, and
\sum_{i}|A^{(i)}_{{\rm new}}|\leq O(\tau)
.

Proof.

For each
i\geq 2
,
|A^{(i)}_{{\rm new}}|\leq\sum_{j}|L^{\star,(i-1)}_{j}|\leq O(\bar{j}\cdot|A^{(i-1)}_{{\rm new}}|/\kappa_{\mathrm{insLM},\gamma})
. Because
|A^{(1)}_{{\rm new}}|\leq n
,
\kappa_{\mathrm{insLM},\gamma}=n^{O(\epsilon)}
and
\bar{j}=O(\log h)=O(\log n)
, after at most
O(1/\epsilon)\leq\lambda_{\mathrm{insLM},t}
iterations,
|L^{(i)}_{{\rm new}}|=|A^{(i)}_{{\rm new}}|<1
and the algorithm terminates. Because
|A^{(1)}_{{\rm new}}|\leq\sum_{j}|L^{\star,(0)}_{j}|=\tau
and
|A^{(i)}_{{\rm new}}|
decreases exponentially over time, we have
\sum_{i}|A^{(i)}_{{\rm new}}|=O(\tau)
.
∎

Quality of the Final Certified-EDs.

For each
j
, the final (after the last iteration) certified-EDs
(\widetilde{C}_{j},\widetilde{L}_{j},\widetilde{\cal N}_{j},\widetilde{\cal R}_{j},\Pi_{\widetilde{\cal R}_{j}\to G})
has qualtiy parameters as follows.

•

\widetilde{C}_{j}=C_{j}+C_{{\rm new},j}
, where
C_{{\rm new},j}=\sum_{i}C_{{\rm new},j}^{(i)}
with size

\displaystyle|C_{{\rm new},j}|

\displaystyle=\sum_{i}|C_{{\rm new},j}^{(i)}|\leq\sum_{i}O(h_{j}^{(i-1)}\cdot|A^{(i)}_{{\rm new}}|/\kappa_{\mathrm{insLM},\gamma})\leq O(h_{j}\cdot\tau/\kappa_{\mathrm{insLM},\gamma}).

•

\widetilde{L}_{j}=L_{j}\cup L_{{\rm new},j}
, where
L_{{\rm new},j}=\bigcup_{i}L_{j}^{\star,(i)}
with size

|L_{{\rm new},j}|=\sum_{i}|L_{j}^{\star,(i)}|\leq\sum_{i}O(|A^{(i)}_{{\rm new}}|/\kappa_{\mathrm{insLM},\gamma})\leq O(\tau/\kappa_{\mathrm{insLM},\gamma}).

•

\widetilde{A}=A+A_{{\rm new}}
, where

\displaystyle A_{{\rm new}}

\displaystyle=\sum_{i}A^{(i)}_{{\rm new}}=\sum_{i}\mathds{1}(T^{(i)}_{{\rm new}})

\displaystyle=\mathds{1}(\bigcup_{j}L^{\star,(0)}_{j})+\sum_{i\geq 2}\mathds{1}(\bigcup_{j}L^{\star,(i-1)}_{j}\setminus\bigcup_{j}L^{(i-2)}_{j})

\displaystyle=\mathds{1}(\bigcup_{j}L^{\star,(0)}_{j})+\mathds{1}(\bigcup_{i\geq 2,j}L^{\star,(i-1)}_{j}\setminus\bigcup_{j}L^{(0)}_{j})

\displaystyle=\mathds{1}(\bigcup_{j}L^{\star}_{j})+\mathds{1}(\bigcup_{j}L_{{\rm new},j}\setminus\bigcup_{j}L_{j})

and
A_{{\rm new}}
has
1/\phi
-uniform density
\rho_{{\rm new}}
.

•

The bounds on
\widetilde{h}_{{\rm cov},j},\widetilde{h}_{\mathrm{sep},j},\widetilde{\omega}_{j}
and
\widetilde{f}_{j}
are trivial by
7.12
.

•

The embedding
\Pi_{\widetilde{\cal R}_{j}\to G}
Trivially has length
\widetilde{h}_{{\rm emb},j}=\max\{h_{{\rm emb},j},O(\lambda_{{\rm init},h}\cdot h_{j})\}
.
Regarding the congestion, because in each iteration
i
,

\gamma^{(i)}_{{\rm emb},j}=\gamma^{(i-1)}_{{\rm emb},j}+O(\omega^{(i-1)}_{j}\cdot(\kappa_{\mathrm{CM},L}+\kappa_{{\rm init},L})\cdot(\omega^{(i-1)}_{j}\cdot\kappa_{\mathrm{CM},\gamma}+\kappa_{{\rm init},\gamma})\cdot\kappa_{\mathrm{insLM},\gamma}/\phi),

and the number of iteration at most
\lambda_{\mathrm{insLM},t}
, the final congestion is

\displaystyle\widetilde{\gamma}_{{\rm emb},j}=\gamma_{{\rm emb},j}+O(\widetilde{\omega}_{j}\cdot(\kappa_{\mathrm{CM},L}+\kappa_{{\rm init},L})\cdot(\widetilde{\omega}_{j}\cdot\kappa_{\mathrm{CM},\gamma}+\kappa_{{\rm init},\gamma})\cdot\kappa_{\mathrm{insLM},\gamma}\cdot\lambda_{\mathrm{insLM},t}/\phi).

The Recourse from
{\cal N}_{j}
to
\widetilde{\cal N}_{j}
Restricted on
\widetilde{A}^{\star}
.

We have

\displaystyle\mathrm{Rcs}_{\mid\widetilde{A}^{\star}}({\cal N}_{j}\to\widetilde{\cal N}_{j})\leq\sum_{i\geq 1}\mathrm{Rcs}_{\mid(\widetilde{A}^{\star}\cap A^{(i)})}({\cal N}_{j}^{(i-1)}\to{\cal N}_{j}^{(i)})

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{i\geq 1}O(\rho_{{\rm new}}^{(i)}(A_{{\rm new}}^{(i)})\cdot\omega^{(i-1)}_{j}\cdot\kappa_{\mathrm{NC},\omega}/\min_{v\in\widetilde{A}^{\star}\cap A^{(i)}}\rho^{(i)}(v))

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{i\geq 1}O(|A_{{\rm new}}^{(i)}|\cdot\omega^{(i-1)}_{j}\cdot\kappa_{\mathrm{NC},\omega}/(\phi\cdot\min_{v\in\widetilde{A}^{\star}\cap A^{(i)}}\rho^{(i)}(v)))

\displaystyle\leq O(\tau\cdot\widetilde{\omega}_{j}\cdot\kappa_{\mathrm{NC},\omega}/(\phi\cdot\min_{v\in\widetilde{A}^{\star}\cap\widetilde{A}}\widetilde{\rho}(v))),

where the second inequality is by
Theorem

7.5
.

The Running Time.

At each iteration
i
and for each
j
, the time to get
(C_{j}^{(i)},L_{j}^{(i)},{\cal N}_{j}^{(i)},{\cal R}_{j}^{(i)},\Pi_{{\cal R}_{j}^{(i)}\to G})
is

\omega_{j}^{(i-1)}\cdot\rho_{{\rm new}}^{(i)}(A_{{\rm new}}^{(i)})\cdot\mathrm{poly}(h^{(i-1)}_{j})\cdot(1/\phi_{j}^{(i)}+2^{O(f_{j}^{(i-1)})}\cdot\omega_{j}^{(i-1)})
.
Summing over all
i
and
j
, the total running time is bounded by

\displaystyle~~~~\lambda_{\mathrm{insLM},t}\cdot\sum_{j}\widetilde{\omega}_{j}\cdot(\tau/\phi)\cdot\mathrm{poly}(h_{j})\cdot n^{O(\epsilon)}\cdot(\kappa_{\mathrm{insLM},\gamma}\cdot\widetilde{\omega}_{j}\cdot(\kappa_{\mathrm{CM},L}+\kappa_{{\rm init},L})/\phi+2^{O(\widetilde{f}_{j})}\cdot\widetilde{\omega}_{j})

\displaystyle\leq(\tau/\phi)\cdot n^{O(\epsilon)}\cdot\sum_{j}(\widetilde{\omega}_{j}^{2}\cdot\mathrm{poly}(h_{j})\cdot(1/\phi+2^{O(\widetilde{f}_{j})}))

∎

8
Dynamic Vertex Sparsifiers for Bounded Distances

In this section, we introduce our online-batch dynamic vertex sparsifier algorithm, where the sparsifier will preserve pairwise distance between a given terminal set. The main theorem of this section is
Theorem

8.2
.

8.1
Vertex Sparsifiers.

Let
G
be a graph with terminals
T\subseteq V(G)
, an
(\alpha_{\mathrm{low}},\alpha_{\mathrm{up}},h)
-sparsifier
H
of
T
on
G
is a weighted graph satisfying the following.

1.

T\subseteq V(H)
.

2.

For each
u,v\in T
, there is
\mathrm{dist}_{H}(u,v)\cdot\alpha_{\mathrm{low}}\geq\mathrm{dist}_{G}(u,v)
.

3.

For each
u,v\in T
s.t.
\mathrm{dist}_{G}(u,v)\leq h
, there is
\mathrm{dist}_{H}(u,v)\leq\alpha_{\mathrm{up}}\cdot\mathrm{dist}_{G}(u,v)
.

The
stretch/approximation
of the sparsifier
H
is
\alpha_{\mathrm{low}}\cdot\alpha_{\mathrm{up}}
. In particular, we say
H
is an
(\alpha_{\mathrm{low}},\alpha_{\mathrm{up}})
-sparsifer of
T
on
G
if property 3 holds for every pair of
u,v\in T
without restriction.

Before stating
Theorem

8.2
, we first explain the term of
fresh
vertices, which appears in
Item

1
of
Theorem

8.2
. In a dynamic algorithm, we say a vertex is fresh, if at the moment it is created, we assign it a
global
vertex identifier different from the identifiers of all vertices appear before. Equivalently, we can image that there is a large
global
pool of vertex identifiers, and we request an unpicked identifier when creating this vertex. Whether a newly created vertex is fresh or not is its inherent property, and we are not allowed to change the global identifier of a vertex once it has been created. We note that in
Theorem

8.2
, when we say a vertex
v
in the sparsifer
H
of
G
is fresh, besides
v\notin V(G)
, we also know that
v
has identifier different from any vertex created before in an algorithm that uses
Theorem

8.2
as a subroutine.

It is important to specify the global identifiers of vertices, because in some following sections (e.g.
Section

11
), we will work on several graphs simultaneously and define some new graph using vertices from different graphs. The new graph is well-defined only when global identifiers exist, because we need to know whether two vertices from two different graphs correspond to the same vertex in the new graph or not. This is also the reason why we cannot allow change the identifier of a vertex, because changing the identifier may cause vertex split operation to the new graph, which is expensive.

8.2
Dynamic vertex sparsifiers.

Let
G
be a dynamic graph with an incremental terminal set
T\subseteq V(G)
and parameters
h
and
\phi\leq 1/t
under
t
batches of updates
\pi^{(1)},\pi^{(2)},...\pi^{(t)}
of edge insertions/deletions, isolated vertex insertions/deletions and terminals insertions s.t. at each time
1\leq i\leq t
,

|\pi^{(i)}|\leq\phi\cdot|G^{(i)}|.

There is an algorithm that maintains an
(\lambda_{H,\mathrm{low}},\lambda_{H,\mathrm{up}},h)
-sparsifier
H
of
T
on
G
, where

\lambda_{H,\mathrm{low}}=\lambda_{\mathrm{rt},h}(t\cdot\lambda_{\mathrm{insLM},t})\cdot\lambda_{{\rm init},h}\cdot 2^{O(t\cdot\lambda_{\mathrm{insLM},t})}=2^{\mathrm{poly}(1/\epsilon)}\text{ and }\lambda_{H,\mathrm{up}}=O(1).

The algorithm further guarantee the following.

1.

H
is further a
(\lambda_{H,\mathrm{low}},\lambda_{H,\mathrm{up}},h)
-sparsifier of extended terminals
\bar{T}
s.t.
\bar{T}=V(H)\cap V(G)
and
T\subseteq\bar{T}
. Vertices in
V(H)\setminus\bar{T}
are fresh vertices.

2.

The size of
H
is bounded by
|H|\leq\kappa_{H,\mathrm{size}}\cdot(|T|+\phi\cdot|G|)

where

\kappa_{H,\mathrm{size}}=\kappa_{j}^{3}\cdot 2^{O(t\cdot\lambda_{\mathrm{insLM},t})}\cdot\lambda_{\mathrm{rt},{\rm prune}}(t\cdot\lambda_{\mathrm{insLM},t})\cdot\kappa_{\mathrm{NC},\omega}^{3}\cdot(\kappa_{\mathrm{CM},L}+\kappa_{{\rm init},L})\cdot(\kappa_{\mathrm{CM},\gamma}+\kappa_{{\rm init},\gamma})=n^{O(\epsilon^{4})},

and
\kappa_{j}=O(\log n)
is a global upper bound of
\lceil 100\cdot\log h\rceil
.

3.

The number of vertices in
H
is bounded by
|V(H)|\leq n^{O(\epsilon^{4})}\cdot|V(G)|
.

4.

The recourse from
H^{(i-1)}
to
H^{(i)}
is
\mathrm{Rcs}(H^{(i-1)}\to H^{(i)})\leq\kappa_{H,\mathrm{rcs}}\cdot|\pi^{(i)}|
, where

\kappa_{H,\mathrm{rcs}}=\kappa_{j}^{3}\cdot 2^{O(t\cdot\lambda_{\mathrm{insLM},t})}\cdot\lambda_{\mathrm{rt},{\rm prune}}(t\cdot\lambda_{\mathrm{insLM},t})\cdot\kappa_{\mathrm{NC},\omega}^{4}\cdot(\kappa_{\mathrm{CM},L}+\kappa_{{\rm init},L})\cdot(\kappa_{\mathrm{CM},\gamma}+\kappa_{{\rm init},\gamma})\cdot\kappa_{\mathrm{insLM},\gamma}=n^{O(\epsilon^{4})}.

5.

The initialization time is
(|G^{(0)}|+|T^{(0)}|/\phi)\cdot\mathrm{poly}(h)\cdot n^{O(\epsilon)}/\phi
and the time to handle the batch
\pi^{(i)}
is
|\pi^{(i)}|\cdot\mathrm{poly}(h)\cdot n^{O(\epsilon)}/\phi^{2}
.

6.

At any time, given a path
P_{H}
on
H
connecting some vertices
u,v\in\bar{T}
, the algorithm can compute a
u
-
v
path
P_{G}
on
G
with
\ell_{G}(P_{G})\leq\lambda_{H,\mathrm{low}}\cdot\ell_{H}(P_{H})
and
|P_{G}|\geq|P_{H}|/2
. The path-unfolding time is
|P_{G}|\cdot 2^{O(1/\epsilon^{4})}
.

We emphasize that an important advantage of our dynamic sparsifier algorithm is that it has
low recourse
, independent of
1/\phi
. This is crucial for our dynamic length-constrained expander hierarchy algorithm in
Section

9
, where we recursively apply
Theorem

8.2
to build some kind of hierarchy of sparsifiers with roughly
\log n/\log(1/\phi)
levels but still require small final recourse.

However, one may notice that
Theorem

8.2
has some drawbacks, and we now discuss how to fix them. Nonetheless,
Theorem

8.2
itself is sufficient for its application in
Section

9
.

•

First, each batched update
\pi^{(i)}
is required to have size no more than
\phi\cdot|E(G^{(i)})|
. This can be easily fixed by a standard rebuilding technique. Namely, we rebuild the sparsifier if the batched update is too large. Indeed, we use this technique when we apply
Theorem

8.2
in
Section

9
.

•

Second, the size of the sparsifier has an additive term
\phi\cdot|E(G)|
, which means when the number of terminals is small, the sparsifier only sparsifies
G
by a factor
\phi
. We can fix this by recursively applying
Theorem

8.2
until the sparsifier has size roughly
|T|
. See
Section

13
for a formal proof.

•

Third, the update time polynomially depends on
h
, which means this algorithm will only be efficient when the sparsifier preserves bounded distance. We will see how to fix this in
Section

13
by exploiting the stacking technique in
Section

12.1
.

In
Section

8.1
, we first show a static construction of the sparsifier from a collection of (specialized) certified-EDs with different length parameters, and then make the sparsifier dynamic by assuming these certified-EDs are dynamic. In
Section

8.2
, we complete the proof of
Theorem

8.2
by showing a dynamic algorithm for these certified-EDs. In order to achieve low recourse, we will exploit
\rho
-dense certified-EDs in
Section

7
.

8.1
Construction Based on Landmarks

Lemma

8.3
shows a static construction of the sparsifier, given a collection of
specialized
certified-EDs at different levels
j
with length parameters
2,4,..,2^{j},...,h
. Precisely, the static construction will only access the moving cuts, landmark sets and pairwise covers (the routers and embedding are only for controlling the diameter of pairwise covers on
G
). The certified-EDs are specialized in the sense that they should be with respect to an
extended
terminal set
\bar{T}
including the original terminal set
T
and all the landmarks.

In fact, the sparsifier we construct is also with respect to this extended terminal set
\bar{T}
. The construction is given in the first paragraph in the proof of
Lemma

8.3
. The intuition that the sparsifier will have low stretch is as follows. Consider a pair of extended terminals
u
and
v
. We look at the level
j
with length parameter
2^{j}
slightly larger than
\mathrm{dist}_{G}(u,v)
. If
\mathrm{dist}_{G-C_{j}}(u,v)
only increases a little bit compared to
\mathrm{dist}_{G}(u,v)
, then the pairwise cover at level
j
(with cover radius
2^{j}
on
G-C_{j}
) will have a cluster covering
u
and
v
and the corresponding star graph will preserve the
u
-
v
distance. Otherwise,
\mathrm{dist}_{G-C_{j}}(u,v)
increases quite a lot, which means there is a
C_{j}
-vertex
w^{\prime}
on the middle of the
u
-
v
shortest path on
G
. Then we look at the landmark
w\in L_{j}\subseteq\bar{T}
of
w^{\prime}
(which is quite close to
w^{\prime}
), and consider pairs
(u,w)
and
(w,v)
recursively.

8.3
.

Let
G
be a graph with terminal set
T
and a parameter
h
. For each integer
1\leq j\leq\bar{j}=\lceil\log(100\cdot h)\rceil
, let
h_{j}=2^{j}
and suppose we are given an integral moving cut
C_{j}
, a landmark set
L_{j}
and a pairwise cover
{\cal N}_{j}
satisfying the following.

•

L_{j}
is a landmark set of
C_{j}
on
G
with distortion
\sigma_{j}=h_{j}/\log^{2}n
.

•

{\cal N}_{j}
is a pairwise cover of
\bar{T}=T\cup(\bigcup_{j^{\prime}}L_{j^{\prime}})
on
G-C_{j}
with cover radius
h_{{\rm cov},j}=h_{j}
and width
\omega_{j}
. Furthermore, the diameter of
{\cal N}_{j}

on
G
is
h_{\mathrm{RealDiam},j}\leq h_{j}\cdot\alpha_{\mathrm{low}}
for some fixed parameter
\alpha_{\mathrm{low}}\geq 1
.

There is an algorithm that computes an
(\alpha_{\mathrm{low}},\alpha_{\mathrm{up}},h)
-sparsifier
H
of
\bar{T}
on
G
with
\alpha_{\mathrm{up}}=O(1)
and
|H|=O(\sum_{j}(|C_{j}|/h_{j}+\omega_{j}\cdot|\bar{T}|))
. The running time is
O(\sum_{j}(|C_{j}|+\omega_{j}\cdot|\bar{T}|))

Proof.

First, we define a set
E_{\mathrm{heavy}}
of
heavy edges
, which collect all edges
e\in E(G)
s.t.
C_{j}(e)\geq h_{j}/10
for some
j
. Note that each edge
e=(u,v)\in E_{\mathrm{heavy}}
must have endpoints
u,v\in\bar{T}
, because
C_{j}(e)\geq h_{j}/10\geq\sigma_{j}
implies
u,v\in L_{j}\subseteq\bar{T}
. Second, for each pairwise cover
{\cal N}_{j}
and each cluster
S\in{\cal N}_{j}
, let
H^{\star}_{S}
be a star graph with
V(H^{\star}_{S})=S\cup\{v_{S}\}
, where
v_{S}
is the
artificial center
, and
E(H^{\star}_{S})=\{(v_{S},v)\mid v\in\mathrm{supp}(S)\}
in which each edge has length
h_{j}
. Exceptionally, when
|S|=1
, its star graph
H^{\star}_{S}
degenerate to an isolated vertex, which is the unique vertex
v\in S
. We call
H^{\star}=\bigcup_{1\leq j\leq\bar{j},S\in{\cal N}_{j}}H^{\star}_{S}
the
star union
of the collection
\{{\cal N}_{j}\mid 1\leq j\leq\bar{j}\}
. At last, the sparsifier
H
is simply the union of the star union and heavy edges, i.e.
H=H^{\star}\cup E_{\mathrm{heavy}}
.

The quality of
H
is shown by
Lemma

8.4
. The number of vertices in
H
is at most
O(\sum_{j}\mathrm{size}({\cal N}_{j}))
. The number of edges in
H
is at most
|E(H)|\leq\sum_{j}(O(|C_{j}|/h_{j})+\mathrm{size}({\cal N}_{j}))\leq O(\sum_{j}(|C_{j}|/h_{j}+\omega_{j}\cdot|\bar{T}|))
. The bound on running time is straightforward.

8.4
.

H
is a
(\alpha_{\mathrm{low}},\alpha_{\mathrm{up}},h)
-sparsifer of
\bar{T}
on
G
with
\alpha_{\mathrm{up}}=O(1)
.

Proof.

We show the three properties of sparsifers in
Definition

8.1
one by one.

Property
1
.

From the definition of pairwise cover (i.e.
Definition

3.2
), for each
{\cal N}_{j}
, we have
\bigcup_{S\in{\cal N}_{j}}S=\bar{T}
. By the construction of
H
, we have
\bar{T}\subseteq V(H^{\star})=V(H)
.

Property
2
.

Let
u,v
be an arbitrary pair of vertices in
\bar{T}
, and let
P_{H}
be the shortest
u
-
v
path in
H
. Consider an arbitrary pair of consecutive
\bar{T}
-vertices
x,y
on
P_{H}
. That is, the subpath of
P_{H}
from
x
to
y
, denoted by
P_{H}(x,y)
, is internally disjoint with
\bar{T}
. By the construction of
H
, there are two cases.

In the first case,
P_{H}(x,y)
is made up with a single edge
e=(x,y)\in E_{\mathrm{heavy}}
. This edge has the same length in both
G
and
H
, so
\mathrm{dist}_{G}(x,y)\leq\ell_{H}(P_{H}(x,y))
. In the second case,
P_{H}(x,y)
is made up with two edges and a internal vertex
w
, where
w
is the artificial center of some cluster
S\in{\cal N}_{j}
for some
j
. We have
x,y\in S
and
