\mathrm{dist}_{G}(x,y)\leq\alpha_{\mathrm{low}}\cdot h_{j}
. Moreover,
\ell_{H}(P_{H}(x,y))=2h_{j}
because these two edges have length
h_{j}
. Therefore,
\mathrm{dist}_{G}(x,y)\leq\alpha_{\mathrm{low}}\cdot\ell_{H}(P_{H}(x,y))
.

Finally, by taking the concatenation of these subpaths
P_{H}(x,y)
, we have

\mathrm{dist}_{G}(u,v)\leq\sum_{(x,y)}\mathrm{dist}_{G}(x,y)\leq\sum_{(x,y)}\alpha_{\mathrm{low}}\cdot\ell_{H}(P_{H}(x,y))=\alpha_{\mathrm{low}}\cdot\mathrm{dist}_{H}(u,v).

Property
3
.

Consider a pair of vertices
u,v\in\bar{T}
with
\mathrm{dist}_{G}(u,v)\leq h
. We now show that
\mathrm{dist}_{H}(u,v)\leq O(1)\cdot\mathrm{dist}_{G}(u,v)
. Let
P^{\star}
be the shortest
u
-
v
path on
G
.

Our strategy is to construct a set
{\cal P}
of paths
P_{1},P_{2},...,P_{\bar{x}}
called
roads
s.t. each road
P_{x}\in{\cal P}
has endpoints
u_{x},v_{x}\in\bar{T}
. These roads should further have the following properties. (1) First,
u_{1}=u
,
v_{\bar{x}}=v
and
v_{x}=u_{x+1}
for each
1\leq x\leq\bar{x}-1
. That is, the roads in
{\cal P}
are laid end to end, where the first road
P_{1}
starts from
u
and the last road
P_{\bar{x}}
ends at
v
. (2) Second,
\sum_{P_{x}\in{\cal P}}\ell_{G}(P_{x})\leq O(1)\cdot\mathrm{dist}_{G}(u,v)
. Namely, the total
G
-length of roads in
{\cal P}
is at most a constant multiple of the real
\mathrm{dist}_{G}(u,v)
. (3) Each
(u_{x},v_{x})
has
\mathrm{dist}_{H}(u_{x},v_{x})\leq O(1)\cdot\ell_{G}(P_{x})
.

These three properties are enough to certify that
\mathrm{dist}_{H}(u,v)\leq O(1)\cdot\mathrm{dist}_{G}(u,v)
, because they imply

\displaystyle\mathrm{dist}_{H}(u,v)\leq\sum_{1\leq x\leq\bar{x}}\mathrm{dist}_{H}(u_{x},v_{x})\leq\sum_{P_{x}\in{\cal P}}O(1)\cdot\ell_{G}(P_{x})\leq O(1)\cdot\mathrm{dist}_{G}(u,v).

Construction of
{\cal P}
.
We construct
{\cal P}
in an algorithmic way. We start with an initial set
{\cal P}_{1}
which only contains a single road
P_{1,1}=P^{\star}
. The
y
-th phase will process a road set
{\cal P}_{y}
. Roughly speaking, each road
P_{y,x}\in{\cal P}_{y}
may be split
19
19

19

The split operation
may not
simply choose a vertex on
P_{y,x}
as the breakpoint and break
P_{y,x}
into two subpaths.
into two new roads
P_{y+1,x^{\prime}},P_{y+1,x^{\prime\prime}}\in{\cal P}_{y+1}
, or added into
{\cal P}_{y+1}
with no change (i.e. it becomes
P_{y+1,x^{\prime}}=P_{y,x}\in{\cal P}_{y+1}
). Intuitively, all the roads generated by this procedure will form a tree structure
{\cal T}
. Naturally, we can define the
ancestor-descendant
relationships between roads. In particular, we call the road
P_{y,x}
the
parent
of
P_{y+1,x^{\prime}}
and
P_{y+1,x^{\prime\prime}}
, and the road
P_{1,1}=P^{\star}
is the
root road
of
{\cal T}
.

Now we describe the algorithm in details. In the
y
-th iteration, we do the following for each road
P_{y,x}\in{\cal P}_{y}
. Let
P_{y-1,\hat{x}}
be its parent if
y\geq 2
. Let
u_{y,x},v_{y,x}\in\bar{T}
be the endpoints of
P_{y,x}
. Let
j^{\star}(P_{y,x})
be the unique level s.t.
10\cdot\ell_{G}(P_{y,x})<h_{j^{\star}(P_{y,x})}\leq 20\cdot\ell_{G}(P_{y,x})
, and let
j(P_{y,x})=\min\{j^{\star}(P_{y,x}),j(P_{y-1,\hat{x}})\}
(if
P_{y,x}
has no parent, then
j(P_{y,x})=j^{\star}(P_{y,x})
). We will study by cases. If every road
P_{y,x}\in{\cal P}_{y}
falls in case 1, we stop the algorithm with
{\cal P}={\cal P}_{y}
and let
\bar{y}=y
denote the index of the last iteration. For simplicity, let
P=P_{y,x}
,
j=j(P_{y,x})
,
u=u_{y,x}
and
v=v_{y,x}
.

The Invariant (
Lemma

8.9
).
Before going into the case study, we first assume this important invariant

5\cdot\ell_{G}(P)\leq h_{j}\leq 20\cdot\ell_{G}(P).

It is proven in
Lemma

8.9
after finishing the case study because the proof depends on some notations and claims below. However, we emphasize that there is no circular argument (see the discussion in
Remark

8.10
).

Intuitively, this invariant says, although we enforce that the
j
-value of
P
cannot be larger than the
j
-value of its parent
\hat{P}
,
j(P)
will only slightly smaller than the best
j^{\star}(P)
and it is still fine to work with
j(P)
in the case study.

Case 1.
The first case is that
P
has only one edge or
\ell_{G-C_{j}}(P)\leq h_{j}
. We add
P^{\prime}=P
into
{\cal P}_{y+1}
with
P
as its parent.

8.5
.

In case 1, we have
\mathrm{dist}_{H}(u,v)\leq 40\cdot\ell_{G}(P)
.

Proof.

Suppose
P
has only one edge
e
. If
C_{j}(e)<h_{j}/10
, then
\mathrm{dist}_{G-C_{j}}(u,v)\leq\ell_{G-C_{j}}(e)\leq\ell_{G}(P)+h_{j}/10\leq h_{j}/5+h_{j}/10\leq h_{j}
, which means there is a cluster
S\in{\cal N}_{j}
containing both
u
and
v
. Hence
\mathrm{dist}_{H}(u,v)\leq\mathrm{dist}_{H^{\star}_{S}}(u,v)\leq 2h_{j}\leq 40\cdot\ell_{G}(P)
. If
C_{j}(e)\geq h_{j}/10
, then
e\in E_{\mathrm{heavy}}
and
\mathrm{dist}_{H}(u,v)\leq\ell_{G}(e)=\ell_{G}(P)
.

Suppose
\ell_{G-C_{j}}(P)\leq h_{j}
. Then similarly,
(u,v)
is covered by a cluster
S\in{\cal N}_{j}
, and
\mathrm{dist}_{H}(u,v)\leq 40\cdot\mathrm{dist}_{G}(u,v)
.
∎

Case 2.
If
P
has at least two edges and there is an edge
e\in P
s.t.
\ell_{G-C_{j}}(e)>\sigma_{j}
, we let
w
be an endpoint of
e
that is not the same with
u
or
v
(
w
must exist since
P
has at least two edges). Let
P^{\prime}
and
P^{\prime\prime}
be subpaths of
P
from
u
to
w
and from
w
to
v
respectively, and then add
P^{\prime}
and
P^{\prime\prime}
into
{\cal P}_{y+1}
with
P
as their parent.

8.6
.

In case 2, we have (i)
\ell_{G-C_{j}}(P^{\prime}),\ell_{G-C_{j}}(P^{\prime\prime})\leq\ell_{G-C_{j}}(P)
, (ii)
\ell_{G}(P^{\prime}),\ell_{G}(P^{\prime\prime})\leq\ell_{G}(P)
, and (iii)
\ell_{G}(P^{\prime})+\ell_{G}(P^{\prime\prime})=\ell_{G}(P)
. Furthermore, the endpoints of
P^{\prime}
and
P^{\prime\prime}
are inside
\bar{T}
.

Proof.

The claims on lengths are trivial. To see that endpoints of
P^{\prime}
and
P^{\prime\prime}
are inside
\bar{T}
, we just need to show
w\in\bar{T}
because
u
and
v
are already known to be in
\bar{T}
. Recall that
w
is an endpoint of some
e\in P
with
\ell_{G-C_{j}}(e)>\sigma_{j}
. Because
L_{j}
is a landmark set of
C_{j}
on
G
with distortion
\sigma_{j}
, we have
w\in L_{j}\subseteq\bar{T}
.
∎

Case 3.
In this case,
P
has at least two edges and all edges
e\in P
have
\ell_{G-C_{j}}(e)\leq\sigma_{j}=h_{j}/\log^{2}n
. Because
\ell_{G}(P)\leq h_{j}/5
by the invariant, we have
C_{j}(P)\geq 4h_{j}/5
. Therefore, we can find an edge
e\in P\cap\mathrm{supp}(C_{j})
with an endpoint
w^{\prime}
s.t.
\ell_{G-C_{j}}(P(u,w^{\prime})),\ell_{G-C_{j}}(P(w^{\prime},v))\leq 2\ell_{G-C_{j}}(P)/3
, where
P(u,w^{\prime})
and
P(w^{\prime},v)
are subpaths of
P
from
u
to
w^{\prime}
and from
w^{\prime}
to
v
respectively. Let
w\in L_{j}\subseteq\bar{T}
be the landmark of
w^{\prime}
s.t.
\mathrm{dist}_{G-C_{j}}(w,w^{\prime})\leq\sigma_{j}=h_{j}/\log^{2}n
.

Now we construct two roads
P^{\prime}
and
P^{\prime\prime}
from
u
to
w
and from
w
to
v
as follows. Let
P_{w}
be the shortest
w^{\prime}
-
w
path on
G-C_{j}
with
\ell_{G-C_{j}}(P_{w})=\mathrm{dist}_{G-C_{j}}(w^{\prime},w)\leq\sigma_{j}
. Then
P^{\prime}
is the concatenation of
P(u,w^{\prime})
and
P_{w}
, and
P^{\prime\prime}
is the concatenation of
P_{w}
and
P(w^{\prime},v)
(and we enforce
P^{\prime}
and
P^{\prime\prime}
to be simple paths by removing redundant parts).

8.7
.

In case 3, we have (i)
\ell_{G-C_{j}}(P^{\prime}),\ell_{G-C_{j}}(P^{\prime\prime})\leq(3/4)\cdot\ell_{G-C_{j}}(P)
, (ii)
\ell_{G}(P^{\prime}),\ell_{G}(P^{\prime\prime})\leq(1+O(1/\log^{2}n))\ell_{G}(P)
, and (iii)
\ell_{G}(P^{\prime})+\ell_{G}(P^{\prime\prime})\leq(1+O(1/\log^{2}n))\ell_{G}(P)
.
Furthermore, endpoints of
P^{\prime}
and
P^{\prime\prime}
are inside
\bar{T}
.

Proof.

For (i), we have

\displaystyle\ell_{G-C_{j}}(P^{\prime})

\displaystyle\leq\ell_{G-C_{j}}(P(u,w^{\prime}))+\ell_{G-C_{j}}(P_{w})

\displaystyle\leq(2/3)\cdot\ell_{G-C_{j}}(P)+20\cdot\ell_{G}(P)/\log^{2}n

\displaystyle\leq(3/4)\cdot\ell_{G-C_{j}}(P),

where the second inequality is because
\ell_{G-C_{j}}(P_{w})\leq\sigma_{j}\leq h_{j}/\log^{2}n\leq 20\cdot\ell_{G}(P)/\log^{2}n
(recall the invariant
h_{j}\leq 20\cdot\ell_{G}(P)
). Similarly, we have
\ell_{G-C_{j}}(P^{\prime\prime})\leq(3/4)\cdot\ell_{G-C_{j}}(P)
.

For (ii) and (iii), we have
\ell_{G}(P^{\prime})+\ell_{G}(P^{\prime\prime})\leq\ell_{G}(P)+2\ell_{G}(P_{w})\leq\ell_{G}(P)+2\ell_{G-C_{j}}(P_{w})\leq\ell_{G}(P)+2\sigma_{j}\leq\ell_{G}(P)+h_{j}/\log^{2}n\leq(1+40/\log^{2}n)\ell_{G}(P)
.
∎

8.8
.

Let
P_{y}
denote
P
. For an arbitrary ancestor
P_{\hat{y}}\in{\cal P}_{\hat{y}}
of
P_{y}
s.t.
j(P_{\hat{y}})=j
, the tree-path from
P_{\hat{y}}
to
P_{y}
on
{\cal T}
has at most
O(\log n)
case-3 roads.

Proof.

Let
{\cal T}(P_{\hat{y}},P_{y})
denote the tree-path from
P_{\hat{y}}
to
P_{y}
. For each

\hat{y}\leq y^{\prime}\leq y
, let
P_{y^{\prime}}\in{\cal P}_{y^{\prime}}
denote the ancestor of
P_{y}
on level
y^{\prime}
. We have
j(P_{y^{\prime}})=j
for all
\hat{y}\leq y^{\prime}\leq y
because
j(P_{\hat{y}})=j(P_{y})
. Note that the case-1 roads on
{\cal T}(P_{\hat{y}},P_{y})
are located consecutively as a suffix. Hence we only need to consider the prefix of
{\cal T}(P_{\hat{y}},P_{y})
including only case-2 and case-3 roads.

For each road
P_{y^{\prime}}
(with
y^{\prime}\leq y-1
) on this prefix. If
P_{y^{\prime}}
is a case-2 road, we have
\ell_{G-C_{j}}(P_{y^{\prime}+1})\leq\ell_{G-C_{j}}(P_{y^{\prime}})
by
8.6
, otherwise
P_{y^{\prime}}
is a case-3 road and
\ell_{G-C_{j}}(P_{y^{\prime}+1})\leq(3/4)\cdot\ell_{G-C_{j}}(P_{y^{\prime}})
. Suppose there are
i
many case-3 roads. Then, the
i
-th case-3 road on
{\cal T}(P_{\hat{y}},P_{y})
, denoted by
\widetilde{P}_{i}
has
\ell_{G-C_{j}}(\widetilde{P}_{i})\leq(3/4)^{(i-1)}\cdot\ell_{G-C_{j}}(P_{\hat{y}})
. On the other hand,
\ell_{G-C_{j}}(\widetilde{P}_{i})\geq h_{j}\geq 1
because it is a case-3 road. Therefore, there must be
i\leq O(\log n)
because
\ell_{G-C_{j}}(P_{y_{1}})
is polynomially bounded.

∎

8.9
.

5\cdot\ell_{G}(P)\leq h_{j}\leq 40\cdot\ell_{G}(P)
.

Proof.

Recall that
j^{\star}(P)
is defined to be such that
10\cdot\ell_{G}(P)<h_{j^{\star}(P)}\leq 20\cdot\ell_{G}(P)
and
j\leq j^{\star}(P)
, so trivially
h_{j}\leq h_{j^{\star}(P)}\leq 20\cdot\ell_{G}(P)
.

It remains to show
5\cdot\ell_{G}(P)\leq h_{j}
. Let
\widetilde{P}
be the farthest ancestor of
P
s.t.
j(\widetilde{P})=j
. Then the parent of
\widetilde{P}
, denoted by
\widehat{\widetilde{P}}
, has
j(\widehat{\widetilde{P}})>j(\widetilde{P})
, so combining
j(\widetilde{P})=\min\{j(\widehat{\widetilde{P}}),j^{\star}(\widetilde{P})\}
, we have
j^{\star}(\widetilde{P})=j(\widetilde{P})=j
. From the definition of
j^{\star}(\widetilde{P})
, we have
10\cdot\ell_{G}(\widetilde{P})<h_{j}
. We will see in a moment that
\ell_{G}(P)\leq 2\cdot\ell_{G}(\widetilde{P})
, which immediately implies
5\cdot\ell_{G}(P)<h_{j}
.

To show
\ell_{G}(P)\leq 2\cdot\ell_{G}(\widetilde{P})
, consider the tree-path
{\cal T}(\widetilde{P},P)
from
\widetilde{P}
to
P
. By
Lemma

8.8
this tree-path has at most
O(\log n)
case-3 roads. We walk from
\widetilde{P}
to
P
on this tree-path. By
8.6
, passing a case-1 or case-2 road will not increase the road length on
G
. By
8.7
, passing a case-3 road will increase the road length on
G
by a factor
(1+O(\log^{2}n))
multiplicatively. Therefore, We can conclude that
\ell_{G}(P)\leq(1+O(1/\log^{2}n))^{O(\log n)}\cdot\ell_{G}(\widetilde{P})\leq 2\cdot\ell_{G}(\widetilde{P})
.
∎

8.10
.

We emphasize that we
did not
make a circular argument by clarifying the dependency between (a)
Lemmas

8.8
and
8.9
, and (b)
8.6
and
8.7
. First,
8.6
and
8.7
for the current road
P
depend on
Lemma

8.9
for
P
, because the algorithmic step processing
P
needs
Lemma

8.9
for
P
. Second,
Lemma

8.8
and
Lemma

8.9
for the current
P
depend on
8.6
and
8.7
for all
ancestor

\widetilde{P}
of
P
. Therefore, because
{\cal T}
is a tree and
Lemma

8.9
holds for the root road trivially, all these claims and lemmas can be established inductively.

Properties of
{\cal P}
.
For property (1), it is straightforward to see that the roads in
{\cal P}
are laid end to end from
u
to
v
. For property (3), because all roads in
{\cal P}
are case-1 (by the terminate condition), each
P_{x}\in{\cal P}
has
\mathrm{dist}_{H}(u_{x},v_{x})\leq 40\cdot\ell_{G}(P_{x})
by
8.5
.

We now prove property (2),
\sum_{P_{x}\in{\cal P}}\ell_{G}(P_{x})\leq O(1)\cdot\mathrm{dist}_{G}(u,v)
, by induction. We call each road in
{\cal P}={\cal P}_{\bar{y}}
a
leaf road
. For each road
P\in{\cal T}
, let
d(P)
denote the maximum number of case-3 roads on the tree-path from
P
to an arbitrary descendant leaf road of
P
, and let
g(P)=\sum_{\text{$P$'s descendant leaf road $P^{\prime}$}}\ell_{G}(P^{\prime})
be the sum of
G
-length over all descendant leaf road
P^{\prime}
of
P
. Let the induction hypothesis be

g(P)\leq(1+O(1/\log^{2}n))^{d(P)}\cdot\ell_{G}(P),

which trivially holds for all leaf road
P
. Now consider a non-leaf road
P
and assume the induction hypothesis holds for
P
’s children. If
P
is a case-1 road, the hypothesis also holds for
P
trivially. If
P
is a case-2 road with children
P^{\prime}
and
P^{\prime\prime}
, we have
d(P)=\max\{d(P^{\prime}),d(P^{\prime\prime})\}
and
g(P)=g(P^{\prime})+g(P^{\prime\prime})
. Then

\displaystyle g(P)

\displaystyle=g(P^{\prime})+g(P^{\prime\prime})

\displaystyle\leq(1+O(1/\log^{2}n))^{d(P^{\prime})}\cdot\ell_{G}(P^{\prime})+(1+O(1/\log^{2}n))^{d(P^{\prime\prime})}\cdot\ell_{G}(P^{\prime\prime})

\displaystyle\leq(1+O(1/\log^{2}n))^{d(P)}\cdot(\ell_{G}(P^{\prime})+\ell_{G}(P^{\prime\prime}))

\displaystyle=(1+O(1/\log^{2}n))^{d(P)}\cdot\ell_{G}(P),

where the last equation is by
8.6
. Similarly, if
P
is a case-3 road with children
P^{\prime}
and
P^{\prime\prime}
, we have
d(P)=\max\{d(P^{\prime}),d(P^{\prime\prime})\}+1
, and then

\displaystyle g(P)

\displaystyle=g(P^{\prime})+g(P^{\prime\prime})

\displaystyle\leq(1+O(1/\log^{2}n))^{\max\{d(P^{\prime}),d(P^{\prime\prime})\}}\cdot(\ell_{G}(P^{\prime})+\ell_{G}(P^{\prime\prime}))

\displaystyle\leq(1+O(1/\log^{2}n))^{\max\{d(P^{\prime}),d(P^{\prime\prime})\}}\cdot(1+O(1/\log^{2}n))\cdot\ell_{G}(P)

\displaystyle=(1+O(1/\log^{2}n))^{d(P)}\cdot\ell_{G}(P),

where the second inequality is by
8.7
.

Now consider the root road
P^{\star}
, the induction shows

g(P^{\star})=(1+O(1/\log^{2}n))^{d(P^{\star})}\cdot\ell_{G}(P^{\star}).

We will see in a moment that
d(P^{\star})=O(\log^{2}n)
, which implies
\sum_{P_{x}\in{\cal P}}\ell_{G}(P_{x})=g(P^{\star})\leq O(1)\cdot\ell_{G}(P^{\star})=O(1)\cdot\mathrm{dist}_{G}(u,v)
.

To see
d(P^{\star})=O(\log^{2}n)
, consider an arbitrary path on
{\cal T}
from the root road to a leaf road
P_{\rm leaf}
, denoted by
{\cal T}(P^{\star},P_{\rm leaf})
. Because the
j
-value is monotonically decreasing, the roads on
{\cal T}(P^{\star},P_{\rm leaf})
with the same
j
-value will located consecutively. Therefore, for each
1\leq j\leq\bar{j}
, the number of case-3 roads
P\in{\cal T}(P^{\star},P_{\rm leaf})
with
j(P)=P
is at most
O(\log n)
by
Lemma

8.8
. This means the total number of case-3 roads on
{\cal T}(P^{\star},P_{\rm leaf})
is at most
O(\bar{j}\cdot\log n)=O(\log^{2}n)
because
h
is polynomially bounded.

∎

∎

Lemma

8.11
is a dynamic version of
Theorem

8.2
, which is straightforward from the static construction of sparsifiers.

8.11
.

Let
G
be a dynamic graph with an incremental terminal set
T
under
t
batches of updates
\pi^{(1)},...,\pi^{(t)}
of edge insertions/deletions, isolated vertex insertions/deletions and terminals insertions. Given a parameter
h
, for each level
1\leq j\leq\bar{j}=\lceil\log(100\cdot h)\rceil
, let
h_{j}=2^{j}
and suppose we are given a sequence of
(C_{j}^{(i)},L_{j}^{(i)},{\cal N}_{j}^{(i)})
over time period
0\leq i\leq t
satisfying the following.

•

The integral moving cut
C_{j}^{(i)}=(C_{j}^{(i-1)})_{\mid E(G^{(i)})}+C_{{\rm new},j}^{(i)}
.

•

L^{(i)}_{j}
is a landmark set of
C^{(i)}_{j}
on
G^{(i)}
with distortion
\sigma_{j}=h_{j}/\log^{2}n
;

•

{\cal N}_{j}^{(i)}
is a pairwise cover of
\bar{T}^{(i)}=T^{(i)}\cup(\bigcup_{j^{\prime}}L_{j^{\prime}}^{(i)})
on
G^{(i)}-C^{(i)}_{j}
with cover radius
h_{{\rm cov},j}\geq h_{j}
, width
\omega_{j}^{(i)}
. Furthermore,

{\cal N}_{j}^{(i)}
has diameter
h_{\mathrm{RealDiam},j}\leq h_{j}\cdot\alpha_{\mathrm{low}}
on
G^{(i)}
for some fixed parameter
\alpha_{\mathrm{low}}
.

There is an algorithm that initializes and maintains an
(\alpha_{\mathrm{low}},\alpha_{\mathrm{up}},h)
-sparsifier
H
of
\bar{T}
on
G
with
\alpha_{\mathrm{up}}=O(1)
,
|V(H^{(i)})|=O(\sum_{j}\omega^{(i)}_{j}\cdot|\bar{T}^{(i)}|)
and size

\displaystyle|H^{(i)}|

\displaystyle=O(\sum_{j}\omega_{j}^{(i)}\cdot|\bar{T}^{(i)}|+t\cdot\sum_{j}|C_{j}^{(i)}|/h_{j}).

Furthermore, vertices in
V(H)\setminus\bar{T}
are fresh.

The recourse from
H^{(i-1)}
to
H^{(i)}
is

O(|\pi^{(i)}|+\sum_{j}t\cdot|C^{(i)}_{{\rm new},j}|/h_{j}+\mathrm{Rcs}({\cal N}^{(i-1)}_{j}\to{\cal N}^{(i)}_{j})).

The initialization time is
\widetilde{O}(\sum_{j}\omega^{(0)}_{j}\cdot|\bar{T}^{(0)}|+|C_{j}^{(0)}|)
and for each batched update
\pi^{(i)}
, the update time is
\widetilde{O}(\sum_{j}|C_{{\rm new},j}^{(i)}|+\mathrm{Rcs}({\cal N}_{j}^{(i-1)}\to{\cal N}_{j}^{(i)}))
.

Proof.

Providing the static construction of sparsifiers in the proof of
Lemma

8.3
, we just need it dynamic. Concretely, we will maintain the invariant
H^{(i)}=H^{\star,(i)}\cup E^{(i)}_{\mathrm{heavy}}
over all time
0\leq i\leq t
. Here
H^{\star,(i)}
is exactly the same as the star union in the proof of
Lemma

8.3
given
\{(C^{(i)}_{j},L^{(i)}_{j},{\cal N}^{(i)}_{j})\}
, but
E^{(i)}_{\mathrm{heavy}}
is a super set of the heavy edge set from
Lemma

8.3
. We will generate the batched update
\pi^{(i)}_{H}
to
H^{(i)}
at time
i
as follows.

Generating
\pi_{H}^{(i)}
.

For the heavy edge set, initially we let
E^{(0)}_{\mathrm{heavy}}
collect all edges
e\in E(G^{(0)})
s.t.
C_{j}^{(0)}(e)\geq h_{j}/(20\cdot t)
for some
j
. Afterwards, at each time
i\geq 1
, let
E_{G,\mathrm{delete}}^{(i)}
be the edges removed from
G^{(i-1)}
to
G^{(i)}
, and let
E_{\mathrm{heavy},{\rm new}}^{(i)}
collect all edges
e\in G^{(i)}
s.t.
C_{{\rm new},j}^{(i)}(e)\geq h_{j}/(20\cdot t)
. Then we update
E^{(i-1)}_{\mathrm{heavy}}
to
E^{(i)}_{\mathrm{heavy}}=E^{(i-1)}_{\mathrm{heavy}}\cup E^{(i)}_{\mathrm{heavy},{\rm new}}\setminus E_{G,\mathrm{delete}}^{(i)}
, so the batched edge insertions
E^{(i)}_{\mathrm{heavy},{\rm new}}
and batched edge deletions
E^{(i)}_{G,\mathrm{delete}}
will also apply to
H^{(i-1)}
, and we add them into
\pi_{H}^{(i)}
.

For the star union, we keep
H^{\star,(i)}=\bigcup_{1\leq j\leq\bar{j},S\in{\cal N}_{j}^{(i)}}H^{\star}_{S}
to be the star union of
\{{\cal N}_{j}^{(i)}\mid 1\leq j\leq\bar{j}\}
at all times. Note that
H^{\star,(i)}
can be updated from
H^{\star,(i-1)}
as follows. Consider the batched update sequence from
{\cal N}_{j}^{(i-1)}
to
{\cal N}_{j}^{(i)}
for all
j
. We add the following unit updates to
\pi_{H}^{(i)}
.

•

Consider a vertex insertion which adds
v
to a cluster
S
. We first apply an isolated vertex insertion
v
if
v\in V(G^{(i)})\setminus V(G^{(i-1)})
is a newly isolated vertex. If
|S|\geq 2
before insertion, we add an edge
(v,v_{S})
with length
h_{j}
to
H^{\star,(i-1)}
. If
|S|=1
(say
S=\{v^{\prime}\}
) before, we add an isolated vertex
v_{S}
and then add two edges
(v,v_{S})
and
(v^{\prime},v_{S})
with lengths
h_{j}
.

•

Similarly, for each virtual node deletions which removes
v
from a cluster
S
, if
|S|\geq 2
after the deletion, we delete the edge
(v,v_{S})
. If
|S|=1
(say
S=\{v^{\prime}\}
) after the deletion, we delete two edges
(v^{\prime},v_{S}),(v,v_{S})
and then the isolated vertex
v_{S}
. Finally, if
v\in V(G^{(i-1)})\setminus V(G^{(i)})
is a removed isolated vertex, we apply an isolated vertex deletion
v
.

Correctness.

Note that at each moment
0\leq i\leq t
, the
H^{(i)}
constructed here is a super graph of the sparsifier
H
constructed in the proof of
Lemma

8.3
given
\{(C_{j}^{(i)},L_{j}^{(i)},{\cal N}_{j}^{(i)})\mid 1\leq j\leq\bar{j}\}
, because the star union is exactly the same, and the heavy edge set from
Lemma

8.3
is a subset of
E^{(i)}_{\mathrm{heavy}}
. The latter holds by the following reason. For each edge
e\in E(G^{(i)})
with
C_{j}^{(i)}(e)\geq h_{j}/10
, let
\hat{i}
denote the first time it is added into
G
. Then
C_{j}^{(i)}(e)=\sum_{\hat{i}\leq i^{\prime}\leq i}C_{{\rm new},j}^{(i)}(e)
(we let
C^{(0)}_{{\rm new},j}(e)=C^{(0)}_{j}(e)
for simplicity), and by averaging,
C_{{\rm new},j}^{(i^{\prime})}(e)\geq h_{j}/(t\cdot 20)
for some moment
\hat{i}\leq i^{\prime}\leq i
, so
e
was added to
E_{\mathrm{heavy}}^{(i^{\prime})}
at that time and remains in
E_{\mathrm{heavy}}^{(i)}
because
e
did not get deleted.

Therefore, we can conclude that
H^{(i)}
is an
(\alpha_{\mathrm{low}},\alpha_{\mathrm{up}},h)
-sparsifier of
\bar{T}^{(i)}
on
G^{(i)}
with
\alpha_{\mathrm{up}}=O(1)
.
The number of vertices in
H^{(i)}
is at most
|V(H^{(i)})|\leq\sum_{j}\mathrm{size}({\cal N}^{(i)}_{j})\leq O(\sum_{j}\omega^{(i)}_{j}\cdot|\bar{T}^{(i)}|)
. The number of edges in
H^{(i)}
is bounded by

\displaystyle|E(H^{(i)})|

\displaystyle=|E(H^{\star,(i)})|+|E_{\mathrm{heavy}}^{(i)}|

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{j}\mathrm{size}({\cal N}_{j}^{(i)})+\sum_{j}|C^{(i)}_{j}|/(h_{j}/(20\cdot t))

\displaystyle\leq O(\sum_{j}\omega_{j}^{(i)}\cdot|\bar{T}^{(i)}|+t\cdot\sum_{j}|C_{j}^{(i)}|/h_{j}).

Note that
|E^{(i)}_{\mathrm{heavy}}|\leq\sum_{j}|C^{(i)}_{j}|/(h_{j}/(20\cdot t))
because an edge
e
with
C^{(i)}_{j}<h_{j}/(20\cdot t)
will not be select in
E^{(i)}_{\mathrm{heavy}}
.

Vertices in
V(H^{(i)})\setminus\bar{T}^{(i)}
are fresh because they are the artificial centers of stars, and we can definitely assign totally new global identifers to them.

The recourse from
H^{(i-1)}
to
H^{(i)}
is bounded by the number of unit updates in
\pi_{H}^{(i)}
. By our construction of
\pi_{H}^{(i)}
, we have

\displaystyle~~~~|E^{(i)}_{\mathrm{heavy},{\rm new}}|+|E^{(i)}_{G,\mathrm{delete}}|+\sum_{j}O(\mathrm{Rcs}({\cal N}_{j}^{(i-1)}\to{\cal N}_{j}^{(i)}))

\displaystyle=O(|\pi^{(i)}|+\sum_{j}(t\cdot|C^{(i)}_{{\rm new},j}|/h_{j}+\mathrm{Rcs}({\cal N}^{(i-1)}_{j}\to{\cal N}^{(i)}_{j}))).

The running time is straightforward from the algorithm description.

∎

8.2
Proof of
Theorem

8.2

We will prove
Theorem

8.2
combining
Lemma

8.11
and an online-batch dynamic algorithm for the collection of specialized certified-EDs shown below. The latter is quite similar to the algorithm in
Theorem

6.4
, except the following. First, we work on
\rho
-dense certified-EDs instead to reduce the recourse of pairwise covers (so the recourse of the sparsifier). Second, recall that the specialized certified-EDs should be with respect to the union of original terminals and all landmarks. Hence, after a usual update step (or the usual initialization step), we will invoke
Lemma

7.11
to add the newly generated landmarks to the node-weighting.

Again, in the following proof, we assume without loss of generality each batched update contains only one type of unit updates.

Proof.

We have argued in
Lemma

8.11
that to maintain a sparsifier, it suffices to maintain
\bar{j}=\lceil 100\cdot h\rceil
levels of pairwise covers. To do this, we will maintain certifed-EDs instead. At the end of each update
i
(and the initialization with
i=0
) and for each level
1\leq j\leq\bar{j}
, the up-to-date dense certified-ED is a
\bar{\rho}^{(i)}
-dense certified-ED
(\bar{C}_{j}^{(i)},\bar{L}_{j}^{(i)},\bar{{\cal N}}_{j}^{(i)},\bar{{\cal R}}_{j}^{(i)},\Pi_{\bar{{\cal R}}_{j}^{(i)}\to G^{(i)}})
of
\bar{A}^{(i)}
on
G^{(i)}
where

\bar{A}^{(i)}=\deg_{G^{(i)}}+\mathds{1}(V(G^{(i)}))+\mathds{1}(T^{(i)})+\mathds{1}(\bigcup_{j^{\prime}}\bar{L}^{(i)}_{j^{\prime}})

with density function

\bar{\rho}^{(i)}\geq\left\{\begin{aligned} &1,\ v\in\deg_{G^{(i)}}+\mathds{1}(V(G^{(i)}))\\
&\frac{(1-1/\mu)^{i}}{\phi},\ v\in\mathds{1}(T^{(i)})+\mathds{1}(\bigcup_{j^{\prime}}\bar{L}^{(i)}_{j^{\prime}})\end{aligned}\right.,

where we fix
\mu=2t
and it is the parameter fed to
Theorem

7.8
. We let
\bar{h}^{(i)}_{j}=\bar{h}^{(i)}_{{\rm cov},j}
be the cover radius of
\bar{\cal N}^{(i)}_{j}
(namely, the length parameter of this dense certified-ED).

The framework of maintaining certified-EDs is as follows. The whole initialization step will invoke
Lemma

7.4
(paragraph
Initialization
) and then
Lemma

7.11
(paragraph
Insert New Landmarks to the Node-Weighting
). For each update step for
\pi^{(i)}
, if it represents isolated vertex insertions or deletions, we go to paragraph
Isolated Vertex Insertions/Deletions
. Otherwise, we will invoke one of
Theorem

7.8
,
Theorem

7.5
and
Theorem

7.10
(paragraphs
Edge Deletion
,
Edge Insertion
and
Terminals Insertion
respectively) depends on the type of
\pi^{(i)}
, and then again invoke
Lemma

7.11
(paragraph
Insert New Landmarks to the Node-Weighting
).

Initialization.

Let
A^{(0)}
be a node-weighting defined by

A^{(0)}=\deg_{G^{(0)}}+\mathds{1}(T^{(0)})\text{ with density function }\rho^{(0)}=\left\{\begin{aligned} &1,\ v\in\deg_{G^{(0)}}\\
&1/\phi,\ v\in\mathds{1}(T^{(0)})\end{aligned}\right..

For each level
1\leq j\leq\bar{j}
, we let

h_{j}=2^{j},\ h_{j}^{(0)}=h_{j}\cdot 2^{O(t\cdot\lambda_{\mathrm{insLM},t})}.

Then we apply
Lemma

7.4
on the initial graph
G^{(0)}
with the
\rho^{(0)}
-dense node-weighting
A^{(0)}
, length parameter
h^{(0)}_{j}
and congestion parameter
\phi^{(0)}_{j}=\phi/\kappa_{{\rm init},L}
.

The output is a
\rho^{(0)}
-dense certified-ED
(C^{(0)}_{j},L^{(0)}_{j},{\cal N}^{(0)}_{j},{\cal R}^{(0)}_{j},\Pi_{{\cal R}^{(0)}_{j}\to G^{(0)}})
of
A^{(0)}
on
G^{(0)}
satisfying that

•

The initial moving cut
C^{(0)}_{j}
has size

\displaystyle|C^{(0)}_{j}|

\displaystyle\leq O(\kappa_{{\rm init},C}\cdot\phi^{(0)}_{j}\cdot h^{(0)}_{j}\cdot\rho^{(0)}(A^{(0)}))\leq O(\phi\cdot h^{(0)}_{j}\cdot\rho^{(0)}(A^{(0)}))\leq O(h^{(0)}_{j}\cdot(\phi\cdot|E(G^{(0)})|+|T^{(0)}|)),

where the second inequality is by
\kappa_{{\rm init},L}\geq\kappa_{{\rm init},C}
.

•

L^{(0)}_{j}
has distortion
\sigma^{(0)}_{j}=O(h_{j}^{(0)}/\kappa_{\sigma})
and size

\displaystyle|L^{(0)}_{j}|

\displaystyle\leq O(\kappa_{{\rm init},L}\cdot\phi^{(0)}_{j}\cdot\rho^{(0)}(A^{(0)}))\leq O(\phi\cdot\rho^{(0)}(A^{(0)}))\leq O(\phi\cdot|E(G^{(0)})|+|T^{(0)}|).

•

{\cal N}^{(0)}_{j}
is a
\rho^{(0)}
-dense
(h^{(0)}_{{\rm cov},j},h^{(0)}_{\mathrm{sep},j},\omega^{(0)}_{j})
-pairwise cover of
A^{(0)}
on
G^{(0)}
with
h^{(0)}_{{\rm cov},j}=h^{(0)}_{\mathrm{sep},j}=h_{j}^{(0)}
and
\omega_{j}^{(0)}=\kappa_{\mathrm{NC},\omega}
.

•

Routers in
{\cal R}_{j}
are initialized by
Theorem

5.1
and suffer
f^{(0)}=0
batched update.

•

\Pi_{{\cal R}^{(0)}_{j}\to G^{(0)}}
has length and congestion

\displaystyle h^{(0)}_{{\rm emb},j}

\displaystyle=O(\lambda_{{\rm init},h}\cdot h_{j}^{(0)})

\displaystyle\gamma^{(0)}_{{\rm emb},j}

\displaystyle=O(\kappa_{{\rm init},\gamma}/\phi^{(0)}_{j})=O(\kappa_{{\rm init},L}\cdot\kappa_{{\rm init},\gamma}/\phi)

Edge Deletion.

Suppose the update
i
is a batched edge deletion
\pi^{(i)}=F^{(i)}\subseteq E(G^{(i-1)})
and the graph becomes
G^{(i)}=G^{(i-1)}\setminus F^{(i)}
.

For each level
j
and its certified-ED
(\bar{C}_{j}^{(i-1)},\bar{L}_{j}^{(i-1)},\bar{{\cal N}}_{j}^{(i-1)},\bar{{\cal R}}_{j}^{(i-1)},\Pi_{\bar{{\cal R}}_{j}^{(i-1)}\to G^{(i-1)}})
of
\bar{A}^{(i-1)}
on
G^{(i-1)}
, we apply
Theorem

7.8
on it with edge deletions
F_{i}
and parameters
\mu=2t
and

\phi^{(i)}_{j}=t\cdot(\bar{\omega}^{(i-1)}_{j}\cdot\kappa_{\mathrm{CM},\gamma}+\kappa_{{\rm init},\gamma})/\bar{\gamma}_{{\rm emb},j}^{(i-1)}

The output is a
\rho^{(i)}
-dense certified-ED
(C_{j}^{(i)},L_{j}^{(i)},{\cal N}_{j}^{(i)},{\cal R}_{j}^{(i)},\Pi_{{\cal R}_{j}^{(i)}\to G^{(i)}})
of
A^{(i)}
satisfying the following. To avoid clutter, we define a parameter
\chi_{j}^{(i)}
for each
0\leq i\leq t,1\leq j\leq\bar{j}
, which will only be used in this proof.

\chi_{j}^{(i)}=\bar{\omega}^{(i-1)}_{j}\cdot(\kappa_{\mathrm{CM},L}+\kappa_{{\rm init},L})\cdot\mu\cdot\lambda_{\mathrm{rt},{\rm prune}}(\bar{f}^{(i-1)})\cdot t\cdot(\bar{\omega}^{(i-1)}\cdot\kappa_{\mathrm{CM},\gamma}+\kappa_{{\rm init},\gamma}).

•

A^{(i)}=\bar{A}^{(i-1)}-\deg_{F^{(i)}}
with density
\rho^{(i)}=\lceil(1-1/\lambda)\cdot\bar{\rho}^{(i-1)}\rceil
, where the virtual nodes
\deg_{F^{(i)}}
we remove are from
\deg_{G^{(i-1)}}\subseteq\bar{A}^{(i-1)}
(each of them has density
1
). Strictly speaking, the output of
Theorem

7.8
is a dense certified-ED of
\bar{A}^{(i-1)}
instead of
A^{(i)}
, but this can be easily fixed by removing virtual nodes
\deg_{F^{(i)}}
from
{\cal N}^{(i)}_{j}
, which will not change the quality.

•

C_{j}^{(i)}=(\bar{C}_{j}^{(i-1)})_{\mid E(G^{(i)})}+C_{{\rm new},j}^{(i)}
with

\displaystyle|C^{(i)}_{{\rm new},j}|

\displaystyle\leq O(\bar{\omega}_{j}^{(i-1)}\cdot\kappa_{{\rm init},C}\cdot\phi^{(i)}_{j}\cdot\mu\cdot\lambda_{\mathrm{rt},{\rm prune}}(\bar{f}^{(i-1)}_{j})\cdot\bar{\gamma}^{(i-1)}_{{\rm emb},j}\cdot|\pi^{(i)}|\cdot\bar{h}^{(i-1)}_{j})\leq O(\chi_{j}^{(i-1)}\cdot|\pi^{(i)}|\cdot\bar{h}_{j}^{(i-1)}),

by the definition of
\phi^{(i)}_{j}
and
\kappa_{{\rm init},C}\leq\kappa_{\mathrm{CM},L}+\kappa_{{\rm init},L}
.

•

L_{j}^{(i)}=\bar{L}_{j}^{(i-1)}\cup L_{{\rm new},j}^{(i)}
with distortion
\sigma_{j}^{(i)}=\bar{\sigma}_{j}^{(i-1)}+O(\bar{h}_{j}^{(i-1)}/\kappa_{\sigma})
and

\displaystyle|L^{(i)}_{{\rm new},j}|

\displaystyle\leq O(\bar{\omega}^{(i-1)}_{j}\cdot(\kappa_{\mathrm{CM},L}+\kappa_{{\rm init},L})\cdot\phi^{(i)}_{j}\cdot\mu\cdot\lambda_{\mathrm{rt},{\rm prune}}(\bar{f}^{(i-1)}_{j})\cdot\bar{\gamma}_{{\rm emb},j}^{(i-1)}\cdot|\pi^{(i)}|)\leq O(\chi_{j}^{(i-1)}\cdot|\pi^{(i)}|).

•

The pairwise cover
{\cal N}_{j}^{(i)}
of
A^{(i)}
on
G^{(i)}
has

h^{(i)}_{{\rm cov},j}=h^{(i)}_{\mathrm{sep},j}=\bar{h}_{j}^{(i-1)}/3,\text{ and }\omega^{(i)}_{j}=\bar{\omega}^{(i-1)}_{j}+\kappa_{\mathrm{NC},\omega}.

•

Routers in
{\cal R}_{j}^{(i)}
are maintained by
Theorem

5.1
under at most
f^{(i)}_{j}=\bar{f}^{(i-1)}_{j}+O(1)
updates.

•

\Pi_{{\cal R}^{(i)}_{j}\to G^{(i)}}
has

\displaystyle h^{(i)}_{{\rm emb},j}

\displaystyle=\max\{\bar{h}^{(i-1)}_{{\rm emb},j},O(\lambda_{{\rm init},h}\cdot\bar{h}^{(i-1)}_{j})\}

\displaystyle\gamma^{(i)}_{{\rm emb},j}

\displaystyle=\bar{\gamma}^{(i-1)}_{{\rm emb},j}+O((\bar{\omega}_{j}^{(i)}\cdot\kappa_{\mathrm{CM},\gamma}+\kappa_{{\rm init},\gamma})/\phi^{(i)}_{j})=(1+O(1/t))\cdot\bar{\gamma}^{(i-1)}_{{\rm emb},j}.

Terminals Insertion.

Suppose the update
\pi^{(i)}
is a batched-terminal insertion
T^{(i)}_{{\rm new}}
. Let
A^{(i)}_{{\rm new}}=\mathds{1}(T^{(i)}_{{\rm new}})
with
1/\phi
-uniform density
\rho^{(i)}_{{\rm new}}
.

For each level
j
, we apply
Theorem

7.5
on the certified-ED
(\bar{C}^{(i-1)}_{j},\bar{L}^{(i-1)}_{j},\bar{\cal N}^{(i-1)}_{j},\bar{\cal R}^{(i-1)}_{j},\Pi_{\bar{\cal R}^{(i-1)}_{j}\to G^{(i-1)}})
of
\bar{A}^{(i-1)}_{j}
on
G^{(i-1)}
with the
\rho^{(i)}_{{\rm new}}
-dense node weighting
A^{(i)}_{{\rm new}}
and parameter

\phi^{(i)}_{j}=\phi/(\bar{\omega}^{(i-1)}_{j}\cdot(\kappa_{\mathrm{CM},L}+\kappa_{{\rm init},L})).

The output is a
\rho^{(i)}
-dense certified-ED
(C^{(i)}_{j},L^{(i)}_{j},{\cal N}^{(i)}_{j},{\cal R}^{(i)}_{j},\Pi_{{\cal R}^{(i)}_{j}\to G^{(i)}})
of
A^{(i)}
on
G^{(i)}
satisfying the following.

•

A^{(i)}=\bar{A}^{(i-1)}+A^{(i)}_{{\rm new}}
with density
\rho^{(i)}=\bar{\rho}^{(i-1)}\uplus\rho^{(i)}_{{\rm new}}
.

•

C^{(i)}_{j}=\bar{C}^{(i-1)}_{j}+C^{(i)}_{{\rm new},j}
with

\displaystyle|C^{(i)}_{{\rm new},j}|

\displaystyle\leq O(\kappa_{{\rm init},C}\cdot\bar{\omega}^{(i-1)}_{j}\cdot\phi^{(i)}_{j}\cdot\bar{h}^{(i-1)}_{j}\cdot\rho_{{\rm new}}(A^{(i)}_{{\rm new}}))\leq O(|\pi^{(i)}|\cdot\bar{h}^{(i-1)}_{j}),

where the second inequality is because
\rho_{{\rm new}}(A^{(i)}_{{\rm new}})=|A^{(i)}_{{\rm new}}|/\phi
and
\kappa_{{\rm init},C}\leq\kappa_{\mathrm{CM},L}+\kappa_{{\rm init},L}
.

•

L^{(i)}_{j}=\bar{L}^{(i-1)}_{j}\cup L^{(i)}_{{\rm new},j}
with distortion
\sigma^{(i)}_{j}=\bar{\sigma}^{(i-1)}_{j}+O(\bar{h}^{(i-1)}_{j}/\kappa_{\sigma})
.

\displaystyle|L^{(i)}_{{\rm new},j}|

\displaystyle\leq O(\bar{\omega}^{(i-1)}_{j}\cdot(\kappa_{\mathrm{CM},L}+\kappa_{{\rm init},L})\cdot\phi_{j}^{(i)}\cdot\rho_{{\rm new}}(A_{{\rm new}}))\leq O(|\pi^{(i)}|).

•

The pairwise cover
{\cal N}_{j}^{(i)}
of
A^{(i)}
on
G^{(i)}
has

h^{(i)}_{{\rm cov},j}=h^{(i)}_{\mathrm{sep},j}=\bar{h}_{j}^{(i-1)}/3,\text{ and }\omega^{(i)}_{j}=\bar{\omega}^{(i-1)}_{j}+\kappa_{\mathrm{NC},\omega}.

•

Routers in
{\cal R}_{j}^{(i)}
are maintained by
Theorem

5.1
under at most
f^{(i)}=\bar{f}^{(i-1)}+O(1)
updates.

•

\Pi_{{\cal R}^{(i)}_{j}\to G^{(i)}}
has length and congestion

\displaystyle h^{(i)}_{{\rm emb},j}

\displaystyle=\max\{\bar{h}^{(i-1)}_{{\rm emb},j},O(\lambda_{{\rm init},h}\cdot\bar{h}^{(i-1)}_{j})\}

\displaystyle\gamma^{(i)}_{{\rm emb},j}

\displaystyle=\bar{\gamma}^{(i-1)}_{{\rm emb},j}+O((\bar{\omega}^{(i-1)}_{j}\cdot\kappa_{\mathrm{CM},\gamma}+\kappa_{{\rm init},\gamma})/\phi^{(i)}_{j})

\displaystyle\leq\bar{\gamma}^{(i-1)}_{{\rm emb},j}+O(\bar{\omega}^{(i-1)}_{j}\cdot\kappa_{\mathrm{CM},\gamma}+\kappa_{{\rm init},\gamma})\cdot\bar{\omega}^{(i-1)}_{j}\cdot(\kappa_{\mathrm{CM},L}+\kappa_{{\rm init},L})/\phi.

Edge Insertion.

If the update
\pi^{(i)}
is a batched edge insertion
E^{(i)}_{{\rm new}}
, let
G^{(i)}=G^{(i-1)}\cup E^{(i)}_{{\rm new}}
. Similarly, we apply
Theorem

7.10
on
(\bar{C}^{(i-1)}_{j},\bar{L}^{(i-1)}_{j},\bar{\cal N}^{(i-1)}_{j},\bar{\cal R}^{(i-1)}_{j},\Pi_{\bar{\cal R}^{(i-1)}_{j}\to G^{(i-1)}})
of
\bar{A}^{(i-1)}_{j}
on
G^{(i-1)}
with new edges
E^{(i)}_{{\rm new}}
and parameter

\phi^{(i)}_{j}=\phi/(\bar{\omega}^{(i-1)}_{j}\cdot\kappa_{\mathrm{CM},L}+\kappa_{{\rm init},L}).

The output is a
\rho^{(i)}
-dense certified-ED
(C^{(i)}_{j},L^{(i)}_{j},{\cal N}^{(i)}_{j},{\cal R}^{(i)}_{j},\Pi_{{\cal R}^{(i)}_{j}\to G^{(i)}})
of
A^{(i)}
on
G^{(i)}
satisfying that
A^{(i)}=\bar{A}^{(i-1)}+A_{{\rm new}}^{(i)}
and
\rho^{(i)}=\bar{\rho}^{(i-1)}\uplus\rho^{(i)}_{{\rm new}}
where
A_{{\rm new}}^{(i)}=\deg_{E^{(i)}_{{\rm new}}}
and
\rho^{(i)}_{{\rm new}}=\mathds{1}(A_{{\rm new}}^{(i)})
. All quality parameters have the same asymtotic bounds as those in the terminal insertion case.

Insert New Landmarks to the Node-Weighting.

In each iteration
i
(including the initialization with
i=0
), after computing
(C^{(i)}_{j},L^{(i)}_{j},{\cal N}^{(i)}_{j},{\cal R}^{(i)}_{j},\Pi_{{\cal R}^{(i)}_{j}\to G^{(i)}})
, we still need
Lemma

7.11
to add the new landmarks into the node-weighting. Precisely, let the new landmarks for each
j
be
L^{\star,(i)}_{j}=L_{{\rm new},j}^{(i)}\setminus\bigcup_{j^{\prime}}\bar{L}_{j^{\prime}}^{(i-1)}
(if
i=0
,
L^{\star,(0)}_{j}=L^{(0)}_{j}
). Then we apply
Lemma

7.11
on the collection of certified-EDs
\{(C^{(i)}_{j},L^{(i)}_{j},{\cal N}^{(i)}_{j},{\cal R}^{(i)}_{j},\Pi_{{\cal R}^{(i)}_{j}\to G^{(i)}})\mid 1\leq j\leq\bar{j}\}
of
A^{(i)}
on
G^{(i)}
with parameter
\phi
and new landmarks
L^{\star,(i)}_{j}\subseteq L^{(i)}_{j}
specified for each
j
.

The output is a collection of
\bar{\rho}^{(i)}
-dense certified-EDs
\{(\bar{C}_{j}^{(i)},\bar{L}_{j}^{(i)},\bar{\cal N}_{j}^{(i)},\bar{\cal R}_{j}^{(i)},\Pi_{\bar{\cal R}_{j}^{(i)}\to G^{(i)}})\mid 1\leq j\leq\bar{j}\}
of
\bar{A}^{(i)}
on
G
satisfying the following. Let
h^{(i)}_{j}=h^{(i)}_{{\rm cov},j}
be the cover radius of
{\cal N}_{j}^{(i)}
for each
j
.
Let
\tau^{(i)}=\sum_{j}|L^{\star,(i)}_{j}|
.

•

\bar{C}_{j}^{(i)}=C_{j}^{(i)}+\bar{C}_{{\rm new},j^{\prime}}^{(i)}
with
|\bar{C}_{{\rm new},j}^{(i)}|=O(h_{j}^{(i)}\cdot\tau^{(i)})

•

\bar{L}_{j}^{(i)}=L_{j}^{(i)}\cup\bar{L}_{{\rm new},j}^{(i)}
with distortion
\bar{\sigma}_{j}^{(i)}=\sigma_{j}^{(i)}+O(h_{j}^{(i)}/\kappa_{\sigma})
and
|\bar{L}_{{\rm new},j}^{(i)}|=O(\tau^{(i)})
.

•

\bar{A}^{(i)}=A^{(i)}+\bar{A}_{{\rm new}}^{(i)}
and
\bar{\rho}^{(i)}=\rho^{(i)}\uplus\bar{\rho}^{(i)}_{{\rm new}}
, where
\bar{A}_{{\rm new}}^{(i)}=\mathds{1}(\bigcup_{j}L^{\star,(i)}_{j})+\mathds{1}(\bigcup_{j}\bar{L}^{(i)}_{{\rm new},j}\setminus\bigcup_{j}L^{(i)}_{j})
and
\bar{\rho}^{(i)}_{{\rm new}}=(1/\phi)\cdot\mathds{1}(\bar{A}^{(i)}_{{\rm new}})
.

•

\bar{\cal N}^{(i)}_{j}
is a
\bar{\rho}^{(i)}
-dense
(\bar{h}_{{\rm cov},j}^{(i)},\bar{h}_{\mathrm{sep},j}^{(i)},\bar{\omega}_{j}^{(i)})
-pairwise cover of
\bar{A}^{(i)}
on
G^{(i)}
with

\bar{h}_{{\rm cov},j}^{(i)}=\bar{h}_{\mathrm{sep},j}^{(i)}=h_{j}^{(i)}/3^{\lambda_{\mathrm{insLM},t}},\text{ and }\bar{\omega}_{j}^{(i)}=\omega_{j}^{(i)}+O(\lambda_{\mathrm{insLM},t}\cdot\kappa_{\mathrm{NC},\omega}).

•

Routers in
\bar{\cal R}_{j}^{(i)}
is maintained by
Theorem

5.1
under at most
\bar{f}^{(i)}=f^{(i)}+O(\lambda_{\mathrm{insLM},t})
update batches.

•

\Pi_{\bar{\cal R}_{j}^{(i)}\to G^{(i)}}
has length
\bar{h}_{{\rm emb},j}^{(i)}=\max\{h_{{\rm emb},j}^{(i)},O(\lambda_{{\rm init},h}\cdot h_{j}^{(i)})\}
and congestion
\bar{\gamma}_{{\rm emb},j}^{(i)}=\gamma_{{\rm emb},j}^{(i)}+\Gamma_{j}^{(i)}/\phi

where we define

\displaystyle\Gamma^{(i)}_{j}=O(\bar{\omega}^{(i)}_{j}\cdot(\kappa_{\mathrm{CM},L}+\kappa_{{\rm init},L})\cdot(\bar{\omega}^{(i)}_{j}\cdot\kappa_{\mathrm{CM},\gamma}+\kappa_{{\rm init},\gamma})\cdot\kappa_{\mathrm{insLM},\gamma}\cdot\lambda_{\mathrm{insLM},t})

as a parameter only used in this proof.

Isolated Vertex Insertions/Deletions.

Similar to the proof of
Theorem

6.4
, it is easy to handle isolated vertex insertions and deletions.

Regarding isolated vertex insertions
V_{{\rm new}}
, for each
v\in V_{{\rm new}}
, we assign it node-weighting
\bar{A}^{(i)}(v)=1
and the density of the unique virtual node
v_{\mathrm{vtul}}\in\bar{A}^{(i)}(v)
is
\bar{\rho}^{(i)}(v_{\mathrm{vtul}})=1
. For all level
j
, we create a singleton cluster
S=\{v_{\mathrm{vtul}}\}
and add
S
into an arbitrary clustering of
\bar{\cal N}^{(i-1)}_{j}
(also initialize the corresponding router
R
of
S
, and
R
can be embedded into
G^{(i)}
trivially).

Regarding isolated vertex deletions
V_{\mathrm{del}}\subseteq V(G^{(i-1)})
, we know each
v\in V_{\mathrm{del}}
has
\bar{A}^{(i-1)}(v)\leq 3
, because
\deg_{G^{(i-1)}}(v)=0
. Therefore, we just need to remove up to
3
virtual nodes
\bar{A}^{(i-1)}(v)
from
\bar{\cal N}^{(i-1)}_{j}
.

Obviously these two types of batched updates will not affect the quality of the certified-EDs. Trivially, the total recourse is
\sum_{j}\mathrm{Rcs}(\bar{\cal N}^{(i-1)}_{j}\to\bar{\cal N}^{(i)}_{j})\leq O(\bar{\omega}^{(i)}_{j}\cdot\bar{j}\cdot|\pi^{(i)}|)
, and the update time is
O(\bar{\omega}^{(i)}_{j}\cdot\bar{j}\cdot|\pi^{(i)}|)=|\pi^{(i)}|\cdot n^{O(\epsilon)}
. As we will see, the bounds on the recourse and update time are asymptotically smaller than those of other types of batched updates. Therefore, to avoid clutter, we will ignore these two types in the following proof.

Quality of Certified-EDs.

At the end of the initialization step
i=0
and each update step
1\leq i\leq t
, for each level
1\leq j\leq\bar{j}
, the certified-ED
(\bar{C}_{j}^{(i)},\bar{L}_{j}^{(i)},\bar{\cal N}_{j}^{(i)},\bar{\cal R}^{(i)}_{j},\Pi_{\bar{\cal R}_{j}^{(i)}\to G^{(i)}})
of
\bar{A}^{(i)}
on
G^{(i)}
satisfies the following.

The moving cut
\bar{C}^{(i)}_{j}
.
Initially,
\bar{C}_{j}^{(0)}
has size

\displaystyle|\bar{C}^{(0)}_{j}|

\displaystyle=|C^{(0)}_{j}|+|\bar{C}^{(0)}_{{\rm new},j}|\leq|C^{(0)}_{j}|+O(h_{j}^{(0)}\cdot\sum_{1\leq j^{\prime}\leq\bar{j}}|L^{(0)}_{j^{\prime}}|)\leq\kappa_{j}\cdot 2^{O(t\cdot\lambda_{\mathrm{insLM},t})}\cdot h_{j}\cdot(\phi\cdot|E(G^{(0)})|+|T^{(0)}|).

For each time
i\geq 1
,
\bar{C}^{(i)}_{j}=\bar{C}^{(i-1)}_{j}+C^{(i)}_{{\rm new},j}+\bar{C}^{(i)}_{{\rm new},j}
. Before deriving the size of the new moving cut, we first give a
global
upper bound on
\chi_{j^{\prime}}^{(i)}
over all
0\leq i\leq t
and
1\leq j^{\prime}\leq\bar{j}
(recall that
\chi_{j^{\prime}}^{(i)}
is defined in the paragraph
Edge Deletion
), which is

\chi=O(\lambda_{\mathrm{rt},{\rm prune}}(t\cdot\lambda_{\mathrm{insLM},t})\cdot t^{4}\cdot\lambda_{\mathrm{insLM},t}^{2}\cdot\kappa_{\mathrm{NC},\omega}^{2}\cdot(\kappa_{\mathrm{CM},L}+\kappa_{{\rm init},L})\cdot(\kappa_{\mathrm{CM},\gamma}+\kappa_{{\rm init},\gamma})),

and then upper bound
\tau^{(i)}
for all
1\leq i\leq t
. Concretely,

\tau^{(i)}=\sum_{1\leq j^{\prime}\leq\bar{j}}|L^{\star,(i)}_{j^{\prime}}|\leq\sum_{1\leq j^{\prime}\leq\bar{j}}|L^{(i)}_{{\rm new},j^{\prime}}|\leq\sum_{1\leq j^{\prime}\leq\bar{j}}O(\chi_{j}^{(i-1)}\cdot|\pi^{(i)}|)\leq O(\kappa_{j}\cdot\chi\cdot|\pi^{(i)}|),

because the bottleneck is the batched edge deletion update. Providing the bound on
\tau^{(i)}
, the size of new moving cut is

\displaystyle|C^{(i)}_{{\rm new},j}|+|\bar{C}^{(i)}_{{\rm new},j}|

\displaystyle\leq|C^{(i)}_{{\rm new},j}|+O(h^{(i)}_{j}\cdot\tau^{(i)})\leq O(\chi_{j}^{(i-1)}\cdot|\pi^{(i)}|\cdot\bar{h}_{j}^{(i-1)})+O(\kappa_{j}\cdot\chi\cdot|\pi^{(i)}|\cdot h^{(i)}_{j})

\displaystyle\leq\kappa_{j}\cdot\chi\cdot|\pi^{(i)}|\cdot h_{j}\cdot 2^{O(t\cdot\lambda_{\mathrm{insLM},t})},

because
\bar{h}_{j}^{(i)}
and
h_{j}^{(i)}
have an upper bound
h_{j}^{(0)}=h_{j}\cdot 2^{O(t\cdot\lambda_{\mathrm{insLM},t})}
over all
i
.

The Landmark Set
\bar{L}^{(i)}_{j}
.
Similar to the calculation of the moving cut, the inital landmark set has size
|\bar{L}_{j}^{(0)}|=O(\kappa_{j}\cdot(\phi\cdot|E(G^{(0)})|+|T^{(0)}|))
. For each update step
i\geq 1
,
\bar{L}^{(i)}_{j}=\bar{L}^{(i-1)}_{j}\cup L^{(i)}_{{\rm new},j}\cup\bar{L}^{(i)}_{{\rm new},j}
and the number of new landmarks is at most

\displaystyle~~~~|L^{(i)}_{{\rm new},j}|+|\bar{L}^{(i)}_{{\rm new},j}|\leq|L^{(i)}_{{\rm new},j}|+O(\tau^{(i)})\leq O(\kappa_{j}\cdot\chi\cdot|\pi^{(i)}|).

The distortion
\bar{\sigma}^{(i)}_{j}=\sum_{0\leq i^{\prime}\leq i}O(h^{(i)}_{j}+\bar{h}^{(i)}_{j})/\kappa_{\sigma}
is always bounded by
2^{O(t\cdot\lambda_{\mathrm{insLM},t})}\cdot h_{j}/\kappa_{\sigma}\leq h_{j}/\log^{2}n
because
t=O(1/\epsilon),\lambda_{\mathrm{insLM},t}=O(1/\epsilon)
and
\kappa_{\sigma}=n^{\epsilon^{4}}
.

The Node-Weighting
\bar{A}_{i}
and Density
\bar{\rho}_{i}
.
Initially

\displaystyle\bar{A}^{(0)}

\displaystyle=A^{(0)}+\bar{A}_{{\rm new}}^{(0)}

\displaystyle=\deg_{G^{(0)}}+\mathds{1}(T^{(0)})+\mathds{1}(\bigcup_{j}L_{j}^{(0)})+\mathds{1}(\bigcup_{j}\bar{L}^{(0)}_{{\rm new},j}\setminus\bigcup_{j}L^{(0)}_{j})

\displaystyle=\deg_{G^{(0)}}+\mathds{1}(T^{(0)})+\mathds{1}(\bigcup_{j}\bar{L}_{j}^{(0)})

For each time
i\geq 1
, by a simple induction, we have

\displaystyle\bar{A}^{(i)}

\displaystyle=\bar{A}^{(i-1)}+A^{(i)}_{{\rm new}}+\bar{A}^{(i)}_{{\rm new}}

\displaystyle=\bar{A}^{(i-1)}+\mathds{1}(T^{(i)}_{{\rm new}})-\deg_{F^{(i)}}+\deg_{E_{{\rm new}}^{(i)}}+\mathds{1}(\bigcup_{j}L^{(i)}_{{\rm new},j}\setminus\bigcup_{j}\bar{L}^{(i-1)}_{j})+\mathds{1}(\bigcup_{j}\bar{L}_{{\rm new},j}^{(i)}\setminus\bigcup_{j}L^{(i)}_{j})

\displaystyle=\deg_{G^{(i)}}+\mathds{1}(T^{(i)})+\mathds{1}(\bigcup_{j}\bar{L}^{(i)}_{j})

The density function is

\bar{\rho}^{(i)}\geq\left\{\begin{aligned} &1,\ v\in\deg_{G^{(i)}}\\
&\frac{(1-1/\mu)^{i}}{\phi}\geq\Omega(1/\phi),\ v\in\mathds{1}(T^{(i)})+\mathds{1}(\bigcup_{j}\bar{L}_{j}^{(i)})\end{aligned}\right.,

because
(1-1/\mu)^{i}\geq\Omega(1)
(recall that we set
\mu=2t
).

The Pairwise Cover
\bar{\cal N}^{(i)}_{j}
.

\bar{\cal N}_{j}^{(i)}
has quality parameters

\bar{h}_{{\rm cov},j}^{(i)}=\bar{h}_{\mathrm{sep},j}^{(i)}=h_{j}^{(0)}/2^{O(i\cdot\lambda_{\mathrm{insLM},t})}\geq 2^{j}=h_{j},\ \bar{\omega}_{j}^{(i)}=O(t\cdot\lambda_{\mathrm{insLM},t}\cdot\kappa_{\mathrm{NC},\omega}),

because we choose
h^{(0)}_{j}=2^{O(t\cdot\lambda_{\mathrm{insLM},t})}
with sufficiently large constant hided in the exponent.

The Routers.
Routers in
\bar{\cal R}_{j}^{(i)}
are maintained by
Theorem

5.1
undergoing at most
\bar{f}^{(i)}=O(t\cdot\lambda_{\mathrm{insLM},t})
batched updates.

The Embedding.

\Pi_{\bar{\cal R}_{j}^{(i)}\to G^{(i)}}
has length, by induction,

\displaystyle\bar{h}^{(i)}_{{\rm emb},j}

\displaystyle=\max\{\bar{h}^{(i-1)}_{{\rm emb},j},O(\lambda_{{\rm init},h}\cdot\bar{h}^{(i-1)}_{j}),O(\lambda_{{\rm init},h}\cdot h^{(i)}_{j})\}\leq\lambda_{{\rm init},h}\cdot 2^{O(t\cdot\lambda_{\mathrm{insLM},t})}\cdot h_{j}.

The congestion
\bar{\gamma}_{{\rm emb},j}^{(i)}
can be bounded as follows. For the initialization and each
\pi^{(i)}
representing terminal insertions or edge insertions, from
\bar{\gamma}^{(i-1)}_{{\rm emb},j}
to
\bar{\gamma}^{(i)}_{{\rm emb},j}
, the congestion increases additively and the increasing is dominated by the landmark insertion step. Formally, we have
\bar{\gamma}_{{\rm emb},j}^{(0)}=O(\Gamma^{(0)}_{j}/\phi)
and
\bar{\gamma}_{{\rm emb},j}^{(i)}=\bar{\gamma}^{(i-1)}_{{\rm emb},j}+O(\Gamma_{j}^{(i)}/\phi)
if
\pi^{(i)}
represents terminal insertion or edge insertion. We further define

\Gamma=O(t^{2}\cdot\lambda_{\mathrm{insLM},t}^{3}\cdot\kappa_{\mathrm{NC},\omega}^{2}\cdot(\kappa_{\mathrm{CM},L}+\kappa_{{\rm init},L})\cdot(\kappa_{\mathrm{CM},\gamma}+\kappa_{{\rm init},\gamma})\cdot\kappa_{\mathrm{insLM},\gamma})

to be a global upper bound of
\Gamma^{(i)}_{j}
over all
0\leq i\leq t
and
1\leq j\leq\bar{j}
. Then we can rewrite
\bar{\gamma}_{{\rm emb},j}^{(0)}=O(\Gamma/\phi)
and
\bar{\gamma}_{{\rm emb},j}^{(i)}=\bar{\gamma}^{(i-1)}_{{\rm emb},j}+O(\Gamma/\phi)
.

When
\pi^{(i)}
represents a batched edge deletion, the congestion increases multiplicatively by a factor
(1+O(1/t))
and then increases additively in the following landmark insertion step. Namely,
\bar{\gamma}^{(i)}_{{\rm emb},j}=(1+O(1/t))\cdot\bar{\gamma}^{(i-1)}_{{\rm emb},j}+O(\Gamma/\phi)
. Therefore,

\displaystyle\bar{\gamma}^{(i)}_{{\rm emb},j}

\displaystyle=O(t\cdot\Gamma\cdot(1+O(1/t))^{t}/\phi)=O(t\cdot\Gamma/\phi).

Construction of the sparsifier
H^{(i)}
.

For each
i\geq 0
, let
\bar{T}^{(i)}=T^{(i)}\cup\bigcup_{j}\bar{L}^{(i)}_{j}
. For each
1\leq j\leq\bar{j}
, the restriction of
\bar{\cal N}_{j}^{(i)}
on
\mathds{1}(\bar{T}^{(i)})\subseteq\bar{A}^{(i)}
, denoted by
\bar{\cal N}^{(i)}_{\mathrm{tmn},j}
, is exactly a pairwise cover of terminal set
\bar{T}^{(i)}
on
G^{(i)}-\bar{C}^{(i)}_{j}
with the same quality parameters

\bar{h}^{(i)}_{{\rm cov},j}=h_{j}=2^{j},\ \bar{\omega}^{(i)}_{j}=O(t\cdot\lambda_{\mathrm{insLM},t}\cdot\kappa_{\mathrm{NC},\omega}),

and by
Lemma

6.3
, its diameter on
G^{(i)}
is

\bar{h}^{(i)}_{\mathrm{RealDiam},j}=\lambda_{\mathrm{rt},h}(\bar{f}^{(i)})\cdot\bar{h}^{(i)}_{{\rm emb},j}\leq\lambda_{\mathrm{rt},h}(t\cdot\lambda_{\mathrm{insLM},t})\cdot\lambda_{{\rm init},h}\cdot 2^{O(t\cdot\lambda_{\mathrm{insLM},t})}\cdot h_{j}.

Note that for the dynamic graph
G
and the dynamic terminal set
T
,
\{\bar{C}_{j}^{(i)},\bar{L}_{j}^{(i)},\bar{\cal N}_{\mathrm{tmn},j}^{(i)}\mid 1\leq j\leq\bar{j}\}
is a valid input to
Lemma

8.11
with
\alpha_{\mathrm{low}}=\lambda_{\mathrm{rt},h}(t\cdot\lambda_{\mathrm{insLM},t})\cdot\lambda_{{\rm init},h}\cdot 2^{O(t\cdot\lambda_{\mathrm{insLM},t})}
. Then
Lemma

8.11
will initialize and maintain a
(\alpha_{\mathrm{low}},\alpha_{\mathrm{up}},h)
-sparsifier
H
of some
\bar{T}\supseteq T
with
\alpha_{\mathrm{up}}=O(1)
and the
\alpha_{\mathrm{low}}
above. Furthermore,
Lemma

8.11
guarantees that vertices in
V(H)\setminus\bar{T}
are fresh.

The Size of
H^{(i)}
.

To bound the size of
H^{(i)}
, we will exploit the condition that
|\pi^{(i^{\prime})}|\leq\phi\cdot|G^{(i^{\prime})}|
for each
1\leq i^{\prime}\leq t
. We first give upper bounds of
|\bar{C}^{(i)}_{j}|
and
|\bar{L}^{(i)}_{j}|
for each
1\leq j\leq\bar{j}
. For the moving cut,

\displaystyle|\bar{C}^{(i)}_{j}|

\displaystyle\leq|\bar{C}^{(0)}_{j}|+\sum_{1\leq i^{\prime}\leq i}(|C^{(i^{\prime})}_{{\rm new},j}|+|\bar{C}^{(i^{\prime})}_{{\rm new},j}|)

\displaystyle\leq\kappa_{j}\cdot h_{j}\cdot 2^{O(t\cdot\lambda_{\mathrm{insLM},t})}\cdot(\phi\cdot E(G^{(0)})+|T^{(0)}|)+\kappa_{j}\cdot\chi\cdot h_{j}\cdot 2^{O(t\cdot\lambda_{\mathrm{insLM},t})}\cdot\sum_{1\leq i^{\prime}\leq i}|\pi^{(i^{\prime})}|

\displaystyle\leq\kappa_{j}\cdot\chi\cdot h_{j}\cdot 2^{O(t\cdot\lambda_{\mathrm{insLM},t})}\cdot(|T^{(0)}|+\phi\cdot\sum_{0\leq i^{\prime}\leq i}|E(G^{(i^{\prime})})|)

\displaystyle\leq\kappa_{j}\cdot\chi\cdot t\cdot h_{j}\cdot 2^{O(t\cdot\lambda_{\mathrm{insLM},t})}\cdot(|T^{(i)}|+\phi\cdot E(G^{(i)})).

Here, the third inequality is by
|\pi^{(i^{\prime})}|\leq\phi\cdot|G^{(i^{\prime})}|
for all
1\leq i^{\prime}\leq t
. The last inequality is because
|G^{(i^{\prime})}|
is almost unchanged during the time period
0\leq i^{\prime}\leq i
. Precisely, for each
0\leq i^{\prime}\leq i-1
, we have
|G^{(i^{\prime})}|\leq|G^{(i^{\prime}+1)}|+|\pi^{(i^{\prime}+1)}|\leq(1+\phi)\cdot|G^{(i^{\prime}+1)}|
, which implies
|G^{(i^{\prime})}|\leq(1+\phi)^{i-i^{\prime}}\cdot|G^{(i)}|\leq O(|G^{(i)}|)
because we require that
\phi\leq 1/t
in the statement of
Theorem

8.2
.

By the same argument, the number of landmarks in
\bar{L}^{(i)}_{j}
is given by

\displaystyle|\bar{L}^{(i)}_{j}|

\displaystyle\leq|\bar{L}^{(0)}_{j}|+\sum_{1\leq i^{\prime}\leq i}|L^{(i)}_{{\rm new},j}|+|\bar{L}^{(i)}_{{\rm new},j}|

\displaystyle\leq O(\kappa_{j}\cdot(\phi\cdot|E(G^{(0)})|+|T^{(0)}|))+O(\kappa_{j}\cdot\chi\cdot\sum_{1\leq i^{\prime}\leq i}|\pi^{(i^{\prime})}|)

\displaystyle\leq O(\kappa_{j}\cdot\chi\cdot t\cdot(|T^{(i)}|+\phi\cdot|E(G^{(i)})|)).

Now we are ready to bound
|V(H^{(i)})|
and
|H^{(i)}|
. By
Lemma

8.11
, we have

|V(H^{(i)})|=O(\sum_{j}\bar{\omega}^{(i)}_{j}\cdot|\bar{T}^{(i)}|)\leq O(\kappa_{j}\cdot t\cdot\lambda_{\mathrm{insLM},t}\cdot\kappa_{\mathrm{NC},\omega}\cdot|V(G^{(i)})|)\leq n^{O(\epsilon^{4})}\cdot|V(G^{(i)})|

because
|\bar{T}^{(i)}|
has a trivial upper bound
|V(G^{(i)})|
(since
\bar{T}^{(i)}\subseteq V(G^{(i)})
).
Regarding the size of
H^{(i)}
, we have

\displaystyle|H^{(i)}|

\displaystyle=O(\sum_{j}\bar{\omega}^{(i)}_{j}\cdot|\bar{T}^{(i)}|+t\cdot\sum_{j}|\bar{C}^{(i)}_{j}|/h_{j})

\displaystyle\leq O(\kappa_{j}\cdot t\cdot\lambda_{\mathrm{insLM},t}\cdot\kappa_{\mathrm{NC},\omega}\cdot(|T^{(i)}|+\sum_{j}|\bar{L}^{(i)}_{j}|)+t\cdot\sum_{j}|\bar{C}^{(i)}_{j}|/h_{j})

\displaystyle\leq\kappa_{j}^{3}\cdot\chi\cdot\kappa_{\mathrm{NC},\omega}\cdot 2^{O(t\cdot\lambda_{\mathrm{insLM},t})}\cdot(|T^{(i)}|+\phi\cdot|E(G^{(i)})|).

Recall that
\chi=O(\lambda_{\mathrm{rt},{\rm prune}}(t\cdot\lambda_{\mathrm{insLM},t})\cdot t^{4}\cdot\lambda_{\mathrm{insLM},t}^{2}\cdot\kappa_{\mathrm{NC},\omega}^{2}\cdot(\kappa_{\mathrm{CM},L}+\kappa_{{\rm init},L})\cdot(\kappa_{\mathrm{CM},\gamma}+\kappa_{{\rm init},\gamma}))
.

The Recourse from
H^{(i-1)}
to
H^{(i)}

. We first bound the recourse from
\bar{\cal N}_{j}^{(i-1)}
to
\bar{\cal N}_{j}^{(i)}
restricted on
\bar{T}^{(i)}
. Trivially, we have

\mathrm{Rcs}_{\mid\mathds{1}(\bar{T}^{(i)})}(\bar{\cal N}_{j}^{(i-1)}\to\bar{\cal N}_{j}^{(i)})\leq\mathrm{Rcs}_{\mid\mathds{1}(\bar{T}^{(i)})}(\bar{\cal N}^{(i-1)}_{j}\to{\cal N}^{(i)}_{j})+\mathrm{Rcs}_{\mid\mathds{1}(\bar{T}^{(i)})}({\cal N}^{(i)}_{j}\to\bar{\cal N}^{(i)}_{j}),

and then we bound the two terms on the right hand side respectively. Recall that
\bar{T}^{(i)}=T^{(i)}\cup\bigcup_{j}\bar{L}^{(i)}_{j}
. The key point is that virtual nodes corresponding to terminals and landmarks have density
\Omega(1/\phi)
at all time, so roughly speaking, the recourse of pairwise covers will drop by a factor
\Omega(1/\phi)
when restricting on
\mathds{1}(\bar{T}^{(i)})
. Precisely, we have

\min_{v\in\mathds{1}(\bar{T}^{(i)})\cap A^{(i)}}\rho^{(i)}(v)\geq\Omega(1/\phi)\text{ and }\min_{v\in\mathds{1}(\bar{T}^{(i)})\cap\bar{A}^{(i)}}\bar{\rho}^{(i)}(v)\geq\Omega(1/\phi).

Therefore, by
Theorem

7.8
(since the batched edge deletion is the bottleneck),

\displaystyle\mathrm{Rcs}_{\mid\mathds{1}(\bar{T}^{(i)})}(\bar{\cal N}^{(i-1)}_{j}\to{\cal N}^{(i)}_{j})

\displaystyle\leq O(\bar{\omega}^{(i-1)}_{j}\cdot\kappa_{\mathrm{NC},\omega}\cdot\mu\cdot\lambda_{\mathrm{rt},{\rm prune}}(\bar{f}^{(i-1)}_{j})\cdot\bar{\gamma}_{{\rm emb},j}^{(i-1)}\cdot|\pi^{(i)}|/\Omega(1/\phi))

\displaystyle\leq O(t^{3}\cdot\lambda_{\mathrm{insLM},t}\cdot\kappa^{2}_{\mathrm{NC},\omega}\cdot\lambda_{\mathrm{rt},{\rm prune}}(t\cdot\lambda_{\mathrm{insLM},t})\cdot\Gamma\cdot|\pi^{(i)}|).

where we use
\bar{\omega}^{(i-1)}_{j}=O(t\cdot\lambda_{\mathrm{insLM},t}\cdot\kappa_{\mathrm{NC},\omega})
,
\bar{f}^{(i-1)}_{j}=O(t\cdot\lambda_{\mathrm{insLM},t})
and
\bar{\gamma}^{(i)}_{{\rm emb},j}\leq O(t\cdot\Gamma/\phi)
.
Also, by
Lemma

7.11
,

\displaystyle\mathrm{Rcs}_{\mid\mathds{1}(\bar{T}^{(i)})}({\cal N}^{(i)}_{j}\to\bar{\cal N}^{(i)}_{j})

\displaystyle\leq O(\tau^{(i)}\cdot\bar{\omega}^{(i)}_{j}\cdot\kappa_{\mathrm{NC},\omega}/(\phi\cdot\Omega(1/\phi)))

\displaystyle\leq O(\kappa_{j}\cdot\chi\cdot t\cdot\lambda_{\mathrm{insLM},t}\cdot\kappa_{\mathrm{NC},\omega}^{2}\cdot|\pi^{(i)}|).

where the second inequality uses
\tau^{(i)}\leq O(\kappa_{j}\cdot\chi\cdot|\pi^{(i)}|)
.

Now we are ready to show the recourse from
H^{(i-1)}
to
H^{(i)}
. By
Lemma

8.11
,

\displaystyle~~~~\mathrm{Rcs}(H^{(i-1)}\to H^{(i)})

\displaystyle\leq O(|\pi^{(i)}|+\sum_{j}(t\cdot(|C^{(i)}_{{\rm new},j}|+|\bar{C}^{(i)}_{{\rm new},j}|)/h_{j}+\mathrm{Rcs}(\bar{\cal N}_{\mathrm{tmn},j}^{(i-1)}\to\bar{\cal N}_{\mathrm{tmn},j}^{(i)})))

\displaystyle\leq O(|\pi^{(i)}|+\sum_{j}(t\cdot(|C^{(i)}_{{\rm new},j}|+|\bar{C}^{(i)}_{{\rm new},j}|)/h_{j}+\bar{\omega}^{(i-1)}_{j}\cdot\mathrm{Rcs}(\bar{T}^{(i-1)}\to\bar{T}^{(i)})+\mathrm{Rcs}_{\mid\mathds{1}(\bar{T}^{(i)})}(\bar{\cal N}_{j}^{(i-1)}\to\bar{\cal N}_{j}^{(i)})))

\displaystyle\leq O(|\pi^{(i)}|+\sum_{j}(\kappa_{j}\cdot\chi\cdot 2^{O(t\cdot\lambda_{\mathrm{insLM},t})}\cdot|\pi^{(i)}|+t\cdot\lambda_{\mathrm{insLM},t}\cdot\kappa_{\mathrm{NC},\omega}\cdot\kappa^{2}_{j}\cdot\chi\cdot|\pi^{(i)}|

\displaystyle~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~+t^{3}\cdot\lambda_{\mathrm{insLM},t}\cdot\kappa^{2}_{\mathrm{NC},\omega}\cdot\lambda_{\mathrm{rt},{\rm prune}}(t\cdot\lambda_{\mathrm{insLM},t})\cdot\Gamma\cdot|\pi^{(i)}|

\displaystyle~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~+\kappa_{j}\cdot\chi\cdot t\cdot\lambda_{\mathrm{insLM},t}\cdot\kappa_{\mathrm{NC},\omega}^{2}\cdot|\pi^{(i)}|))

\displaystyle\leq O(\kappa_{j}^{3}\cdot 2^{O(t\cdot\lambda_{\mathrm{insLM},t})}\cdot\lambda_{\mathrm{rt},{\rm prune}}(t\cdot\lambda_{\mathrm{insLM},t})\cdot\kappa_{\mathrm{NC},\omega}^{4}\cdot(\kappa_{\mathrm{CM},L}+\kappa_{{\rm init},L})\cdot(\kappa_{\mathrm{CM},\gamma}+\kappa_{{\rm init},\gamma})\cdot\kappa_{\mathrm{insLM},\gamma}\cdot|\pi^{(i)}|)

Here the second inequality is because, to update
(\bar{\cal N}^{(i-1)}_{j})_{\mid\bar{T}^{(i-1)}}
to
(\bar{\cal N}^{(i)}_{j})_{\mid\bar{T}^{(i)}}
, we will first update
(\bar{\cal N}^{(i-1)}_{j})_{\mid\bar{T}^{(i-1)}}
to
(\bar{\cal N}^{(i-1)}_{j})_{\mid\bar{T}^{(i)}}
by adding vertices in
\bar{T}^{(i)}\setminus\bar{T}^{(i-1)}
, each of which takes
\bar{\omega}_{j}^{(i-1)}
virtual node insertions to the pairwise cover. The third inequality is because
\mathrm{Rcs}(\bar{T}^{(i-1)}\to\bar{T}^{(i)})\leq|\pi^{(i)}|+\sum_{j}(|L^{(i)}_{{\rm new},j}|+\bar{L}^{(i)}_{{\rm new},j})\leq O(\kappa^{2}_{j}\cdot\chi\cdot|\pi^{(i)}|)
and
\bar{\omega}_{j}^{(i-1)}=O(t\cdot\lambda_{\mathrm{insLM},t}\cdot\kappa_{\mathrm{NC},\omega})
. We obtain the last inequality by plugging in the bounds of
\chi
and
\Gamma
.

The Running Time.

The Initialization Step.
At the initialization step with
i=0
, summing over the initialization of dense certified-EDs (
Lemma

7.4
), the landmark insertion (
Lemma

7.11
) and the initialization step of
Lemma

8.11
, the initialization time is

\displaystyle~~~~\sum_{j}(|G^{(0)}|+\rho^{(0)}(A^{(0)}))\cdot\mathrm{poly}(h_{j}^{(0)})\cdot n^{O(\epsilon)}

\displaystyle+\sum_{j}(\tau^{(0)}/\phi)\cdot(\bar{\omega}^{(0)}_{j})^{2}\cdot\mathrm{poly}(h_{j}^{(0)})\cdot(1/\phi+2^{O(\bar{f}^{(0)}_{j})})

\displaystyle+\tilde{O}(\sum_{j}\bar{\omega}^{(0)}_{j}\cdot|\bar{T}^{(0)}|+|\bar{C}_{j}^{(0)}|)

\displaystyle=(|G^{(0)}|+|T^{(0)}|/\phi)\cdot\mathrm{poly}(h)\cdot n^{O(\epsilon)}/\phi

Update Steps.
For a update step
i\geq 1
, the time to obtain all
(C^{(i)}_{j},L^{(i)}_{j},{\cal N}^{(i)}_{j},{\cal R}^{(i)}_{j},\Pi_{{\cal R}^{(i)}_{j}\to G^{(i)}})
is at most (the bottleneck is when
\pi^{(i)}
represents batched edge deletions)

\displaystyle~~~~\sum_{j}(\bar{\omega}^{(i-1)}_{j}\cdot\lambda\cdot\lambda_{\mathrm{rt},{\rm prune}}(\bar{f}^{(i-1)}_{j})\cdot\bar{\gamma}_{{\rm emb},j}^{(i-1)}\cdot|\pi^{(i)}|)\cdot\mathrm{poly}(\bar{h}^{(i-1)}_{j})\cdot n^{O(\epsilon)}\cdot(1/\phi^{(i)}_{j}+2^{O(\bar{f}^{(i-1)}_{j})}\cdot\bar{\omega}^{(i-1)}_{j}).

The time to compute
(\bar{C}^{(i)}_{j},\bar{L}^{(i)}_{j},{\cal N}^{(i)}_{j},{\cal R}^{(i)}_{j},\Pi_{{\cal R}^{(i)}_{j}\to G^{(i)}})
is, by
Lemma

7.11
,

\displaystyle~~~~\sum_{j}(\tau^{(i)}/\phi)\cdot(\bar{\omega}_{j}^{(i-1)})^{2}\cdot\mathrm{poly}(h_{j})\cdot n^{O(\epsilon)}\cdot(1/\phi+2^{O(\bar{f}_{j}^{(i-1)})}).

The time to update
H^{(i)}
to
H^{(i+1)}
is, by
Lemma

8.11
,

\displaystyle~~~~\tilde{O}(\sum_{j}(|C^{(i)}_{{\rm new},j}|+|\bar{C}^{(i)}_{{\rm new},j}|)+\mathrm{Rcs}(\bar{\cal N}_{\mathrm{tmn},j}^{(i-1)}\to\bar{\cal N}_{\mathrm{tmn},j}^{(i)}))

Summing over these three steps, the total update time is
|\pi^{(i)}|\cdot\mathrm{poly}(h)\cdot n^{O(\epsilon)}/\phi^{2}
.

Path Unfolding.
At any time
i
, given a path
P_{H}
in
H
connecting some
u,v\in\bar{T}
, we construct a
u
-
v
path
P_{G}
in
G
as follows.

From the proof of
Lemma

8.11
, we know
H=H^{\star,(i)}\cup E_{\mathrm{heavy}}
. Here
H^{\star}
is the union of stars
H^{\star}_{S_{\mathrm{tmn}}}
of those clusters
S_{\mathrm{tmn}}\in\bigcup_{j}\bar{\cal N}_{\mathrm{tmn},j}
, and
E_{\mathrm{heavy}}\subseteq E(G)
are some original edges. Therefore, we can decompose
P_{H}
into original edges and
star subpaths
, where a star subpath has exactly two edges from the same star.

Consider a star subpath
P^{\prime}_{H}
. Let
H^{\star}_{S_{\mathrm{tmn}}}
(where
S_{\mathrm{tmn}}\in\bar{\cal N}_{\mathrm{tmn},j}
for some
j
) be the star containing
P^{\prime}_{H}
, and let
u^{\prime},v^{\prime}\in S_{\mathrm{tmn}}
denote the endpoints of
P^{\prime}_{H}
. For now we assume
u^{\prime},v^{\prime}
are two different vertices, and we will discuss the case
u^{\prime}=v^{\prime}
later. We have
\ell_{H}(P^{\prime}_{H})=2h_{j}
because the star of a level-
j
cluster has edge length
h_{j}
(see the definition of stars in the proof of
Lemma

8.3
). We now construct a
u^{\prime}
-
v^{\prime}
path
P^{\prime}_{G}
on
G
. Note that there is a cluster
S\in\bar{\cal N}_{j}
s.t.
S_{\mathrm{tmn}}\subseteq S
(recall that
\bar{\cal N}_{\mathrm{tmn},j}
is the restriction of
\bar{\cal N}_{j}
on
\mathds{1}(\bar{T})
). Let
R\in\bar{\cal R}_{j}
be the router corresponding to
S
, and let
u^{\prime}_{\mathrm{vtul}}
and
v^{\prime}_{\mathrm{vtul}}\in S\subseteq V(R)
be arbitrary virtual nodes of
u^{\prime}
and
v^{\prime}
respectively. By
Theorem

5.1
, we can obtain a
u^{\prime}_{\mathrm{vtul}}
-
v^{\prime}_{\mathrm{vtul}}
path
P^{\prime}_{R}
on
R
with
|P^{\prime}_{R}|\leq\lambda_{\mathrm{rt},h}(\bar{f})
in
O(|P^{\prime}_{R}|)
time. In the embedding
\Pi_{\bar{R}_{j}\to G}
, each edge
e=(x_{\mathrm{vtul}},y_{\mathrm{vtul}})\in P^{\prime}_{R}
(where
x_{\mathrm{vtul}},y_{\mathrm{vtul}}\in S
are endpoints of
e
) is mapped into a path
P^{\prime}_{G,e}
on
G
connecting vertices
x_{\mathrm{vtx}}
and
y_{\mathrm{vtx}}
(where
x_{\mathrm{vtx}},y_{\mathrm{vtx}}
are the original vertex corresponding to virtual nodes
x_{\mathrm{vtul}},y_{\mathrm{vtul}}
) with length
\ell_{G}(P^{\prime}_{G,e})\leq\bar{h}_{{\rm emb},j}
. We construct
P^{\prime}_{G}
by concatenating of these
P^{\prime}_{G,e}
according to the order of edges
e
on
P^{\prime}_{R}
. Obviously,
P^{\prime}_{G}
is a
u^{\prime}
-
v^{\prime}
path on
G
, and we have

\ell_{G}(P^{\prime}_{G})\leq|P^{\prime}_{R}|\cdot\bar{h}_{{\rm emb},j}\leq\lambda_{\mathrm{rt},h}(t\cdot\lambda_{\mathrm{insLM},t})\cdot\lambda_{{\rm init},h}\cdot 2^{O(t\cdot\lambda_{\mathrm{insLM}},t)}\cdot h_{j}.

Because
u^{\prime}
and
v^{\prime}
are two different vertices, trivially we know
P^{\prime}_{G}
has at least one edge, i.e.
|P^{\prime}_{G}|\geq 1
.

In the case that the endpoints
u^{\prime},v^{\prime}\in S_{\mathrm{tmn}}
of
P^{\prime}_{H}
are the same (this may happen because
P^{\prime}_{H}
may not be a simple path). The reason why this case is special is that the above construction of
P^{\prime}_{G}
may give a
P^{\prime}_{G}
with no edge. This can be simply fixed as follows. Because the star
H^{\star}_{S_{\mathrm{tmn}}}
has at least one edge, we have
|S_{\mathrm{tmn}}|\geq 2
(otherwise
H^{\star}_{S_{\mathrm{tmn}}}
will degenerate). we let
w^{\prime}
be an arbitrary vertex in
S_{\mathrm{tmn}}
other than
u^{\prime}
. We construct
P^{\prime\prime}_{G}
for the vertex pair
(u^{\prime},w^{\prime})
as above, and then let the real
P^{\prime}_{G}
be the concatenation of the forward
P^{\prime\prime}_{G}
(from
u^{\prime}
to
w^{\prime}
) and the backward
P^{\prime}_{G}
(from
w^{\prime}
to
u^{\prime}
). Now
\ell_{G}(P^{\prime}_{G})\leq|P^{\prime}_{R}|\cdot\bar{h}_{{\rm emb},j}\leq 2\cdot\lambda_{\mathrm{rt},h}(t\cdot\lambda_{\mathrm{insLM},t})\cdot\lambda_{{\rm init},h}\cdot 2^{O(t\cdot\lambda_{\mathrm{insLM}},t)}\cdot h_{j}
and
|P^{\prime}_{G}|\geq 2
.

Finally, we obtain
P_{G}
from
P_{H}
by substituting each star subpath
P^{\prime}_{H}
on
P_{H}
with
P^{\prime}_{G}
. We have
\ell_{G}(P_{G})\leq\lambda_{\mathrm{rt},h}(t\cdot\lambda_{\mathrm{insLM},t})\cdot\lambda_{{\rm init},h}\cdot 2^{O(t\cdot\lambda_{\mathrm{insLM}},t)}\cdot\ell_{H}(P_{H})
because we did not change the original edges on
P_{H}
, and each star subpath
P^{\prime}_{H}
with length
\ell_{H}(P^{\prime}_{H})=2h_{j}
is replaced by
P^{\prime}_{G}
with length
\ell_{G}(P^{\prime}_{G})\leq 2\cdot\lambda_{\mathrm{rt},h}(t\cdot\lambda_{\mathrm{insLM},t})\cdot\lambda_{{\rm init},h}\cdot 2^{O(t\cdot\lambda_{\mathrm{insLM}},t)}\cdot h_{j}
. Analogously, we can show
|P_{G}|\geq|P_{H}|/2
.

The path-unfolding time is
O(|P_{H}|\cdot\lambda_{\mathrm{rt},h}(t\cdot\lambda_{\mathrm{insLM},t})/\epsilon^{4})+O(|P_{G}|)=2^{O(1/\epsilon^{4})}\cdot|P_{G}|
. There is a term
O(|P_{H}|\cdot\lambda_{\mathrm{rt},h}(t\cdot\lambda_{\mathrm{insLM},t})/\epsilon^{4})
because we will invoke the path-reporting algorithm in
Theorem

5.1

O(|P_{H}|)
times, each time it return some router path
P^{\prime}
with
|P^{\prime}|\leq\lambda_{\mathrm{rt},h}
in time
O(|P^{\prime}|/\epsilon^{4})
.

∎

9
Dynamic Length-Constrained Expander Hierarchy

The notion of
expander hierarchy
was first introduced by
[
GRST21
]
with applications on maintaining tree-flow sparsifiers and congestion-competitive oblivious routing scheme. Later, in
[
HRG22
]
, by developing the concept of lengh-constrained expander, they further introduced
length-constrained expander hierarchy
and applied it on distributed oblivious routing with routing paths under certain length constraint.

This section shows a dynamic algorithm for the length-constrained expander hierarchy. Roughly speaking, the algorithm will invoke dynamic certified-EDs and dynamic vertex sparsifiers on top of each other alternatively. This hierarchy will be used in both
Section

11
and
Section

10
but with different input length parameters.

We note that in the statement of
Theorem

9.1
, we use the global vertex identifiers (see the discussion at the beginning of
Section

8
) to formally define the set intersection/union operations involving vertices from different vertex sparsifiers. Furthermore, we manually set an upper bound
\phi<o(1/(t\cdot\kappa_{H,\mathrm{size}}))
just for simplifying the calculation.

9.1
Expander Hierarchy.

Let
G
be a dynamic graph under
t
batched updates
\pi^{(i)},...,\pi^{(t)}
of edge insertions/deletions and isolated vertex insertions/deletions. Given parameters
\phi\leq o(1/(t\cdot\kappa_{H,\mathrm{size}}))
and
\bar{k}\leq\lambda_{k}
, where
\lambda_{k}=O(1/\epsilon^{2})
is a sufficiently large global parameter, there is an algorithm that maintains an expander hierarchy
({\cal H},{\cal D})
with
\bar{k}
levels, where
{\cal H}
is a collection of vertex sparsifiers
\{H_{k}\mid 1\leq k\leq\bar{k}\}
, and
{\cal D}
is a collection of certified-EDs
(C_{k},L_{k},{\cal N}_{k},{\cal R}_{k},\Pi_{{\cal R}_{k}\to H_{k}})
of some node-weighting
A_{k}
on
H_{k}
. For each level
1\leq k\leq\bar{k}
, let
h_{k}
and
\bar{h}_{k}
be additional given length parameters. The vertex sparsifiers satisfy that
H_{1}=G
and for each
1\leq k\leq\bar{k}-1
,

•

H_{k+1}
is a
(\lambda_{H,\mathrm{low}},\lambda_{H,\mathrm{up}},\bar{h}_{k})
-sparsifier of
V(H_{k+1})\cap V(H_{k})
on
H_{k}
s.t.
L_{k}\subseteq V(H_{k+1})\cap V(H_{k})
.

•

The size of
H_{k+1}
is
|H_{k+1}|\leq O(t\cdot\kappa_{H,\mathrm{size}}\cdot\phi\cdot|H_{k}|)
, and the number of vertices in
H_{k+1}
is
|V(H_{k+1})|\leq n^{O(\epsilon^{4})}\cdot|V(H_{k})|
.

•

V(H_{k})\cap V(H_{k+1})=V(H_{k})\cap\bigcup_{k+1\leq k^{\prime}\leq\bar{k}}V(H_{k^{\prime}})
.

•

Given a path
P_{H_{k+1}}
on
H_{k+1}
connecting some vertices
u,v\in V(H_{k+1})\cap V(H_{k})
, the algorithm can compute a
u
-
v
path
P_{H_{k}}
on
H_{k}
with
\ell_{H_{k}}(P_{H_{k}})\leq\lambda_{H,\mathrm{low}}\cdot\ell_{H_{k+1}}(P_{H_{k+1}})
and
|P_{H_{k}}|\geq|P_{H_{k+1}}|/2
. The path-reporting time is
|P_{H_{k}}|\cdot 2^{O(1/\epsilon^{4})}
.

For each level
1\leq k\leq\bar{k}
, the certified-ED satisfies the following.

•

A_{k}\geq\deg_{H_{k}}+\mathds{1}(V(H_{k}))
.

•

|C_{k}|\leq O(t\cdot\lambda_{\mathrm{dynED},h}\cdot h_{k}\cdot\phi\cdot|H_{k}|)
.

•

L_{k}
has size
|L_{k}|\leq O(t\cdot\phi\cdot|H_{k}|)
and distrotion
\sigma_{k}=O(\lambda_{\mathrm{dynED},h}\cdot h_{k}/\kappa_{\sigma})
.

•

{\cal N}_{k}
is a
b_{k}
-distributed
(h_{{\rm cov},k},h_{\mathrm{sep},k},\omega_{k})
-pairwise cover of
A_{k}
on
H_{k}-C_{k}
with

b_{k}=O(t),\ h_{{\rm cov},k}=h_{\mathrm{sep},k}=h_{k},\ \omega_{k}=O(t\cdot\kappa_{\mathrm{NC},\omega})

•

Each router in
{\cal R}_{k}
is maintained by
Theorem

5.1
under at most
f_{k}=O(t)
batched updates.

•

\Pi_{{\cal R}_{k}\to H_{k}}
has
h_{{\rm emb},k}=O(\lambda_{{\rm init},h}\cdot\lambda_{\mathrm{dynED},h}\cdot h_{k})
and
\gamma_{{\rm emb},k}=O(\kappa_{\mathrm{dynED},\gamma}/\phi)
.

•

At each time
1\leq i\leq t
, we have

\displaystyle\sum_{1\leq k\leq\bar{k}}\mathrm{Rcs}({\cal N}^{(i-1)}_{k}\to{\cal N}^{(i)}_{k})

\displaystyle\leq\kappa_{\mathrm{EH},\mathrm{NC},\mathrm{rcs}}\cdot|\pi^{(i)}|/\phi,

where
\kappa_{\mathrm{EH},\mathrm{NC},\mathrm{rcs}}=\lambda_{\mathrm{rt},{\rm prune}}(t)\cdot t\cdot\kappa_{\mathrm{NC},\omega}\cdot\kappa_{\mathrm{dynED},\gamma}\cdot 2^{O(\lambda_{k})}\cdot\kappa^{\lambda_{k}}_{H,\mathrm{rcs}}=n^{O(\epsilon^{2})}
.

The initialization time is
|G^{(0)}|\cdot\mathrm{poly}(\max_{k}\{h_{k},\bar{h}_{k}\})\cdot n^{O(\epsilon)}/\phi
,
and the update time for batch
\pi^{(i)}
is
|\pi^{(i)}|\cdot\mathrm{poly}(\max_{k}\{h_{k},\bar{h}_{k}\})\cdot n^{O(\epsilon)}/\phi^{2}
.

Proof.

The Algorithm for Certifie-EDs.

The certified-EDs are maintained by
Theorem

6.4
. Precisely, at each level
k
, we will work on graph
H_{k}
under some batched updates
\pi^{(1)}_{k},...,\pi^{(t)}_{k}
. Initially,
H_{1}=G
and
\pi^{(1)}_{1}=\pi^{(1)},...,\pi^{(t)}_{1}=\pi^{(t)}
. The working graph and batched updates for
k\geq 2
will be specified later.

By applying
Theorem

6.4
on
H_{k}
with length parameter
h_{k}
and congestion parameter
\phi
, we can initialize and maintain a certified-ED
(C_{k},L_{k},{\cal N}_{k},{\cal R}_{k},\Pi_{{\cal R}_{k}\to H_{k}})
of
A_{k}
on
H_{k}
satisfying the following.

•

The Node-Weighting
A^{(i)}_{k}
.
At any time
i\geq 0
,

A_{k}^{(i)}=\deg_{H^{(i)}_{k}}+\mathds{1}(V(H^{(i)}_{k})).

•

The Moving Cut
C^{(i)}_{k}
.
Initially,

|C^{(0)}_{k}|=O(\lambda_{\mathrm{dynED},h}\cdot\phi\cdot|A^{(0)}_{k}|\cdot h_{k})=O(\lambda_{\mathrm{dynED},h}\cdot h_{k}\cdot\phi\cdot|H^{(0)}_{k}|).

For each time
i\geq 1
,
C^{(i)}_{k}=(C^{(i-1)}_{k})_{\mid E(H^{(i)}_{k})}+C^{(i)}_{{\rm new},k}
where

|C^{(i)}_{{\rm new},k}|=O(\lambda_{\mathrm{dynED},h}\cdot h_{k}\cdot|\pi^{(i)}_{k}|).

•

The Landmark Set
L^{(i)}_{k}
.
Initially,

|L^{(0)}_{k}|=O(\phi\cdot|A^{(0)}_{k}|)=O(\phi\cdot|H^{(0)}_{k}|).

For each time
i\geq 1
,
L^{(i)}_{k}=(L^{(i-1)}_{k}\cap V(H^{(i)}_{k}))\cup L^{(i)}_{{\rm new},k}
where

|L^{(i)}_{{\rm new},k}|=O(|\pi^{(i)}_{k}|).

•

The distortion of
L^{(i)}_{k}
, the distributed neighborhood cover
{\cal N}^{(i)}_{k}
, the routers
{\cal R}^{(i)}_{k}
and the embedding
\Pi_{{\cal R}^{(i)}_{k}\to H^{(i)}_{k}}
have quality parameters
\sigma_{k}
,
h_{\mathrm{trace},k}
,
\gamma_{\mathrm{trace},k}
,
b_{k}
,
h_{{\rm cov},k}
,
h_{\mathrm{sep},k}
,
\omega_{k}
,
f_{k}
,
h_{{\rm emb},k}
and
\gamma_{{\rm emb},k}
as shown in the theorem statement and we will not repeat them here.

The Reinitialization Condition.
At each time
i\geq 1
, we will reinitialize
Theorem

6.4
at this level
k
if either

•

the dynamic certified-ED algorithm (
Theorem

6.4
) at level
k-1
is reinitialized at time
i
, or

•

|\pi^{(i)}_{k}|\geq\phi\cdot|H^{(i)}_{k}|
.

With the reinitialization steps, we can claim additional properties of
(C_{k}^{(i)},L^{(i)}_{k},{\cal N}^{(i)}_{k},{\cal R}^{(i)}_{k},\Pi_{{\cal R}^{(i)}_{k}\to H^{(i)}_{k}})
at each time
i\geq 1
. If
Theorem

6.4
is not reinitialized at level
k
, we know
|\pi^{(i)}_{k}|\leq\phi\cdot|H^{(i)}_{k}|
, so we have

|C^{(i)}_{{\rm new},k}|=O(\lambda_{\mathrm{dynED},h}\cdot h_{k}\cdot|\pi^{(i)}_{k}|)\leq O(\lambda_{\mathrm{dynED},h}\cdot h_{k}\cdot\phi\cdot|H^{(i)}_{k}|)

and

|L^{(i)}_{{\rm new},k}|=O(|\pi^{(i)}_{k}|)\leq O(\phi\cdot|H^{(i)}_{k}|).

Therefore, at any time
i\geq 0
, let
i^{\star}_{k}
be the most recent reinitialization step of
Theorem

6.4
at level
k
, we can claim the following upper bounds on
|C^{(i)}_{k}|
and
|L^{(i)}_{k}|
:

|C^{(i)}_{k}|\leq O(\lambda_{\mathrm{dynED},h}\cdot h_{k}\cdot\phi\cdot\sum_{i^{\star}_{k}\leq i^{\prime}\leq i}|H^{(i^{\prime})}_{k}|)\leq O(t\cdot\lambda_{\mathrm{dynED},h}\cdot h_{k}\cdot\phi\cdot|H^{(i)}_{k}|),

and

|L^{(i)}_{k}|\leq O(\phi\cdot\sum_{i^{\star}_{k}\leq i^{\prime}\leq i}|H^{(i^{\prime})}_{k}|)\leq O(t\cdot\phi\cdot|H^{(i)}_{k}|).

Note that for each
i^{\star}_{k}\leq i^{\prime}\leq i-1
,
|H^{(i^{\prime})}_{k}|\leq|H^{(i^{\prime}+1)}_{k}|+|\pi^{(i^{\prime}+1)}_{k}|\leq(1+\phi)\cdot|H^{(i^{\prime}+1)}_{k}|
, so
|H^{(i^{\prime})}_{k}|\leq(1+\phi)^{i-i^{\prime}}|H^{(i)}_{k}|\leq O(|H^{(i)}_{k}|)
because the parameter
\phi
is required to be at most
1/t
.

The Algorithm for Sparsifiers.

Given the certified-ED
(C_{k},L_{k},{\cal N}_{k},{\cal R}_{k},\Pi_{{\cal R}_{k}\to H_{k}})
over time, we prepare the next-level graph
H_{k+1}^{(0)},...,H_{k+1}^{(t)}
and the update batches
\pi^{(1)}_{k+1},...,\pi^{(t)}_{k+1}
over time as follows.

We maintain
H_{k+1}
as a sparsifier of terminals
L_{k}
on
H_{k}
over time by applying
Theorem

8.2
with length parameter
\bar{h}_{k}
and congestion parameter
\phi^{\prime}=\phi/O(1)
, where
O(1)
represents some large constant. Moreover, at each time
i\geq 1
, the batched update
\bar{\pi}^{(i)}_{k}
fed to
Theorem

8.2
includes

•

the batched edge insertions/deletions and isolated vertex insertions/deletions from
H^{(i-1)}_{k}
to
H^{(i)}_{k}
, i.e.
\pi^{(i)}_{k}
, and

•

the terminal insertion from
L^{(i-1)}_{k}
and
L^{(i)}_{k}
, i.e.
L^{(i)}_{{\rm new},k}
,

if the dynamic certified-ED algorithm is
not
reinitialized at level
k
(we will discuss in a moment how to handle the opposite). Then
|\bar{\pi}^{(i)}_{k}|
has size

|\bar{\pi}^{(i)}_{k}|=|\pi^{(i)}_{k}|+|L^{(i)}_{{\rm new},k}|\leq O(|\pi^{(i)}_{k}|)=O(\phi\cdot|H^{(i)}_{k}|)\leq\phi^{\prime}\cdot|H^{(i)}_{k}|,

so it meets the requirements of
Theorem

8.2
.

Also,
H^{(i)}_{k+1}
is guaranteed to be a
(\lambda_{H,\mathrm{low}},\lambda_{H,\mathrm{up}},\bar{h}_{k})
-sparsifier of
V(H^{(i)}_{k})\cap V(H^{(i)}_{k+1})
on
H^{(i)}_{k}
s.t.
L^{(i)}_{k}\subseteq V(H^{(i)}_{k})\cap V(H^{(i)}_{k+1})
, and all vertices in
V(H^{(i)}_{k+1})\setminus V(H^{(i)}_{k})
are fresh. The number of vertices in

H^{(i)}_{k+1}
is
|V(H^{(i)}_{k+1})|\leq n^{O(\epsilon^{4})}\cdot|V(H^{(i)}_{k})|
. The size of
H^{(i)}_{k+1}
is

|H^{(i)}_{k+1}|\leq O(\kappa_{H,\mathrm{size}}\cdot(|L^{(i)}_{k}|+\phi\cdot|H^{(i)}_{k}|))\leq O(t\cdot\kappa_{H,\mathrm{size}}\cdot\phi\cdot|H^{(i)}_{k}|),

and
H^{(i)}_{k+1}
can be updated from
H^{(i-1)}_{k+1}
by a batched update
\pi^{(i)}_{k+1}
with size
|\pi^{(i)}_{k+1}|\leq\kappa_{H,\mathrm{rcs}}\cdot|\bar{\pi}^{(i)}_{k}|
.

9.2
.

For each
1\leq k\leq\bar{k}-1
, we have
V(H^{(i)}_{k})\cap V(H^{(i)}_{k+1})=V(H^{(i)}_{k})\cap\bigcup_{k+1\leq k^{\prime}\leq\bar{k}}V(H^{(i)}_{k^{\prime}})
.

Proof.

We first show that, for
0\leq i\leq t
and
1\leq k<k^{\prime}\leq\bar{k}
, the fresh vertices in
V(H^{(i)}_{k^{\prime}})
have global identifiers different from those of
V(H^{(i)}_{k})
, i.e.
V(H^{(i)}_{k^{\prime}})\setminus V(H^{(i)}_{k^{\prime}-1})
and
V(H^{(i)}_{k})
are disjoint. We will argue this inductively over time. Obviously, the statement holds at time
i=0
because we initialize the sparsifiers bottom-up (i.e. in the order
H^{(0)}_{1},...,H^{(0)}_{\bar{k}}
). Fix a time
i\geq 1
. We assume the statement holds at time
i-1
. Then each fresh vertices
v
in
V(H^{(i)}_{k^{\prime}})
is not in
V(H^{(i)}_{k})
by the following reason. If
v
is created at time
i
, then
v\notin V(H^{(i)}_{k})
by the definition of fresh vertices (all vertices in
V(H^{(i)}_{k})
are created before
v
by our algorithm). Otherwise,
v
is created before time
i
. By the assumption,
v\notin V(H^{(i-1)}_{k})
. It remains to show
v
is not in
V(H^{(i)}_{k})\setminus V(H^{(i-1)}_{k})
. Because we update the sparsifiers bottom-up, each vertex in
V(H^{(i)}_{k})\setminus V(H^{(i-1)}_{k})
is created without any knowledge about the global identifiers of
v
. In other words, each vertex in
V(H^{(i)}_{k})\setminus V(H^{(i-1)}_{k})
either is a fresh vertex or has the same identifier with someone in
\bigcup_{k^{\prime\prime}\leq k}V(H^{(i-1)}_{k^{\prime\prime}})
, but
v
is not in
V(H^{(i-1)}_{k^{\prime\prime}})
for all
k^{\prime\prime}\leq k
by the assumption. This implies
v\notin V(H^{(i)}_{k})\setminus V(H^{(i-1)}_{k})
.

Consider a fixed
k
. We now show
V(H^{(i)}_{k^{\prime}})\cap V(H^{(i)}_{k})\subseteq V(H^{(i)}_{k+1})
for each
k^{\prime}\geq k+1
by induction on
k^{\prime}
. When
k^{\prime}=k+1
, it trivially holds. When
k^{\prime}\geq k+2
, assume this statement holds for
k^{\prime}-1
. We have shown that the fresh vertices in
V(H^{(i)}_{k^{\prime}})
are not in
V(H^{(i)}_{k})
, so
V(H^{(i)}_{k^{\prime}})\cap V(H^{(i)}_{k})
is a subset of non-fresh vertices
V(H^{(i)}_{k^{\prime}})\cap V(H^{(i)}_{k^{\prime}-1})
, which implies
V(H^{(i)}_{k^{\prime}})\cap V(H^{(i)}_{k})\subseteq V(H^{(i)}_{k^{\prime}-1})\cap V(H^{(i)}_{k})\subseteq V(H^{(i)}_{k+1})
.

The original claim is a simple corrollary of the above statement.

∎

The Reinitialization Condition.
At time
i\geq 1
, we will reinitialize
Theorem

8.2
at this level
k
if the
Theorem

6.4
maintaining
(C_{k},L_{k},{\cal N}_{k},{\cal R}_{k},\Pi_{{\cal R}_{k}\to H_{k}})
triggers a reinitialization step at time
i
.

Size of
\pi^{(i)}_{k}
and
H^{(i)}_{k}
.

At time
i
, let
k^{\star}
be the minimum level at which the dynamic certified-ED algorithm is reintialized. Then we have for each
1\leq k\leq k^{\star}-1
,
|\bar{\pi}^{(i)}_{k}|\leq O(|\pi^{(i)}_{k}|)
and
|\pi^{(i)}_{k+1}|\leq\kappa_{H,\mathrm{rcs}}\cdot|\bar{\pi}^{(i)}_{k}|
. Therefore, for each
1\leq k\leq k^{\star}
,
|\pi^{(i)}_{k}|\leq O(\kappa_{H,\mathrm{rcs}})^{k-1}\cdot|\pi^{(i)}|
. For each
1\leq k\leq k^{\star}-1
,
|\bar{\pi}^{(i)}_{k}|\leq O(\kappa_{H,\mathrm{rcs}})^{k-1}\cdot|\pi^{(i)}|

For each
1\leq k\leq\bar{k}
, we have

|H^{(i)}_{k}|\leq O(t\cdot\kappa_{H,\mathrm{size}}\cdot\phi)^{k-1}\cdot|G^{(i)}|,

because
|H^{(i)}_{k+1}|\leq O(t\cdot\kappa_{H,\mathrm{size}}\cdot\phi\cdot|H^{(i)}_{k}|)
holds for each
1\leq k\leq\bar{k}-1
.

The Recourse of Pairwise Covers at Time
i
.

Let
k^{\star}
be the minimum level at which the dynamic certified-ED algorithm is reinitialized.

Then at each level
1\leq k\leq k^{\star}-1
, the dynamic certified-ED algorithm performs a normal update step at level
k
and time
i
,
by
Theorem

6.4
, we have

\displaystyle\mathrm{Rcs}({\cal N}^{(i-1)}_{k}\to{\cal N}^{(i)}_{k})

\displaystyle\leq\lambda_{\mathrm{rt},{\rm prune}}(t)\cdot t\cdot\kappa_{\mathrm{NC},\omega}\cdot\kappa_{\mathrm{dynED},\gamma}\cdot|\pi^{(i)}_{k}|/\phi

\displaystyle\leq\lambda_{\mathrm{rt},{\rm prune}}(t)\cdot t\cdot\kappa_{\mathrm{NC},\omega}\cdot\kappa_{\mathrm{dynED},\gamma}\cdot 2^{O(\bar{k})}\cdot\kappa_{H,\mathrm{rcs}}^{\bar{k}}\cdot|\pi^{(i)}|/\phi.

Now consider level
k^{\star}
. The dynamic certified-ED algorithm triggers a reinitialization step at level
k^{\star}
and time
i
. Note that by the reinitialization condition, we have
|\pi^{(i)}_{k^{\star}}|\geq\phi\cdot|H^{(i)}_{k^{\star}}|
,
which implies

|H^{(i)}_{k^{\star}}|\leq O(\kappa_{H,\mathrm{rcs}})^{k^{\star}-1}\cdot|\pi^{(i)}|/\phi.

Furthermore, by the definition of
k^{\star}
, the dynamic sparsifier algorithm at level
k^{\star}-1
that maintains
H_{k^{\star}}
performs a normal update step at time
i
, so we have

\displaystyle|H^{(i-1)}_{k^{\star}}|

\displaystyle\leq|H^{(i)}_{k^{\star}}|+|\pi^{(i)}_{k^{\star}}|\leq O(\kappa_{H,\mathrm{rcs}})^{k^{\star}-1}\cdot|\pi^{(i)}|/\phi.

Therefore, for each level
k\geq k^{\star}
,
\mathrm{Rcs}({\cal N}^{(i-1)}_{k}\to{\cal N}^{(i)}_{k})
can be bounded by

\displaystyle\mathrm{Rcs}({\cal N}^{(i-1)}_{k}\to{\cal N}^{(i)}_{k})

\displaystyle\leq\mathrm{size}({\cal N}^{(i-1)}_{k})+\mathrm{size}({\cal N}^{(i)}_{k})

\displaystyle\leq\omega^{(i-1)}_{k}\cdot|H^{(i-1)}_{k}|+\omega^{(i)}_{k}\cdot|H^{(i)}_{k}|

\displaystyle\leq\omega^{(i-1)}_{k}\cdot|H^{(i-1)}_{k^{\star}}|+\omega^{(i)}_{k}\cdot|H^{(i)}_{k^{\star}}|

\displaystyle\leq O(t\cdot\kappa_{\mathrm{NC},\omega})\cdot O(\kappa_{H,\mathrm{rcs}})^{k^{\star}-1}\cdot|\pi^{(i)}|/\phi

\displaystyle\leq 2^{O(\bar{k})}\cdot t\cdot\kappa_{\mathrm{NC},\omega}\cdot\kappa_{H,\mathrm{rcs}}^{\bar{k}}\cdot|\pi^{(i)}|/\phi.

Here the first inequality is because we can simply delete the whole
{\cal N}^{(i-1)}_{k}
and then add the whole
{\cal N}^{(i)}_{k}
.
The second inequality is by
\mathrm{size}({\cal N}^{(i-1)}_{k})\leq\omega^{(i-1)}_{k}\cdot|A^{(i-1)}_{k}|\leq\omega^{(i-1)}_{k}\cdot|H^{(i-1)}_{k}|
.
The third inequality is because we have
|H^{(i)}_{k+1}|\leq O(t\cdot\kappa_{H,\mathrm{size}}\cdot\phi\cdot|H^{(i)}_{k}|)\leq o(|H^{(i)}_{k}|)
for all
i
and
k
(here we use the condition
\phi\leq o(1/t\cdot\kappa_{H,\mathrm{size}})
).

In conclusion, we have

\displaystyle\sum_{1\leq k\leq\bar{k}}\mathrm{Rcs}({\cal N}^{(i-1)}_{k}\to{\cal N}^{(i)}_{k})

\displaystyle\leq\lambda_{\mathrm{rt},{\rm prune}}(t)\cdot t\cdot\kappa_{\mathrm{NC},\omega}\cdot\kappa_{\mathrm{dynED},\gamma}\cdot 2^{O(\lambda_{k})}\cdot\kappa^{\lambda_{k}}_{H,\mathrm{rcs}}\cdot|\pi^{(i)}|/\phi.

The Initialization and Update Time.

For the initialization, at level
k
, the dynamic certified-EDs algorithm takes time, by
Theorem

6.4
,

|A^{(0)}_{k}|\cdot\mathrm{poly}(h_{k})\cdot n^{O(\epsilon)}=|H^{(0)}_{k}|\cdot\mathrm{poly}(h_{k})\cdot n^{O(\epsilon)},

because
|A^{(0)}_{k}|=O(|H^{(0)}_{k}|)
. The dynamic sparsifier algorithm takes time, by
Theorem

8.2
,

\displaystyle(|H^{(0)}_{k}|+|L^{(0)}_{k}|/\phi)\cdot\mathrm{poly}(\bar{h}_{k})\cdot n^{O(\epsilon)}/\phi\leq|H^{(0)}_{k}|\cdot\mathrm{poly}(\bar{h}_{k})\cdot n^{O(\epsilon)}/\phi,

because
|L^{(0)}_{k}|\leq O(\phi\cdot|H^{(0)}_{k}|)
. The total initialization time summing over all levels is

|G^{(0)}|\cdot\mathrm{poly}(\max_{k}\{h_{k},\bar{h}_{k}\})\cdot n^{O(\epsilon)}/\phi.

About the update time for handling batch
\pi^{(i)}
, let
k^{\star}
be the minimum level at which the dynamic certified-ED algorithm is reinitialized. Then for each level
1\leq k\leq k^{\star}-1
, the dynamic certified-ED algorithm needs update time

|\pi^{(i)}_{k}|\cdot n^{O(\epsilon)}\cdot\mathrm{poly}(h_{k})/\phi
, and
the dynamic sparsifier algorithm needs update time

|\bar{\pi}^{(i)}_{k}|\cdot\mathrm{poly}(\bar{h}_{k})\cdot n^{O(\epsilon)}/\phi^{2}
.
For each level
k\geq k^{\star}
, the dynamic certified-ED and sparsifier algorithms are both reinitialized with running time

|H^{(i)}_{k}|\cdot\mathrm{poly}(\max\{h_{k},\bar{h}_{k}\})\cdot n^{O(\epsilon)}/\phi.

Finally, combining
|\pi^{(i)}_{k}|,|\bar{\pi}^{(i)}_{k}|\leq O(\kappa_{H,\mathrm{rcs}})^{\bar{k}}\cdot|\pi^{(i)}|
