for each
k\leq k^{\star}-1
and
|H^{(i)}_{k}|\leq o(|H^{(i)}_{k^{\star}}|)\leq|\pi^{(i)}_{k^{\star}}|/\phi
, the total update time is

|\pi^{(i)}|\cdot\mathrm{poly}(\max_{k}\{h_{k},\bar{h}_{k}\})\cdot n^{O(\epsilon)}/\phi^{2}.

∎

10
Dynamic Distance Oracles for the Low Distance Regime

Providing the dynamic length-constrained expander hierarchy, we are ready to design dynamic distance oracles. However, the oracles in this section only work for low distance because it has initialization time and update time depending on
\mathrm{poly}(h)
.

10.1
.

Let
G
be a dynamic graph under
t
batches of updates
\pi^{(1)},\pi^{(2)},...,\pi^{(t)}
of edge insertions/deletions and isolated vertex insertions/deletions. Given a parameter
h
, there is an algorithm that maintains an oracle
{\cal O}
s.t. for each
0\leq i\leq t
, the oracle
{\cal O}^{(i)}
supports the following query. Given two vertices
u,v\in V(G)
,
{\cal O}^{(i)}
will declare either
\mathrm{dist}_{G^{(i)}}(u,v)>h
or
\mathrm{dist}_{G^{(i)}}(u,v)\leq\lambda_{\mathrm{LowDO},\alpha}\cdot h
, where

\lambda_{\mathrm{LowDO},\alpha}=2^{O(\lambda_{k})}\cdot(\lambda_{H,\mathrm{low}}\cdot\lambda_{H,\mathrm{up}})^{\lambda_{k}}\cdot(\lambda_{\mathrm{rt},h}(t)\cdot\lambda_{{\rm init},h}\cdot\lambda_{\mathrm{dynED},h})^{\lambda_{k}}.

The initialization time is

|G^{(0)}|\cdot\mathrm{poly}(h)\cdot n^{O(\epsilon)}
, the update time to handle
\pi^{(i)}
is

|\pi^{(i)}|\cdot\mathrm{poly}(h)\cdot n^{O(\epsilon)}
, and the query time is
O(1/\epsilon^{3})
.

Furthermore, if
{\cal O}^{(i)}
declares
\mathrm{dist}_{G^{(i)}}(u,v)\leq\lambda_{\mathrm{LowDO},\alpha}\cdot h
, it can output a path
P
connecting
u
and
v
in
G^{(i)}
with length
\ell_{G^{(i)}}(P)\leq\lambda_{\mathrm{LowDO},\alpha}\cdot h
in additional
2^{O(1/\epsilon^{4})}\cdot|P|
time.

10.1
Fast Certified-ED Access Interfaces

Before showing the query algorithm, we first implement some constant-time access interfaces of certified-EDs, which allow us to achieve sublogarithmic query time at last. Let
(C,L,{\cal N},{\cal R},\Pi_{{\cal R}\to G})
be a certified-ED for some node-weighting
A\geq\mathds{1}(V(G))
on
G
. In fact, our query algorithm will work on the restriction of
{\cal N}
on
\mathds{1}(V(G))\subseteq A
, denoted by
{\cal N}_{\mathrm{vtx}}={\cal N}_{\mid\mathds{1}(V(G))}
. Therefore, the interfaces below are also for
{\cal N}_{\mathrm{vtx}}
. As mentioned in
Definition

3.2
, equivalently
{\cal N}
is a pairwise cover of vertex set
V(G)
.

Membership Queries.

Given a cluster
S\in{\cal N}_{\mathrm{vtx}}
and a vertex
v\in A
, the interface
\textsc{Membership}(S,v)
answers whether
v\in S
or not.

We assign a distinct cluster ID to each cluster
S\in{\cal N}_{\mathrm{vtx}}
, and we also assign a distinct (integral) clustering ID in range
[1,\omega]
to each clustering
{\cal S}\in{\cal N}_{\mathrm{vtx}}
. For each virtual node
v\in A
, we store an array with length
\omega
, where the
k
-th element is the cluster ID of
S
s.t.
v\in S
and the clustering
{\cal S}
containing
S
has clustering ID
k
(if there is no such
S
, then put a symbol
\perp
). For each vertex insertion or deletion update to
{\cal N}_{\mathrm{vtx}}
, it takes constant time to update this structure.

To answer
\textsc{Membership}(S,v)
, we look at the clustering ID of
{\cal S}\ni S
, say
k
. Then
v\in S
if and only if the cluster ID in the
k
-th position of the array of
v
is the same as the cluster ID of
S
. The query time is
O(1)
.

L
-Vertex Queries.

Given a cluster
S\in{\cal N}_{\mathrm{vtx}}
, the interface
\textsc{L-vertex}(S)
returns an arbitrary vertex
v\in S\cap L
, or declares
S\cap L
is empty.

For each cluster
S\in{\cal N}_{\mathrm{vtx}}
, we store all
L
-vertices in
S
in a binary search tree. The query time is trivially
O(1)
since we can return the
L
-vertex at the root of the BST.

Updating these BSTs takes
n^{O(\epsilon)}
time for each unit update to the certified-EDs, which is affordable to our update algorithm. First, for each vertex insertion or deletion to
{\cal N}_{\mathrm{vtx}}
, updating the BST takes
O(\log n)
time. Second, for each
L
-vertex insertion, it takes
\tilde{O}(\omega)=n^{O(\epsilon)}
time to update the BST, because all certified-EDs we maintain have
{\cal N}
with width at most
n^{O(\epsilon)}
. There is no
L
-vertex deletion, because
L
is updated incrementally during our maintenance of certified-EDs.

10.2
Proof of
Theorem

10.1

Proof.

This data structure also exploits dynamic expander hierarchy in
Theorem

9.1
. Precisely, we initialize and maintain an expander hierarchy
({\cal H},{\cal D})
with
\bar{k}=\lambda_{k}
levels by applying
Theorem

9.1
on the dynamic graph
G
with parameters
\kappa_{k}=\lambda_{k}
and
\phi=\kappa_{\phi}=n^{\epsilon^{2}}
.
The additional length parameters at each level
1\leq k\leq\bar{k}
are
\hat{h}_{k}=4\cdot h_{k}
(here we change the notation by passing
\hat{h}_{k}
to the parameter
h_{k}
in
Theorem

9.1
) and
\bar{h}_{k}=(16\cdot\lambda_{\mathrm{RealDiam}}+3)\cdot h_{k}
, where
h_{k}=((16\cdot\lambda_{\mathrm{RealDiam}}+3)\cdot\lambda_{H,\mathrm{up}})^{k-1}\cdot h
for some parameter
\lambda_{\mathrm{RealDiam}}=O(\lambda_{\mathrm{rt},h}(t)\cdot\lambda_{{\rm init},h}\cdot\lambda_{\mathrm{dynED},h})
with sufficiently large hidden constant.

The hierarchy
({\cal H},{\cal D})
has properties listed in
Theorem

9.1
. For clarity, we now restate the properties we will use in the proof. For each level
1\leq k\leq\bar{k}
, let
H_{k}
be the vertex spasifier and let
(C_{k},L_{k},{\cal N}_{k},{\cal R}_{k},\Pi_{{\cal R}_{k}\to H_{k}})
be the certified-ED of some node-weighting
A_{k}
on
H_{k}
. The vertex sparsifiers satsify that
H_{1}=G
and for each
1\leq k\leq\bar{k}-1
,

•

H_{k+1}
is a
(\lambda_{H,\mathrm{low}},\lambda_{H,\mathrm{up}},\bar{h}_{k})
-sparsifier of
L_{k}
on
H_{k}
,

•

H_{k+1}
has size
|H_{k+1}|\leq O(t\cdot\kappa_{H,\mathrm{size}}\cdot\phi\cdot|H_{k}|)
.

•

Given a path
P_{H_{k+1}}
on
H_{k+1}
connecting some vertices
u,v\in\bar{T}_{k}
, there is an algorithm that computes a
u
-
v
path
P_{H_{k}}
on
H_{k}
with
\ell_{H_{k}}(P_{H_{k}})\leq\lambda_{H,\mathrm{low}}\cdot\ell_{H_{k+1}}(P_{H_{k+1}})
and
|P_{H_{k}}|\geq|P_{H_{k+1}}|/2
. The path-reporting time is
|P_{H_{k+1}}|\cdot 2^{O(1/\epsilon^{4})}
.

The certified-EDs satisfy that, for each
1\leq k\leq\bar{k}
,

•

L_{k}
is a landmark set of
C_{k}
on
H_{k}
with distortion
\sigma_{k}=O(\lambda_{\mathrm{dynED},h}\cdot\hat{h}_{k}/\kappa_{\sigma})
.

•

{\cal N}_{k}
is a
b_{k}
-distributed
(h_{{\rm cov},k},\omega_{k},h_{{\rm diam},k})
-neighborhood cover of some node-weighting
A_{k}\geq\deg_{H_{k}}+\mathds{1}(H_{k})
on
H_{k}-C_{k}
with

b_{k}=O(t),\ h_{{\rm cov},k}=\hat{h}_{k}=4\cdot h_{k},\text{
and }\omega_{k}=O(t\cdot\kappa_{\mathrm{NC},\omega}).

In fact, our query algorithm will work on
{\cal N}_{k,\mathrm{vtx}}=({\cal N}_{k})_{\mid\mathds{1}(V(H_{k}))}
, i.e. the restriction of
{\cal N}_{k}
on vertices
V(H_{k})
. Obviously,
{\cal N}_{k,\mathrm{vtx}}
the same quality parameters
b_{k},h_{{\rm cov},k}
and
\omega_{k}
.

•

The routers in
{\cal R}_{k}
are maintained by
Theorem

5.1
under at most
f_{k}=O(t)
batched updates, and the embedding
\Pi_{{\cal R}_{k}\to H_{k}}
has length
h_{{\rm emb},k}=O(\lambda_{{\rm init},h}\cdot\lambda_{\mathrm{dynED},h}\cdot\hat{h}_{k})
. We note that this property is only for showing that, by
Lemma

6.3
, each cluster
S\in{\cal N}_{k,\mathrm{vtx}}
has diameter on
H_{k}
at most

h_{\mathrm{RealDiam},k}=\lambda_{\mathrm{rt},h}(f_{k})\cdot h_{{\rm emb},k}\leq\lambda_{\mathrm{RealDiam}}\cdot h_{k}

where the last inequality holds by our choice of
\lambda_{\mathrm{RealDiam}}
.

10.2
.

At each time
0\leq i\leq t
, there exists a level
k_{0}\leq\bar{k}
(depending on
i
) s.t.
C_{k_{0}}
is empty.

Proof.

Because we have
|H^{(i)}_{k+1}|\leq O(t\cdot\kappa_{H,\mathrm{size}}\cdot\phi\cdot|H^{(i)}_{k}|)\leq|H^{(i)}_{k}|/n^{\Theta(\epsilon^{2})}
for each time
i
and level
k
, because
t\cdot\kappa_{H,\mathrm{size}}=n^{O(\epsilon^{4})}
and
\phi=1/n^{\epsilon^{2}}
. Since the number of levels is
\lambda_{k}=O(1/\epsilon^{2})
(with sufficiently large hidden constant factor), there exists
k_{0}
s.t. the sparsifier
H^{(i)}_{k_{0}+1}
is empty, which means
L^{(i)}_{k_{0}}
and
C^{(i)}_{k_{0}}
are empty.
∎

The above claim says that the hierarchy has at most
\lambda_{k}=O(1/\epsilon^{2})
nontrivial levels over all time. In what follows, we let
k^{(0)}
(depends on the current time
i
) denote the minimum level s.t.
C_{k_{0}}
is empty.

The Query Algorithm

We now describe a query algorithm that given two vertices
u,v\in V(G)
, outputs either
Close
or
Far
, where
Close
means
\mathrm{dist}_{G}(u,v)\leq\lambda_{\mathrm{LowDO},\alpha}\cdot h
, while
Far
means
\mathrm{dist}_{G}(u,v)>h
.

The algorithm is bottom-up. When we are at level
k
, the input is two vertices
u_{k},v_{k}\in V(H_{k})
. Initially, we start from level
1
and let the input be
u_{1}=u
and
v_{1}=v
(trivially
u_{1},v_{1}\in V(H_{1})
because
H_{1}=G
). We do the following at level
k
.

1.

We consider the ball cover
{\cal S}_{v_{k}}
of
v_{k}
in
{\cal N}_{k,\mathrm{vtx}}
(see
Definition

3.3
to recall the definition of ball cover). If there is a cluster
S\in{\cal S}_{v_{k}}
containing
u_{k}
(by querying
\textsc{Membership}(S,u_{k})
), we terminate the algorithm with output
Close
.

2.

Otherwise, we have
u_{k}\notin\bigcup_{S\in{\cal S}_{v_{k}}}S
. If we have reached the level
k_{0}
with empty
C_{k_{0}}
, we terminate the algorithm with output
Far
.

3.

Otherwise, now we are at some level
k<k_{0}
, and we will pick the input
u_{k+1}
and
v_{k+1}
of the next level as follows. We select
v_{k+1}
to be an arbitrary
L_{k}
-vertex in some cluster
S\in{\cal S}_{v_{k}}
(querying
\textsc{L-vertex}(S)
for each
S\in{\cal S}_{v_{k}}
). If there is no such
v^{\prime}_{k}
, we terminate the algorithm with answer
Far
. The vertex
u_{k+1}
is picked by an analogous algorithm. Note that
u_{k+1},v_{k+1}\in L_{k}\subseteq V(H_{k+1})
, and we proceed to the next level
k+1
.

The Query Time and Correctness.

The correctness of the query algorithm is shown by
Lemmas

10.3
and
10.4
. Consider the running time at each level
k
. Step 1 takes
O(b_{k})
time, because the ball cover
{\cal S}_{v_{k}}
has at most
b_{k}
clusters and the membership query of
u
and each cluster
S\in{\cal S}_{v_{k}}
takes
O(1)
time. Step 2 takes constant time trivially. Step 3 takes
O(b_{k})
time because for each cluster
S
, selecting a
L_{k}
-vertex in
S
(or declare there is no
L_{k}
-vertex) takes constant time. In conclusion, the total query time is

O(\sum_{1\leq k\leq\bar{k}}b_{k})=O(\bar{k}\cdot t)=O(\lambda_{k}\cdot t)=O(1/\epsilon^{3}).

10.3
.

If the input vertices
u
and
v
have
\mathrm{dist}_{G}(u,v)\leq h
, the query algorithm will output
Close
.

Proof.

First we will prove, by induction, that each level
k
reached by the algorithm has
\mathrm{dist}_{H_{k}}(u_{k},v_{k})\leq h_{k}
. This statement trivially holds for the first level. For each level
k\geq 2
reached by the algorithm, suppose the statement holds for the level
k-1
. Then we have

\displaystyle\mathrm{dist}_{H_{k}}(u_{k+1},v_{k+1})

\displaystyle\leq\mathrm{dist}_{H_{k}}(u_{k+1},u_{k})+\mathrm{dist}_{H_{k}}(u_{k},v_{k})+\mathrm{dist}_{H_{k}}(v_{k},v_{k+1})

\displaystyle\leq 2\cdot h_{\mathrm{RealDiam},k}+h_{k}\leq\bar{h}_{k}.

Because
u_{k+1},v_{k+1}\in L_{k}
and
H_{k+1}
is a
(\lambda_{H,\mathrm{low}},\lambda_{H,\mathrm{up}},\bar{h}_{k})
-sparsifier of
L_{k}
on
H_{k}
, we have

\mathrm{dist}_{H_{k+1}}(u_{k+1},v_{k+1})\leq\lambda_{H,\mathrm{up}}\cdot\mathrm{dist}_{H_{k}}(u_{k+1},v_{k+1})\leq h_{k+1}.

Now we show that the algorithm will not terminate with output
Far
. If the algorithm reach the level
k=k_{0}
with empty
C_{k_{0}}
,
\mathrm{dist}_{H_{k}-C_{k}}(u_{k},v_{k})=\mathrm{dist}_{H_{k}}(u_{k},v_{k})\leq h_{k}\leq h_{{\rm cov},k}
and
u_{k}
must be inside the ball cover
{\cal S}_{v_{k}}
, and the algorithm will terminate with output
Close
. For other level
k<k_{0}
, if the algorithm reaches level
k
but does not terminate with output
Close
at step 1, then
\mathrm{dist}_{H_{k}-C_{k}}(u_{k},v_{k})>h_{{\rm cov},k}\geq 4h_{k}
and
u_{k+1}
and
v_{k+1}
can be found successfully by the following reason. Consider the
u_{k}
-to-
v_{k}
shortest path
P
on
G
. We know
\ell_{H_{k}}(P)\leq h_{k}
but
\ell_{H_{k}-C_{k}}(P)\geq 4\cdot h_{k}
. Then there must be a
C_{k}
-vertex
v^{\prime}_{k}\in P
with
\mathrm{dist}_{H_{k}-C_{k}}(v_{k},v^{\prime}_{k})\leq h_{k}
by taking the
C_{k}
-vertex on
P
closest to
v_{k}
. By the definition of landmarks, there exists a
L_{k}
-vertex
v_{k+1}
s.t.
\mathrm{dist}_{H_{k}-C_{k}}(v^{\prime}_{k},v_{k+1})\leq\sigma_{k}
. Therefore,
\mathrm{dist}_{H_{k}-C_{k}}(v_{k},v_{k+1})\leq h_{k}+\sigma_{k}\leq 4\cdot h_{k}=h_{{\rm cov},k}
, because
\sigma_{k}\leq h_{k}
from
\lambda_{\mathrm{dynED},h}=2^{O(t)}=2^{O(1/\epsilon)}
and
\kappa_{\sigma}=n^{\epsilon^{4}}
. The same argument holds for
u_{k+1}
.

∎

10.4
.

If the query algorithm outputs
Close
, then
\mathrm{dist}_{G}(u,v)\leq\lambda_{\mathrm{LowDO},\alpha}\cdot h
.

Proof.

Suppose the algorithm terminates with output
Close
at level
k^{\star}
. Then by the end condition,

\mathrm{dist}_{H_{k^{\star}}}(u_{k^{\star}},v_{k^{\star}})\leq h_{\mathrm{RealDiam},k^{\star}}=\lambda_{\mathrm{RealDiam}}\cdot h_{k^{\star}}.

For each level
k<k^{\star}
, because
H_{k+1}
is a
(\lambda_{H,\mathrm{low}},\lambda_{H,\mathrm{up}},\bar{h}_{k})
-sparsifier of
L_{k}
on
H_{k}
and
u_{k+1},v_{k+1}\in L_{k}
, we have

\mathrm{dist}_{H_{k}}(u_{k+1},v_{k+1})\leq\lambda_{H,\mathrm{low}}\cdot\mathrm{dist}_{H_{k+1}}(u_{k+1},v_{k+1}),

which implies

\displaystyle\mathrm{dist}_{H_{k}}(u_{k},v_{k})

\displaystyle\leq\mathrm{dist}_{H_{k}}(u_{k},u_{k+1})+\mathrm{dist}_{H_{k}}(u_{k+1},v_{k+1})+\mathrm{dist}_{H_{k}}(v_{k+1},v_{k})

\displaystyle\leq 2\cdot h_{\mathrm{RealDiam},k}+\mathrm{dist}_{H_{k}}(u_{k+1},v_{k+1})

\displaystyle\leq 2\cdot\lambda_{\mathrm{RealDiam}}\cdot h_{k}+\lambda_{H,\mathrm{low}}\cdot\mathrm{dist}_{H_{k+1}}(u_{k+1},v_{k+1}).

Combining all together, we have

\displaystyle\mathrm{dist}_{G}(u,v)\leq O(\lambda_{H,\mathrm{low}}^{k^{\star}-1}\cdot\lambda_{\mathrm{RealDiam}}\cdot h_{k^{\star}})\leq 2^{O(\lambda_{k})}\cdot(\lambda_{H,\mathrm{low}}\cdot\lambda_{H,\mathrm{up}})^{\lambda_{k}}\cdot\lambda_{\mathrm{RealDiam}}^{\lambda_{k}}\cdot h

∎

The Initialization and Update Time.

Note that the oracle
{\cal O}
is exactly the length-constrained hierarchy
({\cal H},{\cal D})
, so by
Theorem

9.1
and the fact that
\phi=\kappa_{\phi}=n^{\epsilon^{2}}
and

\max_{k}\{\hat{h}_{k},\bar{h}_{k}\}=O(\lambda_{\mathrm{RealDiam}}\cdot\lambda_{H,\mathrm{up}})^{\lambda_{k}}=n^{O(\epsilon)}\cdot h,

the initialization time is
|G^{(0)}|\cdot\mathrm{poly}(h)\cdot n^{O(\epsilon)}
,
and the update time to handle
\pi^{(i)}
is

|\pi^{(i)}|\cdot\mathrm{poly}(h)\cdot n^{O(\epsilon)}
.

Path Reporting.

Given two vertices
u,v\in V(G)
, if the query algorithm returns
Close
, we can compute a path
P
connecting
u
and
v
on
G
as follows. More precisely, we consider the levels from top to bottom (i.e. from level
k^{\star}
to
1
), and at each level
k
, we will generate a path
P_{H_{k}}
connecting
u_{k}
and
v_{k}
on
H_{k}
. Then the path
P_{H_{1}}
is what we want.

At the top level
k^{\star}
, because the output is
Close
, there is a cluster
S_{\mathrm{vtx}}\in{\cal N}_{k^{\star},\mathrm{vtx}}
containing
u_{k^{\star}}
and
v_{k^{\star}}
. Let
S\in{\cal N}_{k^{\star}}
be the original cluster corresponding to
S_{\mathrm{vtx}}
, and let
u_{k^{\star},\mathrm{vtul}},v_{k^{\star},\mathrm{vtul}}\in S
be arbitrary virtual nodes of
u_{k^{\star}}
and
v_{k^{\star}}
respectively. By
Theorem

5.1
on the router
R
corresponding to
S
, we can obtain a
u_{k^{\star}}
-
v_{k^{\star}}
path
P_{R}
on
R
. Substituting each edge on
P_{R}
with its embedding path in
\Pi_{{\cal R}_{k^{\star}}\to H_{k^{\star}}}
lead to the desired
P_{H_{k^{\star}}}
. We have
\ell_{H_{k^{\star}}}(P_{H_{k^{\star}}})\leq\lambda_{\mathrm{rt}}(f_{k^{\star}})\cdot h_{{\rm emb},k^{\star}}\leq\lambda_{\mathrm{RealDiam}}\cdot h_{k^{\star}}
, because
|P_{R}|\leq\lambda_{\mathrm{rt}}(f_{k^{\star}})
and
\Pi_{{\cal R}_{k^{\star}}\to H_{k^{\star}}}
has length
h_{{\rm emb},k^{\star}}
.

At each level
1\leq k\leq\bar{k}-1
, note that we have generated a path
P_{H_{k+1}}
connecting
u_{k+1}
and
v_{k+1}
on
H_{k+1}
with length
\lambda_{\mathrm{RealDiam}}\cdot h_{k+1}
. By the guarantees of vertex sparsifiers, we can unfold
P_{H_{k+1}}
to obtain a
u_{k+1}
-
v_{k+1}
path
P^{\prime}_{H_{k}}
on
H_{k}
with
\ell_{H_{k}}(P^{\prime}_{H_{k}})\leq\lambda_{H,\mathrm{low}}\cdot\ell_{H_{k+1}}(P_{H_{k+1}})
and
|P^{\prime}_{H_{k}}|\geq|P_{H_{k+1}}|/2
. Next, because there is some cluster in
{\cal N}_{k,\mathrm{vtx}}
containing both
u_{k}
and
u_{k+1}
, similar to the algorithm at level
k^{\star}
, we can obtain a
u_{k}
-
u_{k+1}
path
P_{H_{k},u}
on
H_{k}
with
\ell_{H_{k}}(P_{H_{k},u})\leq\lambda_{\mathrm{RealDiam}}\cdot h_{k}
. Similarly, we can obtain a
v_{k}
-
v_{k+1}
path
P_{H_{k},v}
on
H_{k}
with
\ell_{H_{k}}(P_{H_{k},v})\leq\lambda_{\mathrm{RealDiam}}\cdot h_{k}
. Finally,
P_{H_{k}}
is the concatenation of
P_{H_{k},u}
,
P^{\prime}_{H_{k}}
and
P_{H_{k},v}
with guarantee
\ell_{H_{k}}(P_{H_{k}})\leq 2\cdot\lambda_{\mathrm{RealDiam}}\cdot h_{k}+\lambda_{H,\mathrm{low}}\cdot\ell_{H_{k+1}}(P_{H_{k+1}})
and
|P_{H_{k}}|\geq|P_{H_{k+1}}|/2
.

Following the analysis in
Lemma

10.4
, the final path
P_{G}=P_{H_{1}}
on
G=H_{1}
is a
u
-
v
path with length
\ell_{G}(P_{G})\leq\lambda_{\mathrm{LowDO},\alpha}\cdot h
. The total path-reporting time can be bounded by
\sum_{1\leq k\leq\bar{k}}|P_{H_{k}}|\cdot 2^{O(1/\epsilon^{4})}\leq 2^{O(1/\epsilon^{4})}\cdot|P_{G}|
, because
|P_{H_{k}}|\leq 2^{O(\lambda_{k})}\cdot|P_{G}|
and
\lambda_{k}=O(1/\epsilon^{2})
.

∎

11
Dynamic Length-Reducing Emulators

In this section, we will show a dynamic length-reducing emulator algorithm. It will be used in
Section

12
to remove the
\mathrm{poly}(h)
-dependency of the running time of
Theorem

10.1
.

11.1
Length-Reducing Emulators.

Let
G
be a graph, an
(\alpha_{\mathrm{low}},\alpha_{\mathrm{up}},h)
-length-reducing emulator
Q
is an unweighted graph with the following properties.

(1)

V(G)\subseteq V(Q)
.

(2)

Each pair of vertices
u,v\in V(G)
s.t.
\mathrm{dist}_{G}(u,v)\leq h
has
\mathrm{dist}_{Q}(u,v)\leq\alpha_{\mathrm{up}}
.

(3)

Each pair of vertices
u,v\in V(G)
has
\mathrm{dist}_{G}(u,v)\leq\alpha_{\mathrm{low}}\cdot h\cdot\mathrm{dist}_{Q}(u,v)
.

11.2
.

Let
G
be a dynamic graph with parameters
h
and
\phi
under
t
batches of updates
\pi^{(1)},\pi^{(2)},...,\pi^{(t)}
of edge insertions/deletions and isolated vertex insertions/deletions. There is an algorithm that initializes and maintains a
(\lambda_{Q,\mathrm{low}},\lambda_{Q,\mathrm{up}},h)
-length-reducing-emulator
Q
of
G
with

\displaystyle\lambda_{Q,\mathrm{low}}

\displaystyle=\lambda_{\mathrm{rt},h}(t)\cdot\lambda_{{\rm init},h}\cdot\lambda_{\mathrm{dynED},h}\cdot 2^{O(\lambda_{k})}\cdot(\lambda_{H,\mathrm{up}}\cdot\lambda_{H,\mathrm{low}})^{\lambda_{k}}=2^{\mathrm{poly}(1/\epsilon)},

\displaystyle\lambda_{Q,\mathrm{up}}

\displaystyle=4\cdot\lambda_{k}=O(1/\epsilon^{2}).

The algorithm further guarantee the following.

•

The size of
Q
is bounded by
|Q|\leq\kappa_{Q,\mathrm{size}}\cdot|G|
, where
\kappa_{Q,\mathrm{size}}=O(\lambda_{k}\cdot t\cdot\kappa_{\mathrm{NC},\omega})=n^{O(\epsilon^{4})}
. In additional, it can also be bounded by
|Q|\leq n^{O(\epsilon^{2})}\cdot|V(G)|
.

•

The recourse from
Q^{(i-1)}
to
Q^{(i)}
is
O(\kappa_{Q,\mathrm{rcs}}\cdot|\pi^{(i)}|)
, where
\kappa_{Q,\mathrm{rcs}}=\kappa_{\mathrm{EH},\mathrm{NC},\mathrm{rcs}}\cdot\kappa_{\phi}

and
\kappa_{\phi}=n^{\epsilon^{2}}
.

•

The initialization time is

|G^{(0)}|\cdot\mathrm{poly}(h)\cdot n^{O(\epsilon)}

and the update time is

|\pi^{(i)}|\cdot\mathrm{poly}(h)\cdot n^{O(\epsilon)}
.

•

Given a path
P_{Q}
on
Q
connecting some vertices
u,v\in V(G)
, the algorithm can compute a
u
-
v
path
P_{G}
on
G
with
\ell_{G}(P_{G})\leq\lambda_{Q,\mathrm{low}}\cdot h\cdot\ell_{Q}(P_{Q})
and
|P_{G}|\geq|P_{Q}|/2^{O(\lambda_{k})}
in time
2^{O(1/\epsilon^{4})}\cdot|P_{G}|
.

Proof.

Our construction of the emulator
Q
is based on the length-constrained expander hierarchy. Precisely, we initialize and maintain a length-constrained expander hierarchy
({\cal H},{\cal D})
with
\bar{k}=\lambda_{k}
levels. Recall
\lambda_{k}=O(1/\epsilon^{2})
is a sufficiently large global parameter and we define a global parameter
\kappa_{\phi}=n^{\epsilon^{2}}
. We apply
Theorem

9.1
on
G
with parameters
\phi=1/\kappa_{\phi}=1/n^{\epsilon^{2}}
and
\bar{k}=\lambda_{k}
. The additional length parameter for each level
1\leq k\leq\bar{k}
is
\hat{h}_{k}=4\cdot h_{k}
(here we change the notation by passing
\hat{h}_{k}
to the parameter
h_{k}
in
Theorem

9.1
) and
\bar{h}_{k}=2\cdot h_{k}
, where
h_{k}=h\cdot(2\cdot\lambda_{H,\mathrm{up}})^{k-1}
.

For each level
1\leq k\leq\bar{k}
, let
H_{k}
be the vertex spasifier and let
(C_{k},L_{k},{\cal N}_{k},{\cal R}_{k},\Pi_{{\cal R}_{k}\to H_{k}})
be the certified-ED of some node-weighting
A_{k}
on
H_{k}
. We now list the properties we will exploit in the remaining proof as follows. Regarding the sparsifiers,
H_{1}=G
, and for each
1\leq k\leq\bar{k}-1
,

•

H_{k+1}
is a
(\lambda_{H,\mathrm{low}},\lambda_{H,\mathrm{up}},\bar{h}_{k})
-sparsifier of
V(H_{k+1})\cap V(H_{k})
on
H_{k}
s.t.
L_{k}\subseteq V(H_{k+1})\cap V(H_{k})
(in particular,
H_{k+1}
is a sparsifier of
L_{k}
on
H_{k}
),

•

H_{k+1}
has size
|H_{k+1}|\leq O(t\cdot\kappa_{H,\mathrm{size}}\cdot\phi\cdot|H_{k}|)
and number of vertices
|V(H_{k+1})|\leq n^{O(\epsilon^{4})}\cdot|V(H_{k})|
,

•

V(H_{k})\cap V(H_{k+1})=V(H_{k})\cap\bigcup_{k+1\leq k^{\prime}\leq\bar{k}}V(H_{k^{\prime}})
.

•

Given a path
P_{H_{k+1}}
on
H_{k+1}
connecting some vertices
u,v\in V(H_{k+1})\cap V(H_{k})
, there is an algorithm that computes a
u
-
v
path
P_{H_{k}}
on
H_{k}
with
\ell_{H_{k}}(P_{H_{k}})\leq\lambda_{H,\mathrm{low}}\cdot\ell_{H_{k+1}}(P_{H_{k+1}})
and
|P_{H_{k}}|\geq|P_{H_{k+1}}|/2
. The path-reporting time is
|P_{H_{k+1}}|\cdot 2^{O(1/\epsilon^{4})}
.

The certified-EDs satisfy that, for each
1\leq k\leq\bar{k}
,

•

L_{k}
is a landmark set of
C_{k}
on
H_{k}
with distortion
\sigma_{k}=O(\lambda_{\mathrm{dynED},h}\cdot\hat{h}_{k}/\kappa_{\sigma})
. At the top level,
C_{\bar{k}}
and
L_{\bar{k}}
are empty.

•

{\cal N}_{k}
is a
b_{k}
-distributed
(h_{{\rm cov},k},h_{\mathrm{sep},k},\omega_{k})
-pairwise cover of
A_{k}\geq\deg_{H_{k}}+\mathds{1}(V(H_{k}))
on
H_{k}-C_{k}
with

b_{k}=O(t),\ h_{{\rm cov},k}=\hat{h}_{k}=4\cdot h_{k},\ \omega_{k}=O(t\cdot\kappa_{\mathrm{NC},\omega}).

•

The routers in
{\cal R}_{k}
are maintained by
Theorem

5.1
under at most
f_{k}=O(t)
batched updates.

•

The embedding
\Pi_{{\cal R}_{k}\to H_{k}}
has length
h_{{\rm emb},k}=O(\lambda_{{\rm init},h}\cdot\lambda_{\mathrm{dynED},h}\cdot\hat{h}_{k})
.

Construction of
Q
.

For each level
1\leq k\leq\bar{k}
, let
{\cal N}_{\mathrm{vtx},k}=({\cal N}_{k})_{\mid\mathds{1}(V(H_{k}))}
by restricting
{\cal N}_{k}
on vertices
V(H_{k})
. Note that
{\cal N}_{\mathrm{vtx},k}
is a pairwise cover of
T_{k}
with the same quality parameters as those of
{\cal N}_{k}
.

For each cluster
S\in{\cal N}_{\mathrm{vtx},k}
, let
Q^{\star}_{S}
be the star graph with
V(Q^{\star}_{S})=S\cup\{v_{S}\}
, where
v_{S}
is the
artificial center
of
S
(which is a newly created fresh vertex) and we call
S
the
branch-vertices
of
Q^{\star}_{S}
. The edges of

Q^{\star}_{S}
are
E(Q^{\star}_{S})=\{(v_{S},v)\mid v\in S\}
. Then we construct
Q
as

Q:=\bigcup_{1\leq k\leq\bar{k},S\in{\cal N}_{\mathrm{vtx},k}}Q^{\star}_{S}.

Note that, when taking the union of
Q^{\star}_{S}
, we use the global vertex identifiers to identify vertices. We emphasize that
Q
is an unweighted graph and each edge in
E(Q)
has unit length.

11.3
.

The graph
Q
is a
(\alpha_{\mathrm{low}},\alpha_{\mathrm{up}},h)
-length-reducing emulator of
G
with

\alpha_{\mathrm{up}}=4\bar{k},\ \alpha_{\mathrm{low}}=\lambda_{\mathrm{rt},h}(t)\cdot\lambda_{{\rm init},h}\cdot\lambda_{\mathrm{dynED},h}\cdot 2^{O(\bar{k})}\cdot(\lambda_{H,\mathrm{up}}\cdot\lambda_{H,\mathrm{low}})^{\bar{k}}.

Proof.

Let
k_{0}
be the minimum level s.t.
C_{k_{0}}
and
L_{k_{0}}
are empty in the up-to-date hierarchy. Note that
k_{0}
must exist at all time (but may be different depending on the current time) by
10.2
.

Property (1).
The first property
V(G)\subseteq V(Q)
is trivial, because at the first level, the union of star graphs of
{\cal N}_{\mathrm{vtx},1}
will contain
V(G)
.

Property (2).
Consider two vertices
u,v\in V(G)
with
\mathrm{dist}_{G}(u,v)\leq h
. We will prove
\mathrm{dist}_{Q}(u,v)\leq 4\bar{k}
by induction. Let the induction hypothesis be, for each level
1\leq k\leq k_{0}
and each
u,v\in T_{k}
with
\mathrm{dist}_{H_{k}}(u,v)\leq h_{k}
, there is
\mathrm{dist}_{Q}(\tau_{k}(u),\tau_{k}(v))\leq 4(k_{0}-k+1)
.

For the base case
k=k_{0}
, note that
C_{k}
is empty, so
{\cal N}_{\mathrm{tmn},k}
is a pairwise cover of
T_{k}
on
H_{k}
with cover radius
h_{{\rm cov},k}=4\cdot h_{k}
. Therefore,
u,v\in T_{k}
will be contained by some cluster
S\in{\cal N}_{\mathrm{tmn},k}
, and by our construction, the star graph
Q^{\star}_{S}
has a path connecting
\tau_{k}(u)
and
\tau_{k}(v)
with length
2\leq 4(\bar{k}-k+1)
.

For an inductive step at level
k
s.t.
1\leq k\leq k_{0}-1
,
{\cal N}_{\mathrm{tmn},k}
is a pairwise cover of
T_{k}
on
H_{k}-C_{k}
with cover radius
h_{{\rm cov},k}\geq 4\cdot h_{k}
. If
\mathrm{dist}_{H_{k}-C_{k}}(u,v)\leq 4\cdot h_{k}
, then some cluster
S\in{\cal N}_{\mathrm{tmn},k}
will contain
u
and
v
, and
\mathrm{dist}_{Q}(\tau_{k}(u),\tau_{k}(v))\leq\mathrm{dist}_{Q^{\star}_{S}}(\tau_{k}(u),\tau_{k}(v))\leq 2\leq 4(\bar{k}-k+1)
.

Otherwise, we have
\mathrm{dist}_{H_{k}-C_{k}}(u,v)>4h_{k}
. Consider the shortest
u
-
v
path
P
in
H_{k}
. By
11.4
, we can find a vertex
u^{\prime}\in P
with
\mathrm{dist}_{H_{k}-C_{k}}(u,u^{\prime})\leq 2h_{k}
and also a landmark
w_{u}\in L_{k}
with
\mathrm{dist}_{H_{k}-C_{k}}(u^{\prime},w_{u})\leq h_{k}/100
. Similarly, there are such
v^{\prime}\in P
and
w_{v}\in L_{k}
with
\mathrm{dist}_{H_{k}-C_{k}}(v,v^{\prime})\leq 2h_{k}
and
\mathrm{dist}_{H_{k}-C_{k}}(v^{\prime},w_{v})\leq h_{k}/100
.

11.4
.

There is a vertex
u^{\prime}\in P
s.t.
\mathrm{dist}_{H_{k}-C_{k}}(u,u^{\prime})\leq 2h_{k}
and there is a landmark
w_{u}\in L_{k}
with
\mathrm{dist}_{H_{k}-C_{k}}(w_{u},u^{\prime})\leq h_{k}/100
.

Proof.

Let
P_{u}
be the longest prefix of
P
from
u
with length at most
2h_{k}
in
H_{k}-C_{k}
, and let
P^{+}_{u}
be the prefix from extending
P_{u}
along
P
by one more edge. Observe that
C_{k}(P^{+}_{u})\geq\ell_{H_{k}-C_{k}}(P^{+}_{u})-\ell_{H_{k}}(P^{+}_{u})\geq 2h_{k}-h_{k}\geq h_{k}
. We can simply pick
u^{\prime}\in P_{u}
incident to some
e\in\mathrm{supp}(C_{k})\cap P^{+}_{u}
, and let
w_{u}\in L_{k}
be the landmark of
u^{\prime}
with
\mathrm{dist}_{H_{k}-C_{k}}(w_{u},u^{\prime})\leq\sigma_{k}\leq h_{k}/100
.

∎

Now, because
\mathrm{dist}_{H_{k}-C_{k}}(u,w_{u})\leq 4h_{k}\leq h_{{\rm cov},k}
, there is a cluster
S\in{\cal N}_{\mathrm{vtx},k}
containing
u
and
w_{u}
, which implies

\mathrm{dist}_{Q}(u,w_{u})\leq 2\text{ and similarly }\mathrm{dist}_{Q}(v,w_{v})\leq 2.

Also,
\mathrm{dist}_{H_{k}}(w_{u},w_{v})\leq\mathrm{dist}_{H_{k}-C_{k}}(w_{u},u^{\prime})+\mathrm{dist}_{H_{k}}(u^{\prime},v^{\prime})+\mathrm{dist}_{H_{k}-C_{k}}(v^{\prime},w_{v})\leq 1.02h_{k}\leq 2h_{k}=\bar{h}_{k}
. Since
H_{k+1}
is always a
(\lambda_{H,\mathrm{low}},\lambda_{H,\mathrm{up}},\bar{h}_{k})
-sparsifier of
L_{k}
on
H_{k}
, we have
\mathrm{dist}_{H_{k+1}}(w_{u},w_{v})\leq\lambda_{H,\mathrm{up}}\cdot\bar{h}_{k}=2\cdot\lambda_{H,\mathrm{up}}\cdot h_{k}=h_{k+1}
, so

\mathrm{dist}_{Q}(w_{u},w_{v})\leq 4(k_{0}-k)

by the induction. Finally, we can conclude that
\mathrm{dist}_{Q}(u,v)\leq\mathrm{dist}_{Q}(u,w_{u})+\mathrm{dist}_{Q}(w_{u},w_{v})+\mathrm{dist}_{Q}(w_{v},v)\leq 2+4(k_{0}-k)+2=4(k_{0}-k+1)
as desired.

Property (3).
Consider an arbitrary pair of vertices
u,v\in V(G)
. We will show
\mathrm{dist}_{G}(u,v)\leq\alpha_{\mathrm{low}}\cdot h\cdot\mathrm{dist}_{Q}(u,v)
by induction.

We begin with some notations. For each level
k
, let

Q_{k}=\bigcup_{S\in{\cal N}_{k}}Q^{\star}_{S},\text{ and }Q_{\geq k}=\bigcup_{k^{\prime}\geq k,S\in{\cal N}_{k^{\prime}}}Q^{\star}_{S}.

Let the induction hypothesis be, for each level
1\leq k\leq\bar{k}
and each pair of vertices
u,v\in V(H_{k})
, we have

\mathrm{dist}_{H_{k}}(u,v)\leq\beta\cdot(2\cdot\lambda_{H,\mathrm{up}}\cdot\lambda_{H,\mathrm{low}})^{\bar{k}-k}\cdot h_{k}\cdot\mathrm{dist}_{Q_{\geq k}}(u,v).

where
\beta=O(\lambda_{\mathrm{rt},h}(t)\cdot\lambda_{{\rm init},h}\cdot\lambda_{\mathrm{dynED},h})
.

The Base Case.
For the base case
k=\bar{k}
, consider the
u
-
v
shortest path on
Q_{\geq k}
, denoted by
P_{Q_{\geq k}}(u,v)
. Note that
Q_{\geq k}=Q_{k}
in this case, and
Q_{k}
is a union of stars whose branch-vertices are in
V(H_{k})
. If we partition
P_{Q_{\geq k}}(u,v)
into subpaths with endpoints in
V(H_{k})
but no internal vertex in
V(H_{k})
, then each subpath
P^{\prime}
(with endpoints
u^{\prime},v^{\prime}
) has length exactly
2
and it is contained by some
Q^{\star}_{S}\subseteq Q_{k}
for some
S\in{\cal N}_{\mathrm{vtx},k}
s.t.
u^{\prime},v^{\prime}\in S
. Thus, by
Lemma

6.3
,

\displaystyle\mathrm{dist}_{H_{k}}(u^{\prime},v^{\prime})

\displaystyle\leq\lambda_{\mathrm{rt},h}(f_{k})\cdot h_{{\rm emb},k}\leq O(\lambda_{\mathrm{rt},h}(t)\cdot\lambda_{{\rm init},h}\cdot\lambda_{\mathrm{dynED},h}\cdot h_{k}).

and because the number of such subpaths
P^{\prime}
is
\mathrm{dist}_{Q_{\geq k}}(u,v)/2
, we have

\mathrm{dist}_{H_{k}}(u,v)\leq O(\lambda_{\mathrm{rt},h}(t)\cdot\lambda_{{\rm init},h}\cdot\lambda_{\mathrm{dynED},h}\cdot h_{k}\cdot\mathrm{dist}_{Q_{\geq k}}(u,v))

as desired.

The Inductive Step.
For an inductive step
1\leq k\leq\bar{k}-1
, again we consider the
u
-
v
shortest path
P_{Q_{\geq k}}(u,v)
on
Q_{\geq k}
. We partition
P_{Q_{\geq k}}(u,v)
into (maximal) subpaths totally on
Q_{k}
or totally on
Q_{\geq k+1}
.

11.5
.

V(Q_{k})\cap V(Q_{\geq k+1})=V(H_{k})\cap V(H_{k+1})
.

Proof.

By the construction of
Q
,
V(Q_{k})
is the union of
V(H_{k})
and artificial centers of stars, and similarly
V(Q_{\geq k+1})
is the union of
\bigcup_{k+1\leq k^{\prime}\leq\bar{k}}V(H_{k^{\prime}})
and artificial centers. Because the artificial centers of
V(Q_{k})
and
V(Q_{\geq k+1})
are disjoint, we have
V(Q_{k})\cap V(Q_{\geq k+1})=V(H_{k})\cap\bigcup_{k+1\leq k^{\prime}\leq\bar{k}}V(H_{k^{\prime}})=V(H_{k})\cap V(H_{k+1})
by the guarantee of sparsifiers.
∎

For each subpath
P^{\prime}_{\geq k+1}
(with endpoints
u^{\prime},v^{\prime}
) on
Q_{\geq k+1}
, we have
u^{\prime},v^{\prime}\in V(H_{k})\cap V(H_{k+1})
by
11.5
. Then, the induction hypothesis at level
k+1
tells

\mathrm{dist}_{H_{k+1}}(u^{\prime},v^{\prime})\leq\beta\cdot(2\cdot\lambda_{H,\mathrm{up}}\cdot\lambda_{H,\mathrm{low}})^{\bar{k}-k-1}\cdot h_{k+1}\cdot\mathrm{dist}_{Q_{\geq k+1}}(u^{\prime},v^{\prime})

By property (2) in
Definition

8.1
of the sparsifier
H_{k+1}
and the fact that
u^{\prime},v^{\prime}\in V(H_{k})\cap V(H_{k+1})
, we have

\displaystyle\mathrm{dist}_{H_{k}}(u^{\prime},v^{\prime})

\displaystyle\leq\lambda_{H,\mathrm{low}}\cdot\mathrm{dist}_{H_{k+1}}(u^{\prime},v^{\prime})

\displaystyle\leq\beta\cdot\lambda_{H,\mathrm{low}}\cdot(2\cdot\lambda_{H,\mathrm{up}}\cdot\lambda_{H,\mathrm{low}})^{\bar{k}-k-1}\cdot h_{k+1}\cdot\mathrm{dist}_{Q_{\geq k+1}}(u^{\prime},v^{\prime})

\displaystyle\leq\beta\cdot\lambda_{H,\mathrm{low}}\cdot(2\cdot\lambda_{H,\mathrm{up}}\cdot\lambda_{H,\mathrm{low}})^{\bar{k}-k-1}\cdot(2\cdot\lambda_{H,\mathrm{up}}\cdot h_{k})\cdot\mathrm{dist}_{Q_{\geq k+1}}(u^{\prime},v^{\prime})

\displaystyle\leq\beta\cdot(2\cdot\lambda_{H,\mathrm{up}}\cdot\lambda_{H,\mathrm{low}})^{\bar{k}-k}\cdot h_{k}\cdot\mathrm{dist}_{Q_{\geq k}}(u^{\prime},v^{\prime}),

where the last inequality is by
\mathrm{dist}_{Q_{\geq k+1}}(u^{\prime},v^{\prime})=\mathrm{dist}_{Q_{\geq k+1}}(P^{\prime}_{\geq k+1})=\mathrm{dist}_{Q_{\geq k}}(P^{\prime}_{\geq k+1})=\mathrm{dist}_{Q_{\geq k}}(u^{\prime},v^{\prime})
.

Next, for each subpath
P^{\prime}_{k}
(with endpoints
u^{\prime},v^{\prime}
) totally on
Q_{k}
, we have
u^{\prime},v^{\prime}\in V(H_{k})
by
11.5
and the fact that
u,v\in V(H_{k})
. An argument similar to the base case can show

\mathrm{dist}_{H_{k}}(u^{\prime},v^{\prime})\leq\beta\cdot h_{k}\cdot\mathrm{dist}_{Q_{k}}(u^{\prime},v^{\prime})=\beta\cdot h_{k}\cdot\mathrm{dist}_{Q_{\geq k}}(u^{\prime},v^{\prime}).

Lastly, we can conclude

\mathrm{dist}_{H_{k}}(u,v)\leq\beta\cdot(2\cdot\lambda_{H,\mathrm{up}}\cdot\lambda_{H,\mathrm{low}})^{\bar{k}-k}\cdot h_{k}\cdot\mathrm{dist}_{Q_{\geq k}}(u,v)

by concatenating the subpaths.

∎

The Size of
Q
.

By the construction, we have

|Q|\leq\sum_{1\leq k\leq\bar{k}}\mathrm{size}({\cal N}_{\mathrm{vtx},k})\leq\sum_{k}\omega_{k}\cdot|V(H_{k})|\leq O(\lambda_{k}\cdot t\cdot\kappa_{\mathrm{NC},\omega}\cdot|G|),

where the last inequality is because
|V(H_{k})|\leq|H_{k}|\leq|G|
(since for each
k
,
|H_{k+1}|\leq O(t\cdot\kappa_{H,\mathrm{size}}\cdot\phi\cdot H_{k})\leq|H_{k}|
).

Besides, from
|V(H_{k+1})|\leq n^{O(\epsilon^{4})}\cdot|V(H_{k})|
, we have
|V(H_{k})|\leq n^{O(\lambda_{k}/\epsilon^{4})}\cdot|V(G)|=n^{O(\epsilon^{2})}\cdot|V(G)|
. Therefore, we can claim another bound
|Q|\leq n^{O(\epsilon^{2})}\cdot|V(G)|
.

The Recourse from
Q^{(i-1)}
to
Q^{(i)}
.

By our construction of
Q
, each vertex insertion/deletion from
{\cal N}^{(i-1)}_{\mathrm{vtx},k}
to
{\cal N}^{(i)}_{\mathrm{vtx},k}
will cause at most constant number of unit updates from
Q^{(i-1)}
to
Q^{(i)}
. Therefore,

\displaystyle\mathrm{Rcs}(Q^{(i-1)}\to Q^{(i)})

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{1\leq k\leq\bar{k}}\mathrm{Rcs}({\cal N}^{(i-1)}_{\mathrm{vtx},k}\to{\cal N}^{(i)}_{\mathrm{vtx},k})\leq\sum_{1\leq k\leq\bar{k}}\mathrm{Rcs}({\cal N}^{(i-1)}_{k}\to{\cal N}^{(i)}_{k})

\displaystyle\leq O(\kappa_{\mathrm{EH},\mathrm{NC},\mathrm{rcs}}\cdot|\pi^{(i)}|/\phi)=O(\kappa_{\mathrm{EH},\mathrm{NC},\mathrm{rcs}}\cdot\kappa_{\phi}\cdot|\pi^{(i)}|).

The Initialization and Update time.

The running time is dominated by the initialization and maintenance of the length-constrained expander hierarchy. Hence by
Theorem

9.1
, the initialization time is

|E(G^{(0)})|\cdot\mathrm{poly}(h)\cdot n^{O(\epsilon)}

and the update time is

|\pi^{(i)}|\cdot\mathrm{poly}(h)\cdot n^{O(\epsilon)}
.

Path Unfolding.

Given a path
P_{Q}
connecting
u,v\in V(G)
in
Q
, the
u
-
v
path
P_{G}
in
G
can be generated by the following algorithm, which basically follows the analysis of property (3) in
Lemma

11.3
. We will use the same notations in the proof of property (3) in
Lemma

11.3
.

Precisely, we consider the levels from top to bottom (i.e. from level
\bar{k}
to level
1
). At each level
k
, we implement a subroutine that given a path
P_{Q_{\geq k}}
on
Q_{\geq k}
connecting two vertices
u,v\in V(H_{k})
, returns a
u
-
v
path
P_{H_{k}}
on
H_{k}
with
\ell_{H_{k}}(P_{H_{k}})\leq\beta\cdot(2\cdot\lambda_{H,\mathrm{up}}\cdot\lambda_{H,\mathrm{low}})^{\bar{k}-k}\cdot h_{k}\cdot\ell_{Q_{\geq k}}(P_{Q_{\geq k}})
and
|P_{H_{k}}|\geq|P_{Q\geq k}|/2^{\bar{k}-k+1}
in time
|P_{H_{k}}|\cdot 2^{O(1/\epsilon^{4})}\cdot(2^{\bar{k}-k+1}-1)
.

For the top level
k=\bar{k}
, we have
Q_{\geq k}=Q_{k}
and we decompose the path
P_{Q_{\geq k}}
into
star subpaths
s.t. each subpath
P^{\prime}_{Q_{k}}
connects two vertices
u^{\prime},v^{\prime}\in V(H_{k})
and is contained by some star in
Q_{k}
. Each star subpath
P^{\prime}_{Q_{k}}
can be unfolded to a
u^{\prime}
-
v^{\prime}
path
P^{\prime}_{H_{k}}
on
H_{k}
with
\ell_{H_{k}}(P^{\prime}_{H_{k}})\leq\lambda_{\mathrm{rt}}(f_{k})\cdot h_{{\rm emb},k}\leq\beta\cdot h_{k}\leq\beta\cdot h_{k}\cdot\ell_{Q_{k}}(P^{\prime}_{Q_{k}})
(the last inequality is because each star subpath has length exactly 2) and
|P^{\prime}_{H_{k}}|\geq|P^{\prime}_{Q_{k}}|/2
(which needs to take care of the case
u^{\prime}=v^{\prime}
). This can be done by exploiting the router of the cluster of this star and the embedding in time
2^{O(1/\epsilon^{4})}+O(|P^{\prime}_{H_{k}}|)
. A similar argument has appeared several times in e.g. the path-unfolding paragraph in the proof of
Theorem

8.2
, so we omit it here. By substituting each star subpath
P^{\prime}_{Q_{k}}
on
P_{Q_{\geq k}}
with
P^{\prime}_{H_{k}}
, we can obtain the desired
P_{H_{k}}
. The total running time is
|P_{Q_{k}}|\cdot 2^{O(1/\epsilon^{4})}+\sum|P^{\prime}_{H_{k}}|=|P_{H_{k}}|\cdot 2^{O(1/\epsilon^{4})}
because we invoke the path-reporting algorithm of routers
O(|P_{Q_{k}}|)
times.

For an arbitrary level
1\leq k\leq\bar{k}-1
, we decompose the path
P_{Q_{\geq k}}
into subpaths totally on
Q_{k}
or totally on
Q_{\geq k+1}
.

1.

For each subpath
P^{\prime}_{Q_{\geq k+1}}
totally on
Q_{\geq k+1}
, note that it has endpoints
u^{\prime},v^{\prime}\in V(Q_{\geq k+1})\cap V(Q_{k})=V(H_{k+1})\cap V(H_{k})
by
11.5
. The subroutine at level
k+1
will give a
u^{\prime}
-
v^{\prime}
path
P^{\prime}_{H_{k+1}}
on
H_{k+1}
, and the path unfolding algorithm for sparsifiers will further give a
u^{\prime}
-
v^{\prime}
path
P^{\prime}_{H_{k}}
on
H_{k}
s.t.
\ell_{H_{k}}(P^{\prime}_{H_{k}})\leq\lambda_{H,\mathrm{low}}\cdot\ell_{H_{k+1}}(P^{\prime}_{H_{k+1}})
and
|P^{\prime}_{H_{k}}|\geq|P^{\prime}_{H_{k+1}}|/2
.

2.

For each subpath
P^{\prime}_{Q_{k}}
totally on
Q_{k}
, it has endpoints
u^{\prime},v^{\prime}\in V(H_{k})
. Similar to the algorithm for the top level
\bar{k}
, we can get a
u^{\prime}
-
v^{\prime}
path
P^{\prime}_{H_{k}}
on
H_{k}
with
\ell_{H_{k}}(P^{\prime}_{H_{k}})\leq\beta\cdot h_{k}\cdot\ell_{Q_{k}}(P^{\prime}_{Q_{k}})
and
|P^{\prime}_{H_{k}}|\geq|P^{\prime}_{Q_{k}}|/2
.

By substituting each subpath of
P_{Q_{\geq k}}
with its unfolded path on
H_{k}
, we can obtain the desired
P_{H_{k}}
. The running time for this subroutine is

\sum_{\text{each }P^{\prime}_{Q_{\geq k+1}}}(|P^{\prime}_{H_{k+1}}|\cdot 2^{O(1/\epsilon^{4})}\cdot(2^{\bar{k}-k}-1)+|P^{\prime}_{H_{k}}|\cdot 2^{O(1/\epsilon^{4})})+\sum_{\text{each }P^{\prime}_{Q_{k}}}|P^{\prime}_{H_{k}}|\cdot 2^{O(1/\epsilon^{4})},

which is at most
|P_{H_{k}}|\cdot 2^{O(1/\epsilon^{4})}\cdot(2^{\bar{k}-k+1}-1)
because each
P^{\prime}_{H_{k+1}}
in case 1 has
|P^{\prime}_{H_{k+1}}|\leq 2|P^{\prime}_{H_{k}}|

Finally, the subroutine for level
1
can return the
u
-
v
path
P_{G}
in
G
with guarantee
\ell_{G}(P_{G})\leq\kappa_{Q,\mathrm{low}}\cdot\ell_{Q}(P_{Q})
and
|P_{G}|\geq|P_{Q}|/2^{O(\lambda_{k})}
. The path-unfolding time is
|P_{G}|\cdot 2^{O(1/\epsilon^{4})}\cdot 2^{\lambda_{k}}=|P_{G}|\cdot 2^{O(1/\epsilon^{4})}
because
\lambda_{k}=O(1/\epsilon^{2})
.

∎

12
Fully Dynamic Distance Oracles

In this section, we finally prove our main result:

12.1
.

Let
G
be a fully dynamic undirected graph under edge insertions/deletions and isolated vertex insertions/deletions s.t. edge lengths on
G
are all polynomially bounded positive integers. Let
m_{0}
denote the initial graph size and
n
be an upper bound of number of vertices.

For some sufficiently small constant
c>0
, given a parameter
1/\log^{c}n\leq\epsilon\leq 1
, there is an approximate deterministic fully dynamic distance oracle supports the following query. At any time, given two vertices
u,v
in the current
G
, if
u
and
v
are disconnected in
G
, the oracle answers
u
and
v
are disconnected. Otherwise, the oracle answers an approximate distance
\widetilde{d}
s.t.
\mathrm{dist}_{G}(u,v)\leq\widetilde{d}\leq 2^{\mathrm{poly}(1/\epsilon)}\cdot\mathrm{dist}_{G}(u,v)
in
O(\log\log n/\epsilon^{4})
query time. The oracle can further output

•

a (possibly non-simple) path
P
connecting
u
and
v
on
G
with
\ell_{G}(P)\leq 2^{\mathrm{poly}(1/\epsilon)}\cdot\mathrm{dist}_{G}(u,v)
in additional
O(|P|)
time, and

•

a simple path
P_{\mathrm{sp}}
connecting
u
and
v
on
G
with
\ell_{G}(P_{\mathrm{sp}})\leq 2^{\mathrm{poly}(1/\epsilon)}\cdot\mathrm{dist}_{G}(u,v)
in additional
O(|P_{\mathrm{sp}}|\cdot n^{\epsilon})
time.

The oracle has initialization time
O(m_{0}\cdot n^{\epsilon})
and worst-case update time
O(n^{\epsilon})
.

Our approach is to “stack” length-reducing emulators on top of each other. These emulators allow us to assume that the distances are small, so we can apply the distance oracle for the short-distance regime from
Section

10
.
The approach is standard and was used in many previous works

[
Che18
,
Chu21
,
BGS22
,
ŁN22
,
CZ23b
]
.

12.1
One Level of the Stacking

12.2
.

Let
G
be a graph under
t
batches of updates
\pi^{(1)},\pi^{(2)},...,\pi^{(t)}
of edge insertions and edge deletions. Given a parameter
h
, there is an algorithm that maintains a
distance-reduced
graph
G_{\mathrm{stk}}
s.t.
V(G)\subseteq V(G_{\mathrm{stk}})
and for each
u,v\in V(G)
,

\displaystyle\mathrm{dist}_{G}(u,v)

\displaystyle\leq\lambda_{\mathrm{stk},\mathrm{low}}\cdot h\cdot\mathrm{dist}_{G_{\mathrm{stk}}}(u,v),\text{ and }

\displaystyle\mathrm{dist}_{G_{\mathrm{stk}}}(u,v)

\displaystyle\leq\lambda_{\mathrm{stk},\mathrm{up}}\cdot\max\{\mathrm{dist}_{G}(u,v)/h,1\},

where
\lambda_{\mathrm{stk},\mathrm{low}}=\lambda_{Q,\mathrm{low}}=2^{\mathrm{poly}(1/\epsilon)}
and
\lambda_{\mathrm{stk},\mathrm{up}}=O(\lambda_{Q,\mathrm{up}})=O(1/\epsilon^{2})
. The size of
G_{\mathrm{stk}}
is
|G_{\mathrm{stk}}|\leq\kappa_{\mathrm{stk},\mathrm{size}}\cdot|G|
, where
\kappa_{\mathrm{stk},\mathrm{size}}=\kappa_{Q,\mathrm{size}}+1
. The algorithm further guarantee the following.

•

The recourse from
G_{\mathrm{stk}}^{(i-1)}
to
G_{\mathrm{stk}}^{(i)}
is at most
\kappa_{\mathrm{stk},\mathrm{rcs}}\cdot|\pi^{(i)}|
, where
\kappa_{\mathrm{stk},\mathrm{rcs}}=\kappa_{Q,\mathrm{rcs}}+1
.

•

The initialization time is

|G^{(0)}|\cdot\mathrm{poly}(h)\cdot n^{O(\epsilon)}

and the update time is

|\pi^{(i)}|\cdot\mathrm{poly}(h)\cdot n^{O(\epsilon)}
.

•

Given a path
P_{G_{\mathrm{stk}}}
on
G_{\mathrm{stk}}
connecting
u,v\in V(G)
, the algorithm can compute a
u
-
v
path
P_{G}
on
G
with
\ell_{G}(P_{G})\leq\lambda_{\mathrm{stk},\mathrm{low}}\cdot h\cdot\ell_{G_{\mathrm{stk}}}(P_{G_{\mathrm{stk}}})
and
|P_{G}|\geq|P_{G_{\mathrm{stk}}}|/2^{O(\lambda_{k})}
in time
2^{O(1/\epsilon^{4})}\cdot|P_{G}|
.

Proof.

We maintain a
(\lambda_{Q,\mathrm{low}},\lambda_{Q,\mathrm{up}},h)
-length-reducing emulator
Q
of
G
by
Theorem

11.2
. Note that each edge in
Q
has unit length. Let
E_{\mathrm{heavy}}=\{(u,v)\mid(u,v)\in E(G),\ell_{G}(u,v)\geq h/3\}
, and we define the length of each edge
e\in E_{\mathrm{heavy}}
to be
\lceil\ell_{G}(u,v)/h\rceil
. Then we define
G_{\mathrm{stk}}=Q\cup E_{\mathrm{heavy}}
.

Quality of
G_{\mathrm{stk}}
.

We first show
\mathrm{dist}_{G}(u,v)\leq\lambda_{\mathrm{stk},\mathrm{low}}\cdot h\cdot\mathrm{dist}_{G_{\mathrm{stk}}}(u,v)
. Let
P_{\mathrm{stk}}
be a shortest
u
-
v
path in
G_{\mathrm{stk}}
. We decompose
P_{\mathrm{stk}}
into subpaths with endpoints in
V(G)
but no intermediate vertex in
V(G)
. Note that each subpath is either a single edge
e\in E_{\mathrm{heavy}}
or a path totally inside
Q
. For each subpath which is an edge
e=(u,v)\in E_{\mathrm{heavy}}
, we have
\mathrm{dist}_{G}(u,v)\leq\ell_{G_{\mathrm{stk}}}(e)\cdot h
by the definition of
E_{\mathrm{heavy}}
. For each subpath
P^{\prime}
inside
Q
, we have
\mathrm{dist}_{G}(u,v)\leq\lambda_{Q,\mathrm{low}}\cdot h\cdot\mathrm{dist}_{Q}(u,v)=\lambda_{Q,\mathrm{low}}\cdot h\cdot\ell_{Q}(P^{\prime})
. Therefore, we can conclude that
\mathrm{dist}_{G}(u,v)\leq\lambda_{\mathrm{stk},\mathrm{low}}\cdot h\cdot\mathrm{dist}_{G_{\mathrm{stk}}}(u,v)
.

Next we show
\mathrm{dist}_{G_{\mathrm{stk}}}(u,v)\leq\lambda_{\mathrm{stk},\mathrm{up}}\cdot\lceil\mathrm{dist}_{G}(u,v)/h\rceil
. We first consider the case when
\mathrm{dist}_{G}(u,v)\leq h
, in which we have
\mathrm{dist}_{G_{\mathrm{stk}}}(u,v)\leq\mathrm{dist}_{Q}(u,v)\leq\lambda_{Q,\mathrm{up}}
.

For a general case with
\mathrm{dist}_{G}(u,v)>h
. Let
P
be a shortest
u
-
v
path in
G
. We decompose
P
in the following way. First, we view each edge
e\in P
with
\ell_{G}(e)\geq h/3
as a
singleton subpath
, and then remove those edges, which breaks
P
into subpaths called
segments
. For each segment
P^{\prime}
with
\ell_{G}(P^{\prime})<h
, we call it a
light segment
. For each segment
P^{\prime}
with
\ell_{G}(P^{\prime})\geq h
, we further decompose it into sub-segments
P^{\prime\prime}
with
h/3\leq\ell_{G}(P^{\prime\prime})\leq h
(this is doable since each edge on such segments has length at most
h/3
), and we call these sub-segments
heavy sub-segments
.

For each singleton subpath
P^{\prime}
connecting
u^{\prime},v^{\prime}
, we have
\mathrm{dist}_{G_{\mathrm{stk}}}(u^{\prime},v^{\prime})\leq\lceil\ell_{G}(P^{\prime})/h\rceil\leq 3\ell_{G}(P^{\prime})/h
, because the edge
e\in P^{\prime}
is inside
E_{\mathrm{heavy}}
. For each light segment
P^{\prime}
connecting
u^{\prime},v^{\prime}
, we have
\mathrm{dist}_{G_{\mathrm{stk}}}(u^{\prime},v^{\prime})\leq\mathrm{dist}_{Q}(u^{\prime},v^{\prime})\leq\lambda_{Q,\mathrm{up}}
. For each heavy sub-segment
P^{\prime\prime}
connecting
u^{\prime\prime}
and
v^{\prime\prime}
, we have
\mathrm{dist}_{G_{\mathrm{stk}}}(u^{\prime\prime},v^{\prime\prime})\leq\mathrm{dist}_{Q}(u^{\prime},v^{\prime})\leq\lambda_{Q,\mathrm{up}}\leq 3\cdot\lambda_{Q,\mathrm{up}}\cdot\ell_{G}(P^{\prime\prime})/h
. Also, note that the number of light segments is at most the number of singleton subpaths plus 1. Summing over all things,

\displaystyle\mathrm{dist}_{G_{\mathrm{stk}}}(u,v)

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{\text{singleton subpath }P^{\prime}}3\ell_{G}(P^{\prime})/h+\sum_{\text{light segments }P^{\prime}}\lambda_{Q,\mathrm{up}}+\sum_{\text{heavy sub-segments }P^{\prime\prime}}3\cdot\lambda_{Q,\mathrm{up}}\cdot\ell_{G}(P^{\prime\prime})/h

\displaystyle\leq\lambda_{Q,\mathrm{up}}+\sum_{\text{singleton subpath }P^{\prime}}(1+\lambda_{Q,\mathrm{up}})\cdot 3\cdot\ell_{G}(P^{\prime})/h+\sum_{\text{heavy sub-segments }P^{\prime\prime}}3\cdot\lambda_{Q,\mathrm{up}}\cdot\ell_{G}(P^{\prime\prime})/h

\displaystyle\leq\lambda_{Q,\mathrm{up}}+3(1+\lambda_{Q,\mathrm{up}})\ell_{G}(P)/h

\displaystyle\leq O(\lambda_{Q,\mathrm{up}})\cdot\mathrm{dist}_{G}(u,v)/h,

where the second inequality is because we distribute the term
\lambda_{Q,\mathrm{up}}
of light segments to singleton subpaths (and there may be one left), and the last inequality is because
\ell_{G}(P)=\mathrm{dist}_{G}(u,v)\geq h
.

The Size of
G_{\mathrm{stk}}
.

By
Theorem

11.2
,
|Q|\leq\kappa_{Q,\mathrm{size}}\cdot|G|
, so
|G_{\mathrm{stk}}|\leq|Q|+|G|\leq(\kappa_{Q,\mathrm{size}}+1)\cdot|G|
.

The Recourse from
G_{\mathrm{stk}}^{(i-1)}
to
G_{\mathrm{stk}}^{(i)}
.

The total recourse is simply the sum of recourse from
Q^{(i-1)}
to
Q^{(i)}
and the changes from
E^{(i-1)}_{\mathrm{heavy}}
to
E^{(i)}_{\mathrm{heavy}}
. The former is
\kappa_{Q,\mathrm{rcs}}\cdot|\pi^{(i)}|
by
Theorem

11.2
and the latter is trivially at most
|\pi^{(i)}|
, so
\mathrm{Rcs}(G^{(i-1)}_{\mathrm{stk}}\to G^{(i)}_{\mathrm{stk}})\leq(\kappa_{Q,\mathrm{rcs}}+1)\cdot|\pi^{(i)}|
.

The Initialization and Update Time.

They have the same bounds as those in
Theorem

11.2
.

Path Unfolding.

Note that
G_{\mathrm{stk}}
is the union of the emulator
Q
and some original edges
E_{\mathrm{heavy}}
. Therefore, given a path
P_{G_{\mathrm{stk}}}
connecting some
u,v\in V(G)
, we just decompose it into original edges and subpaths totally on
Q
, and then unfold each
Q
-subpath to a path on
G
using
Theorem

11.2
.

∎

12.2
Online-Batch Dynamic Distance Oracles from Stacking

12.3
.

Let
G
be a graph under
t
batched updates
\pi^{(1)},...,\pi^{(t)}
of edge deletions and edge insertions. There is an oracle supporting the following query. Given two vertices
u,v\in V(G)
, if
u
and
v
are disconnected in
G
, the oracle answers they are disconnected. Otherwise, the oracle answers an approximation
\widetilde{d}
of
\mathrm{dist}_{G}(u,v)
s.t.
\mathrm{dist}_{G}(u,v)\leq\widetilde{d}\leq\lambda_{\mathrm{DO},\alpha}\cdot\mathrm{dist}_{G}(u,v)
.
The approximation ratio

\lambda_{\mathrm{DO},\alpha}=O(\lambda_{\mathrm{LowDO},\alpha})\cdot(\lambda_{\mathrm{stk},\mathrm{low}}\cdot\lambda_{\mathrm{stk},\mathrm{up}})^{\lambda_{x}}\cdot 2^{O(1/\epsilon^{4})},

where
\lambda_{x}=O(1/\epsilon)
is a sufficiently large global parameter. Furthermore, the algorithm can output a path
P
connecting
u
and
v
on
G
with
\ell_{G}(P)\leq\lambda_{\mathrm{DO},\alpha}\cdot\mathrm{dist}_{G}(u,v)
.

The initialization time is
|G^{(0)}|\cdot n^{O(\epsilon)}
, the update time for batch
\pi^{(i)}
is
|\pi^{(i)}|\cdot n^{O(\epsilon)}
, the distance query time is
O(\log\log n/\epsilon^{4})
, and the additional path-reporting time is
O(|P|)
.

Proof.

The Oracle.

We pick a parameter
h=n^{\epsilon}
and construct a chain
{\cal G}
of
\bar{x}=\lambda_{x}
many graphs
G_{1},G_{2},...,G_{\bar{x}}
with the following properties, where
\lambda_{x}=O(1/\epsilon)
is a sufficiently large global parameter.

(1)

G_{1}=G
is the original graph.

(2)

For each
2\leq x\leq\bar{x}
,
G_{x}
is the distance-reduced graph from applying
Lemma

12.2
on
G_{x-1}
with length parameter
h
.

12.4
.

For the last graph
G_{\bar{x}}
, we have
\mathrm{dist}_{G_{\bar{x}}}(u,v)\leq h
for all connected
u,v\in V(G)
.

Proof.

Consider an arbitrary pair of connected vertices
u
and
v
. Note that
\mathrm{dist}_{G}(u,v)=\mathrm{poly}(n)
because we assume the edge lengths are all polynomial and the graph
G
is connected. For each
1\leq x\leq\bar{x}-1
, we have the following by
Lemma

12.2
.

•

If
\mathrm{dist}_{G_{x}}(u,v)>h
, then
\mathrm{dist}_{G_{x+1}}(u,v)\leq\lambda_{\mathrm{stk},\mathrm{up}}\cdot\mathrm{dist}_{G_{x}}(u,v)/h
.

•

If
\mathrm{dist}_{G_{x}}(u,v)\leq h
, then
\mathrm{dist}_{G_{x+1}}(u,v)\leq\lambda_{\mathrm{stk},\mathrm{up}}\leq h
because
\lambda_{\mathrm{stk},\mathrm{up}}=2^{\mathrm{poly}(1/\epsilon)}
and
h=n^{\epsilon}
.

Therefore, if there is some
1\leq x\leq\bar{x}-1
s.t.
\mathrm{dist}_{G_{x}}(u,v)\leq h
, we have
\mathrm{dist}_{G_{\bar{x}}}(u,v)\leq h
. Otherwise, we have
\mathrm{dist}_{G_{\bar{x}}}(u,v)\leq(\lambda_{\mathrm{stk},\mathrm{up}}/h)^{\lambda_{x}-1}\cdot\mathrm{dist}_{G}(x,y)\leq\mathrm{poly}(n)/(n^{\epsilon})^{\lambda_{x}-1}\leq h
because
\lambda_{x}=O(1/\epsilon)
is sufficiently large.

∎

Next, for each
G_{x}
, we construct several low-distance oracles with different length thresholds. Precisely, for each
1\leq y\leq\bar{y}=\lceil\log h\rceil+1
, let
h_{y}=2^{y}
and we construct a low-distance oracle
{\cal O}_{x,y}
by applying
Theorem

10.1
on
G_{x}
with length parameter
h_{y}
.

Note that
{\cal G}
and its low-distance oracles
\{{\cal O}_{x,y}\}
can be maintain under batched updates by
Lemma

12.2
and
Theorem

10.1
. Precisely, suppose
G_{x}
is under batched updates
\pi_{x}^{(1)},...,\pi_{x}^{(t)}
(the first graph
G_{1}
has
(\pi_{1}^{(1)},...,\pi_{1}^{(t)})=(\pi^{(1)},...,\pi^{(t)})
), we maintain
{\cal O}_{x,y}
for each
1\leq y\leq\bar{y}
under
\pi^{(1)}_{x},...,\pi^{(t)}_{x}
by
Theorem

10.1
. Also, we maintain
G_{x+1}
under
\pi^{(1)}_{x},...,\pi^{(t)}_{x}
by
Lemma

12.2
, which will generate the batched updates
\pi^{(1)}_{x+1},...,\pi^{(t)}_{x+1}
for
G_{x+1}
s.t.
|\pi^{(i)}_{x+1}|\leq\kappa_{\mathrm{stk},\mathrm{rcs}}\cdot|\pi^{(i)}_{x}|
for each
1\leq i\leq t
. From this, we have
|\pi^{(i)}_{x}|\leq(\kappa_{\mathrm{stk},\mathrm{rcs}})^{x-1}\cdot|\pi^{(i)}|
for each
1\leq x\leq\bar{x}
.

The Initialization and Update Time.

First we bound the size of graphs
G_{x}
. At any time, we have
|G_{x+1}|\leq\kappa_{\mathrm{stk},\mathrm{size}}\cdot|G_{x}|
for each
1\leq x\leq\bar{x}-1
. Simply we have
|G_{x}|\leq\kappa_{\mathrm{stk},\mathrm{size}}^{\lambda_{x}}\cdot|G|\leq n^{O(\epsilon)}\cdot|G|
for all
x
, because
\kappa_{\mathrm{stk},\mathrm{size}}=n^{O(\epsilon^{4})}
.

For the initialization, the construction all graphs in
{\cal G}
takes total time
\sum_{1\leq x\leq\bar{x}}|G_{x}^{(0)}|\cdot\mathrm{poly}(h)\cdot n^{O(\epsilon)}
,
and the construction of all low-distance oracles
\{{\cal O}_{x,y}\}
takes total time
\sum_{1\leq x\leq\bar{x}}\sum_{1\leq y\leq\bar{y}}|G^{(0)}_{x}|\cdot\mathrm{poly}(h)\cdot n^{O(\epsilon)}
.
Hence the total initialization time is
|G^{(0)}|\cdot n^{O(\epsilon)}
because
h=n^{\Theta(\epsilon)},\bar{x}\leq\lambda_{h}=O(1/\epsilon)
and
\bar{y}=O(\log h)=O(\log n)
.

The update time to handle each
\pi^{(i)}
is bounded by
\sum_{1\leq x\leq\bar{x}}|\pi^{(i)}_{x}|\cdot\mathrm{poly}(h)\cdot n^{O(\epsilon)}=|\pi^{(i)}|\cdot n^{O(\epsilon)}
, because
|\pi^{(i)}_{x}|\leq(\kappa_{\mathrm{stk},\mathrm{rcs}})^{\lambda_{x}}\cdot|\pi^{(i)}|=|\pi^{(i)}|\cdot n^{O(\epsilon)}
(this is from
\kappa_{\mathrm{stk},\mathrm{rcs}}=n^{O(\epsilon^{2})}
and
\lambda_{x}=O(1/\epsilon)
)

The Query Algorithm.

Suppose a query
(u,v)
arrives right after the update
\pi^{(i)}
. We will query
{\cal G}^{(i)}
and
\{{\cal O}^{(i)}_{x,y}\}
at that moment, and for simplicity, we omit the superscript
(i)
in what follows.

The algorithm is as foolows.

1.

We first find the minimum
x^{\star}
s.t.
{\cal O}_{x^{\star},\bar{y}}
declares
\mathrm{dist}_{G_{x^{\star}}}(u,v)\leq\lambda_{\mathrm{LowDO},\alpha}\cdot h_{\bar{y}}
but for each
1\leq x\leq x^{\star}-1
,
{\cal O}_{x^{\star},\bar{y}}
declares
\mathrm{dist}_{G_{x}}(u,v)>h_{\bar{y}}
. Note that if
u
and
v
are connected, such
x^{\star}
must exist because
\mathrm{dist}_{G_{\bar{x}}}(u,v)\leq h\leq h_{\bar{y}}
by
12.4
. If we cannot find such
x^{\star}
, we can safely declare
u
and
v
are disconnected.

2.

Next, we use a binary search to find a
y^{\star}\in[1,\bar{y}]
s.t.
{\cal O}_{x^{\star},y^{\star}}
declares
\mathrm{dist}_{G_{x^{\star}}}(u,v)\leq\lambda_{\mathrm{LowDO},\alpha}\cdot h_{y^{\star}}
and if
y^{\star}\geq 2
,
{\cal O}_{x^{\star},y^{\star}-1}
declares
\mathrm{dist}_{G_{x^{\star}}}(u,v)>h_{y^{\star}-1}
.

3.

The output is
\widetilde{d}=h^{x^{\star}-1}\cdot h_{y^{\star}}\cdot(\lambda_{\mathrm{stk},\mathrm{low}}^{x^{\star}-1}\cdot\lambda_{\mathrm{LowDO},\alpha})
.

The Correctness and the Query Time.

The correctness of the query algorithm is given by
Lemma

12.5
. The total query time is
O((\lambda_{x}+\log\bar{y})\cdot\lambda_{k}\cdot t)=O(\log\log n/\epsilon^{4})
by the following reasons. Step 1 takes
O(\lambda_{x}\cdot\lambda_{k}\cdot t)=O(1/\epsilon^{4})
because we make at most
\bar{x}=\lambda_{x}=O(1/\epsilon)
queries to low-distance oracles, each of which takes
O(\lambda_{k}\cdot t)=O(1/\epsilon^{3})
time by
Theorem

10.1
. Step 2 takes
O(\log\bar{y}\cdot\bar{k}\cdot t)
time because the binary search query the low-distance oracles at most
O(\log\bar{y})=O(\log\log n/\epsilon^{4})
times and
\bar{y}=O(\log h)=O(\epsilon\cdot\log n)
.

12.5
.

If
u
and
v
are connected, the approximate distance
\widetilde{d}
satisfies

\mathrm{dist}_{G}(u,v)\leq\widetilde{d}\leq 2\cdot\lambda_{\mathrm{stk},\mathrm{up}}^{x^{\star}-1}\cdot\lambda_{\mathrm{stk},\mathrm{low}}^{x^{\star}-1}\cdot\lambda_{\mathrm{LowDO},\alpha}\cdot\mathrm{dist}_{G}(u,v).

Proof.

First, we bound
\mathrm{dist}_{G^{\star}}(u,v)
using
\mathrm{dist}_{G}(u,v)
. For each
1\leq x\leq x^{\star}-1
, we know
{\cal O}_{x,\bar{y}}
declared
\mathrm{dist}_{G_{x}}(u,v)>h_{\bar{y}}\geq h
. Therefore, from
Lemma

12.2
, we have

\mathrm{dist}_{G_{x+1}}(u,v)\leq\lambda_{\mathrm{stk},\mathrm{up}}\cdot\max\{\mathrm{dist}_{G_{x}}(u,v)/h,1\}=\lambda_{\mathrm{stk},\mathrm{up}}\cdot\mathrm{dist}_{G_{x}}(u,v)/h
,
which implies

\mathrm{dist}_{G_{x^{\star}}}(u,v)\cdot h^{x^{\star}-1}\leq\lambda_{\mathrm{stk},\mathrm{up}}^{x^{\star}-1}\cdot\mathrm{dist}_{G}(u,v).

On the lower bound side, we have for each
1\leq x\leq x^{\star}-1
,

\mathrm{dist}_{G_{x}}(u,v)\leq\lambda_{\mathrm{stk},\mathrm{low}}\cdot h\cdot\mathrm{dist}_{G_{x+1}}(u,v)
,
so

\mathrm{dist}_{G_{x^{\star}}}(u,v)\cdot h^{x^{\star}-1}\geq\mathrm{dist}_{G}(u,v)/\lambda_{\mathrm{stk},\mathrm{low}}^{x^{\star}-1}.

Next, we bound
\mathrm{dist}_{G^{\star}}(u,v)
using
h_{y^{\star}}
. On the lower bound side, we have

\mathrm{dist}_{G_{x^{\star}}}(u,v)\geq h_{y^{\star}}/2

because if
y^{\star}\geq 2
,
{\cal O}_{x^{\star},y^{\star}-1}
will declare
\mathrm{dist}_{G_{x^{\star}}}(u,v)>h_{y^{\star}-1}=h_{y^{\star}}/2
, and if
y^{\star}=1
, we still have
\mathrm{dist}_{G_{x^{\star}}}(u,v)\geq 1=h_{y^{\star}}/2
, since all edges have positive weights and
h_{1}=2
. On the upper bound side,
{\cal O}_{x^{\star},y^{\star}}
declares

\mathrm{dist}_{G_{x^{\star}}}(u,v)\leq\lambda_{\mathrm{LowDO},\alpha}\cdot h_{y^{\star}}.

Combining the above bounds of
\mathrm{dist}_{G_{x^{\star}}}(u,v)
, we have

\mathrm{dist}_{G}(u,v)/(\lambda_{\mathrm{stk},\mathrm{low}}^{x^{\star}-1}\cdot\lambda_{\mathrm{LowDO},\alpha})\leq h_{y^{\star}}\cdot h^{x^{\star}-1}\leq 2\cdot\lambda_{\mathrm{stk},\mathrm{up}}^{x^{\star}-1}\cdot\mathrm{dist}_{G}(u,v).

∎

Path Reporting.

Let
u,v\in V(G)
be the input vertices. Because
{\cal O}_{x^{\star},y^{\star}}
declares
\mathrm{dist}_{G_{x^{\star}}}(u,v)\leq\lambda_{\mathrm{LowDO},\alpha}\cdot h_{y^{\star}}
, by
Theorem

10.1
, we can obtain a
u
-
v
path
P_{G_{x^{\star}}}
on
G_{x^{\star}}
with
\ell_{G_{x^{\star}}}(P_{G_{x^{\star}}})\leq\lambda_{\mathrm{LowDO},\alpha}\cdot h_{y^{\star}}
in
2^{O(\lambda_{k})}\cdot|P_{G_{x^{\star}}}|
time. Next, we iterate
x
from
x^{\star}-1
to
1
. For each
x
, by the path unfolding algorithm in
Lemma

12.2
(with input
P_{G_{x+1}}
), we obtain a
u
-
v
path
P_{G_{x}}
on
G_{x}
with
\ell_{G_{x}}(P_{G_{x}})\leq\lambda_{\mathrm{stk},\mathrm{low}}\cdot h\cdot\ell_{G_{x+1}}(P_{G_{x+1}})
and
|P_{G_{x}}|\geq|P_{G_{x+1}}|/2^{O(\lambda_{k})}
in time
2^{O(\lambda_{k})}\cdot|P_{G_{x}}|
.

At last, we obtain a
u
-
v
path
P_{G}=P_{G_{1}}
on
G
with
\ell_{G}(P_{G})\leq\lambda_{\mathrm{stk},\mathrm{low}}^{x^{\star}-1}\cdot\lambda_{\mathrm{LowDO},\alpha}\cdot h^{x^{\star}-1}\cdot h_{y^{\star}}
. From the proof of
Lemma

12.5
, we have
h_{y^{\star}}\leq 2\cdot\lambda_{\mathrm{stk},\mathrm{low}}^{x^{\star}-1}/h^{x^{\star}-1}\cdot\mathrm{dist}_{G}(u,v)
. Therefore,
\ell_{G}(P_{G})\leq 2\cdot\lambda_{\mathrm{LowDO},\alpha}\cdot(\lambda_{\mathrm{stk},\mathrm{low}}\cdot\lambda_{\mathrm{stk},\mathrm{up}})^{x^{\star}-1}\cdot\mathrm{dist}_{G}(u,v)
. The path-reporting time can be bounded by
\sum_{1\leq x\leq x^{\star}}2^{O(1/\epsilon^{4})}\cdot|P_{G_{x}}|\leq 2^{O(1/\epsilon^{4})}\cdot 2^{O(\lambda_{k}\cdot\lambda_{x})}\cdot|P_{G}|
because each
P_{G_{x}}
has
|P_{G_{x}}|\leq P_{G}\cdot 2^{O(\lambda_{x}\cdot\lambda_{k})}
and
\lambda_{x}\cdot\lambda_{k}=O(1/\epsilon^{3})
.

We note that, when
P_{G}
is not required to be a simple path, it is trivial to move the overhead on path-reporting time to approximation. For example, we can define a new
u
-
v
path
\widetilde{P}_{G}
by walking along
P_{G}
but going back and forth on each edge
2^{O(\lambda_{k}\cdot\lambda_{x})}
times. Then we have
\ell_{G}(\widetilde{P}_{G})\leq 2^{O(1/\epsilon^{4})}\cdot\ell_{G}(P_{G})\leq\lambda_{\mathrm{DO},\alpha}\cdot\mathrm{dist}_{G}(u,v)
. Moreover, because
|\widetilde{P}_{G}|=2^{O(1/\epsilon^{4})}\cdot|P_{G}|
, we can rewrite the path-reporting time to be
O(|\widetilde{P}_{G}|)
.

∎

12.3
Fully Dynamic Algorithms from Online-Batch Algorithms

The last step is to turn our online-batch dynamic distance oracle to a fully dynamic one. This can be done by a standard online-batch-to-fully-dynamic technique, which was implicitly shown in
[
NSW17
]
and formalized in
[
JS22
]
afterwards.

12.6
Theorem 10.1 in
[
JS22
]
, Section 5 in
[
NSW17
]
.

Let
G
be a graph undergoing batch updates. Assume for two parameters
\zeta
and
w
, there is a preprocessing algorithm with preprocessing time
t_{\rm preprocess}
and an update algorithm with amortized update time
t_{\rm amortized}
for a data structure
{\cal D}
in the online-batch setting with batch number
\zeta
and sensitivity
w
, where
t_{\rm preprocess}
and
t_{\rm amortized}
are functions the map the upper bounds of some graph measures throughout the update, e.g. maximum number of edges, to non-negative numbers.

Then for any
\xi\leq\zeta
satisfying
w\geq 2\cdot 6^{\xi}
, there is a fully dynmaic algorithm with preprocessing time
O(2^{\xi}\cdot t_{\rm preprocess})
and worst case update time
O(4^{\xi}\cdot(t_{\rm preprocess}/w+w^{(1/\xi)}\cdot t_{\rm amortized}))
to maintain a set of
O(2^{\xi})
instances of the data structure
{\cal D}
such that after each update, the update algorithm specifies one of the maintained data structure instances satisfying the following conditions

(1)

The specified data structure instances is for the up-to-date graph.

(2)

The online-batch update algorithm is executed for at most
\xi
times on the specified data structure instance with each update batch of size at most
w
.

The sensitivity
w
is the maximum size of each update batch allowed by the online-batch dynamic algorithm. For our online-batch dynamic distance oracle in
Theorem

12.3
, there is no such restriction and we can simply choose
w=n^{2}
. Recall that we mentioned in
Section

3.3
that we will set the batch number
\zeta=t=\Theta(1/\epsilon)
. Furthermore, we pick
\xi=\zeta=\Theta(1/\epsilon)
.

By
Theorem

12.3
, our online-batch dynamic distance oracle algorithm has preprocessing time
t_{\rm preprocess}=|G^{(0)}|\cdot n^{O(\epsilon)}=n^{2+O(\epsilon)}
. An online-batch dynamic algorithm has amortized update time
t_{\rm amortized}
if it can handle each update batch
\pi^{(i)}
in time
|\pi^{(i)}|\cdot t_{\rm amortized}
. By this definition,
Theorem

12.3
has
t_{\rm amortized}=n^{O(\epsilon)}
.

Therefore, by
Lemma

12.6
, we can obtain a fully dynamic distance oracle algorithm with initialization time
O(2^{\xi}\cdot|G^{(0)}|\cdot n^{O(\epsilon)})=|G^{(0)}|\cdot n^{O(\epsilon)}
(we can check in the proof of
Lemma

12.6
in
[
JS22
]
that the initialization time only depends on the initial graph size), worst-case update time
O(4^{\xi}\cdot(t_{\rm preprocess}/w+w^{(1/\xi)}\cdot t_{\rm amortized}))=n^{O(\epsilon)}
, the same query time
O(\log\log n/\epsilon^{4})
and the same path-reporting time
O(|P|)
. This completes the proof of
Theorem

12.1
except the part about reporting simple paths, which will be included in
Section

12.4
.

12.4
Report Simple Paths

In this section, we will discuss how to ensure the path reported by the oracle is simple, while keeping the path-reporting time linear (with an
n^{O(\epsilon)}
overhead actually) to the number of edges in this path. Note that, after we obtain a non-simple
u
-
v
path
P
from the oracle, we cannot just pick an arbitrary
u
-
v
simple path
P_{\mathrm{sp}}
on
P
as the output, because
|P|
can be much larger than
|P_{\mathrm{sp}}|
, and then the path-reporting time
O(|P|)
cannot be bounded by
|P_{\mathrm{sp}}|\cdot n^{O(\epsilon)}
. An algorithm for addressing this issue has been proposed by
[
Chu21
]
. We include it below for completeness, and integrate it into the oracle for modularity (the algorithm in
[
Chu21
]
is on the application side for using an oracle which may report non-simple paths).

Let
\alpha^{\star}=\lceil\lambda_{\mathrm{DO},\alpha}\rceil
be the approximation factor of our oracle (round it up to an integer). We restate the query interfaces of the oracle here. Let
u,v\in V(G)
be a given pair of vertices.

•

The oracle can return an integral value
\widetilde{d}
s.t.
\mathrm{dist}_{G}(u,v)\leq\widetilde{d}\leq\alpha^{\star}\cdot\mathrm{dist}_{G}(u,v)
. The distance query time is
O(\log\log n/\epsilon^{4})
.

•

The oracle can return a
u
-
v
path
P
on
G
s.t.
\mathrm{dist}_{G}(u,v)\leq\ell_{G}(P)\leq\alpha^{\star}\cdot\mathrm{dist}_{G}(u,v)
. The path-reporting time is
O(\log\log n/\epsilon^{4}+|P|)
(which includes the distance query time).

For each
1\leq y\leq\bar{y}=O(\log n)
(s.t.
2^{\bar{y}}
upper bounds the diameter of
G
over time) and
1\leq z\leq\bar{z}=\lceil 1/\epsilon\rceil+1
, let
d_{y}=2^{y}
and
L_{z}=\lceil n^{\epsilon}\rceil^{z}
, and we define
G_{y,z}
to be a dynamic graph exactly the same with
G
(undergoing the same sequence of updates) except that we increase the length of each edge (including initial edges and new edges) by
d_{y}/L_{z}
additionally. We maintain an oracle
{\cal O}_{y,z}
on each graph
G_{y,z}
, and also an oracle
{\cal O}
on the original graph
G
. Strictly speaking, our oracle required the input dynamic graph has positive integral edge lengths, and we can ensure this by scaling up the edge lengths of
G_{y,z}
(and then removing this scaling in the answers).

Let
u,v\in V(G)
be the input of the path-reporting query, and let
\widetilde{d}
be the approximate

u
-
v
distance on
G
returned by
{\cal O}
. The algorithm has at most
\bar{z}
phases (from phase
1
to phase
\bar{z}
, but it might terminate at some intermediate phase). Let
z
be the current phase. At the beginning of phase
z
, we have an invariant that any
u
-
v
path
P
on
G
with
\ell_{G}(P)\leq\widetilde{d}\cdot(10\cdot\alpha^{\star})^{\bar{z}-z+1}
has
|P|\geq L_{z-1}
(we define
L_{0}=1
). For the first phase, this invariant trivially holds, and for each phase
z\geq 2
, this invariant is guaranteed by phase
z-1
as we will see.

Let
y
be the minimum s.t.
d_{y}\geq\widetilde{d}\cdot(10\cdot\alpha^{\star})^{\bar{z}-z}
, which implies
d_{y}\leq 2\cdot\widetilde{d}\cdot(10\cdot\alpha^{\star})^{\bar{z}-z}
. We query the oracle
{\cal O}_{y,z}
with input
(u,v)
, and it will return a path
P_{y,z}
on
G_{y,z}
, which corresponds to a path on
G
.

•

Suppose
\ell_{G_{y,z}}(P_{y,z})\leq 2\cdot\alpha^{\star}\cdot d_{y}
. Then we know
|P_{y,z}|\leq 2\cdot\alpha^{\star}\cdot L_{z}
because each edge on
G_{y,z}
has an addition length increasing
d_{y}/L_{z}
. We take an arbitrary
u
-
v
simple path
P_{\mathrm{sp}}
on
P_{y,z}
. Trivially
\ell_{G}(P_{\mathrm{sp}})\leq\ell_{G}(P_{y,z})\leq\ell_{G_{y,z}}(P_{y,z})\leq 2\cdot\alpha^{\star}\cdot d_{y}\leq 4\cdot\alpha^{\star}\cdot\widetilde{d}\cdot(10\cdot\alpha^{\star})^{\bar{z}-z}
. Therefore, by the invariant, we know
|P_{\mathrm{sp}}|\geq L_{z-1}
. We terminate the algorithm with output
P_{\mathrm{sp}}
.

•

Otherwise, we have
\ell_{G_{y,z}}(P_{y,z})>2\cdot\alpha^{\star}\cdot d_{y}
, which implies
\mathrm{dist}_{G_{y,z}}(u,v)\geq\ell_{G_{y,z}}(P_{y,z})/\alpha^{\star}>2\cdot d_{y}
from the approximation guarantee of the oracle. Now we show that the invariant of phase
z+1
holds, i.e. an arbitrary
u
-
v
path
P
with
\ell_{G}(P)\leq\widetilde{d}\cdot(10\cdot\alpha^{\star})^{\bar{z}-z}
has
|P|\geq L_{z}
. Assume
|P|<L_{z}
for contradiction. Then we have
\ell_{G_{y,z}}(P)\leq\ell_{G}(P)+d_{y}\cdot|P|/L_{z}\leq\widetilde{d}\cdot(10\cdot\alpha^{\star})^{\bar{z}-z}+d_{y}\leq 2\cdot d_{y}
, a contradiction.

The algorithm must finally output some
u
-
v
simple path
P_{\mathrm{sp}}
before the last phase
\bar{z}
because the invariant at the last phase cannot hold, since the shortest
u
-
v
path
P
on
G
has
\ell_{G}(P)=\mathrm{dist}_{G}(u,v)\leq\widetilde{d}\cdot(10\cdot\alpha^{\star})
and
|P|\leq n-1<L_{\bar{z}-1}
.

Let
z^{\star}
be the phase at which
P_{\mathrm{sp}}
is obtained. Then
\ell_{G}(P_{\mathrm{sp}})\leq 4\cdot\alpha^{\star}\cdot\widetilde{d}\cdot(10\cdot\alpha^{\star})^{\bar{z}-z^{\star}}\leq 4\cdot(\alpha^{\star})^{2}\cdot(10\cdot\alpha^{\star})^{\bar{z}}\cdot\mathrm{dist}_{G}(u,v)\leq 2^{\mathrm{poly}(1/\epsilon)}\cdot\mathrm{dist}_{G}(u,v)
. The total path-report time is
\sum_{1\leq z\leq z^{\star}}O(|P_{y,z}|)\leq\sum_{1\leq z\leq z^{\star}}O(2\cdot\alpha^{\star}\cdot L_{z})\leq|P_{\mathrm{sp}}|\cdot n^{O(\epsilon)}
where the last inequality is because for each
1\leq z\leq z^{\star}
,
|L_{z}|\leq\lceil n^{\epsilon}\rceil\cdot L_{z^{\star}-1}\leq|P_{\mathrm{sp}}|\cdot n^{O(\epsilon)}
. Maintaining all the oracles
{\cal O}
and
{\cal O}_{y,z}
will only increase the initialization time and the update time by a factor of
O(\bar{y}\cdot\bar{z})=O(\log n/\epsilon)
, which is negligible.

13
Extension: Dynamic Vertex Sparsifiers for All Distances

In this section, we generalize our results about dynamic vertex sparsifiers in
Section

8
. The theorem below restates
Theorem

1.8

13.1
.

Let
G
be a fully dynamic graph with a terminal set
T\subseteq V(G)
undergoing a sequence of edge insertions/deletions, isolated vertex insertions/deletions and terminal insertions/deletions. For
\alpha_{\mathrm{low}}=2^{\mathrm{poly}(1/\epsilon)}
and
\alpha_{\mathrm{up}}=2^{O(1/\epsilon^{3})}
, there is a deterministic algorithm that maintains either

1.

a graph
H
with size
|H|\leq|T|\cdot n^{O(\epsilon^{4})}
which is an
(\alpha_{\mathrm{low}},\alpha_{\mathrm{up}})
-sparsifier of
T
on
G
at all time, using
n^{O(\epsilon)}
amortized update time, or

2.

a collection of
2^{O(1/\epsilon)}
graphs
H_{1},H_{2},...,H_{2^{O(1/\epsilon)}}
with sizes at most
|T|\cdot n^{O(\epsilon^{4})}
using
n^{O(\epsilon)}
worst-case update time, such that at each moment
i
, one of these graphs is an
(\alpha_{\mathrm{low}},\alpha_{\mathrm{up}})
-sparsifier of
T
on
G
and it will be specified by the algorithm.

The initialization time is
|G^{(0)}|\cdot n^{O(\epsilon)}
.

13.2
.

Let
G
be an online-batch dynamic graph with a fully dynamic terminal set
T\subseteq V(G)
under
t
batches of updates
\pi^{(1)},...,\pi^{(t)}
of edge insertions/deletions, isolated vertex insertions/deletions and terminal insertions/deletions. Given parameters
h
, there is an algorithm that maintains an
(\alpha_{\mathrm{low}},\alpha_{\mathrm{up}},h)
-sparsifier
H
of
T
on
G
with size
|H|\leq O(\kappa_{H,\mathrm{size}}\cdot|T|)=|T|\cdot n^{O(\epsilon^{4})}
,

\alpha_{\mathrm{low}}=\lambda_{H,\mathrm{low}}^{\lambda_{k}}=2^{\mathrm{poly}(1/\epsilon)}\text{ and }\alpha_{\mathrm{up}}=\lambda_{H,\mathrm{up}}^{\lambda_{k}}=2^{O(1/\epsilon^{2})}

The initialization time is
|G^{(0)}|\cdot\mathrm{poly}(h)\cdot n^{O(\epsilon)}
and the update time for
\pi^{(i)}
is
|\pi^{(i)}|\cdot\mathrm{poly}(h)\cdot n^{O(\epsilon)}
.

Proof.

We fix a parameter
\phi=\kappa_{\phi}=n^{\epsilon^{2}}
and
\bar{k}=\lambda=O(1/\epsilon^{2})
to be sufficently large. For each
1\leq k\leq\bar{k}
, we define
h_{k}=h\cdot\lambda_{H,\mathrm{up}}^{k-1}
.
Our strategy is to construct and maintain a hierarchy of sparsifiers with
\bar{k}
levels by applying
Theorem

8.2
repeatedly. For each level
1\leq k\leq\bar{k}
, we let
H_{k}
and
T_{k}
denote the sparsifier and terminal set at this level. We will guarantee the invariant
T_{1}\supseteq T_{2}\supseteq...\supseteq T_{\bar{k}}\supseteq T
at any time.

Initially, let
H_{1}^{(0)}=G^{(0)}
and
T_{1}^{(0)}=T^{(0)}
be the first-level sparsifier and terminal set. At each level
1\leq k\leq\bar{k}-1
, we initialize a
(\lambda_{H,\mathrm{low}},\lambda_{H,\mathrm{up}},h_{k})
-sparsifier
H_{k+1}^{(0)}
of terminal set
T_{k}^{(0)}
on
H_{k}^{(0)}
via
Theorem

8.2
, and let
T_{k+1}^{(0)}=T^{(0)}
be the terminal set of the next level. When an update
\pi^{(i)}
comes, we update the hierarchy from the bottom up. Concretely, at each level
1\leq k\leq\bar{k}-1
, let
\pi^{(i)}_{k}
be the update at this level (the first-level update is
\pi^{(i)}_{1}=\pi^{(i)}
). Let
(\pi^{(i)}_{k})_{\mid T^{-}},(\pi^{(i)}_{k})_{\mid T^{+}}\subseteq\pi^{(i)}_{k}
denote the terminal deletions and insertions in
\pi^{(i)}_{k}
respectively. Let
\hat{\pi}^{(i)}_{k}=\pi^{(i)}_{k}\setminus(\pi^{(i)}_{k})_{\mid T^{-}}
represent edge insertions, edge deletions and terminal insertions in
\pi^{(i)}_{k}
.

•

If
|(\pi^{(i)}_{k})_{\mid T^{-}}|>\phi\cdot|H^{(i)}_{k}|
or
|\hat{\pi}^{(i)}_{k}|>\phi\cdot|H^{(i)}_{k}|
, we reinitialize the sparsifier and terminal set via
Theorem

8.2
at all level above. Concretely, for each level
k^{\prime}
s.t.
k\leq k^{\prime}\leq\bar{k}-1
, initialize a
(\lambda_{H,\mathrm{low}},\lambda_{H,\mathrm{up}},h_{k^{\prime}})
-sparsifier
H_{k^{\prime}+1}^{(i)}
of terminal set
T_{k^{\prime}}^{(i)}=T^{(i)}
on
H_{k^{\prime}}^{(i)}
via
Theorem

8.2
. The update algorithm at time
i
terminates after the reinitialization.

•

Otherwise, we have
|(\pi^{(i)}_{k})_{\mid T^{-}}|\leq\phi\cdot|H^{(i)}_{k}|
and
|\hat{\pi}^{(i)}_{k}|\leq\phi\cdot|H^{(i)}_{k}|
. We feed
\hat{\pi}^{(i)}_{k}
to the
Theorem

8.2
maintaining
H_{k+1}
(
|\hat{\pi}^{(i)}_{k}|\leq\phi\cdot|H^{(i)}_{k}|
meets the requirement of
Theorem

8.2
), it will update
H_{k+1}^{(i-1)}
to
H_{k+1}^{(i)}
s.t.
H_{k+1}^{(i)}
is a
(\lambda_{H,\mathrm{low}},\lambda_{H,\mathrm{up}},h_{k})
-sparisifer of
T_{k}^{(i)}=T_{k}^{(i-1)}\cup(\pi^{(i)}_{k})_{\mid T^{+}}
on
H_{k}^{(i)}
. Furthermore, let
\bar{\pi}_{k}^{(i)}
be the batched update that updates
H_{k}^{(i-1)}
to
H_{k}^{(i)}
. We define the batched update at the next level be
\pi_{k+1}^{(i)}=\bar{\pi}_{k}^{(i)}\cup(\pi_{k}^{(i)})_{\mid T^{+}}\cup(\pi_{k}^{(i)})_{\mid T^{-}}
.

Quality of the Highest Sparsifier
H=H_{\bar{k}}
.

First,
T\subseteq V(H)
holds because we indeed have the invariant
T_{1}\supseteq T_{2}\supseteq...T_{\bar{k}}\supseteq T
at any time by the algorithm.

Second, we show that for each
u,v\in T
, we have
\mathrm{dist}_{H}(u,v)\cdot\alpha_{\mathrm{low}}\geq\mathrm{dist}_{G}(u,v)
, where
\alpha_{\mathrm{low}}=\lambda_{H,\mathrm{low}}^{\bar{k}-1}=2^{\mathrm{poly}(1/\epsilon)}
. The reason is as follows. For each level

1\leq k\leq\bar{k}-1
, because
T\subseteq T_{k}
and
H_{k+1}
is a
(\lambda_{H,\mathrm{low}},\lambda_{H,\mathrm{up}},h_{k})
-sparsifier of
T_{k}
on
H_{k}
, we have
\mathrm{dist}_{H_{k+1}}(u,v)\cdot\lambda_{H,\mathrm{low}}\geq\mathrm{dist}_{H_{k}}(u,v)
. Therefore,
\mathrm{dist}_{H}(u,v)\cdot\alpha_{\mathrm{low}}\geq\mathrm{dist}_{G}(u,v)
.

Third, for each
u,v\in T
s.t.
\mathrm{dist}_{G}(u,v)\leq h
, we have
\mathrm{dist}_{H}(u,v)\leq\alpha_{\mathrm{up}}\cdot\mathrm{dist}_{G}(u,v)
, where
\alpha_{\mathrm{up}}=\lambda_{H,\mathrm{up}}^{\bar{k}-1}=2^{O(1/\epsilon^{2})}
, by the following reason. At each level
1\leq k\leq\bar{k}-1
,
\mathrm{dist}_{H_{k+1}}(u,v)\leq\lambda_{H,\mathrm{up}}\cdot\mathrm{dist}_{H_{k}}(u,v)
for each
u,v\in T\subseteq T_{k}
s.t.
\mathrm{dist}_{H_{k}}(u,v)\leq h_{k}=h\cdot\lambda_{H,\mathrm{up}}^{k-1}
. This implies for each
u,v\in T
,
\mathrm{dist}_{H_{k}}(u,v)\leq h\cdot\mathrm{dist}_{G}(u,v)\cdot\lambda_{H,\mathrm{up}}^{k-1}
at each level
1\leq k\leq\bar{k}
, and in particular,
\mathrm{dist}_{H}(u,v)=\mathrm{dist}_{H_{\bar{k}}}(u,v)\leq\alpha_{\mathrm{up}}\cdot\mathrm{dist}_{G}(u,v)
.

The size of
H
is
|H|\leq O(\kappa_{H,\mathrm{size}}\cdot|T|)
by the following reason. For each level
1\leq k\leq\bar{k}-1
, we have

\displaystyle|H_{k+1}|

\displaystyle\leq\kappa_{H,\mathrm{size}}\cdot(|T_{k}|+\phi\cdot|H_{k}|)

\displaystyle\leq\kappa_{H,\mathrm{size}}\cdot(|T|+(\phi+3t\phi\cdot(1+\phi)^{t})\cdot|H_{k}|)

\displaystyle\leq\kappa_{H,\mathrm{size}}\cdot|T|+|H_{k}|/n^{\Theta(\epsilon^{2})},

where the second inequality is by
13.3
and the third inequality is by
\phi=n^{\epsilon^{2}}
. This implies
|H_{k+1}|\leq O(\kappa_{H,\mathrm{size}}\cdot|T|)+|G|/(n^{\Theta(\epsilon^{2})})^{k}
. Because
\bar{k}=O(1/\epsilon^{2})
is sufficiently large, we have
|H_{\bar{k}}|\leq O(\kappa_{H,\mathrm{size}}\cdot|T|)
.

13.3
.

For each level
1\leq k\leq\bar{k}-1
and each moment
0\leq i\leq t
, we have
|T_{k}^{(i)}|\leq|T^{(i)}|+3t\phi\cdot(1+\phi)^{t}|H_{k}^{(i)}|
.

Proof.

Let
i^{\star}\leq i
be the latest moment that
H_{k+1}^{(i^{\star})}
is obtained by (re)initialization. Then by the algorithm, we have
T_{k}^{(i^{\star})}=T^{(i^{\star})}
. Consider any moment
i^{\prime}
s.t.
i^{\star}+1\leq i^{\prime}\leq i
, we have
|(\pi^{(i^{\prime})}_{k})_{\mid T^{-}}|\leq\phi\cdot|H^{(i^{\prime})}_{k}|
and
|\hat{\pi}^{(i^{\prime})}_{k}|\leq\phi\cdot|H^{(i^{\prime})}_{k}|
, which implies
|\pi^{(i^{\prime})}_{k}|\leq 2\phi\cdot|H^{(i^{\prime})}_{k}|
.
Obviously, the difference between
|T^{(i)}|
and
|T^{(i^{\star})}|
is bounded by the total size of batched updates during this period. Formally, we have

\displaystyle|T^{(i)}|\geq|T^{(i^{\star})}|-\sum_{i^{\star}+1\leq i^{\prime}\leq i}|\pi^{(i^{\prime})}_{k}|\geq|T^{(i^{\star})}|-\sum_{i^{\star}+1\leq i^{\prime}\leq i}2\phi\cdot|H^{(i^{\prime})}_{k}|.

Similarly, the difference between
|T^{(i)}_{k}|
and
|T^{(i^{\star})}_{k}|
is bounded by

\displaystyle|T^{(i)}_{k}|\leq|T^{(i^{\star})}_{k}|+\sum_{i^{\star}+1\leq i^{\prime}\leq i}|\hat{\pi}^{(i^{\prime})}_{k}|\leq|T^{(i^{\star})}_{k}|+\sum_{i^{\star}+1\leq i^{\prime}\leq i}\phi\cdot|H^{(i^{\prime})}_{k}|.

Next, we bound
|H^{(i^{\prime})}_{k}|
in terms of
|H^{(i)}_{k}|
. For each
i^{\star}+1\leq i^{\prime}\leq i
, we have
|H^{(i^{\prime}-1)}_{k}|\leq|H^{(i^{\prime})}_{k}|+|\hat{\pi}^{(i^{\prime})}_{k}|\leq(1+1/\phi)|H^{(i^{\prime})}_{k}|
because
\hat{\pi}^{(i^{\prime})}_{k}
is the batched update from
H^{(i^{\prime}-1)}_{k}
to
H^{(i^{\prime})}_{k}
. This means

|H^{(i^{\prime})}_{k}|\leq(1+\phi)^{i-i^{\prime}}\cdot|H^{(i)}_{k}|\leq(1+\phi)^{t}\cdot|H^{(i)}_{k}|.

Putting all together, we have
|T^{(i)}_{k}|\leq|T^{(i)}|+3t\phi(1+\phi)^{t}\cdot|H^{(i)}_{k}|
.

∎

The Running Time.

The initialization time is

\sum_{k}(|H_{k}^{(0)}|+|T^{(0)}|/\phi)\cdot\mathrm{poly}(h_{k})\cdot n^{O(\epsilon)}/\phi=|G^{(0)}|\cdot\mathrm{poly}(h)\cdot n^{O(\epsilon)}.

Now consider the batched update
\pi^{(i)}
at time
i
, and let
k^{\star}
be the minimum level that a reinitialization is triggered. For each level
1\leq k\leq k^{\star}-2
, we have
|\pi^{(i)}_{k+1}|\leq|\bar{\pi}^{(i)}_{k+1}|+|\pi^{(i)}_{k}|\leq O(\kappa_{H,\mathrm{rcs}}\cdot|\pi^{(i)}_{k}|)\leq O(\kappa_{H,\mathrm{rcs}})^{k}\cdot|\pi^{(i)}|\leq n^{O(\epsilon)}\cdot|\pi^{(i)}|
. The total update time summing over all levels
1\leq k\leq k^{\star}-1
is

\sum_{1\leq k\leq k^{\star}-1}|\pi^{(i)}_{k}|\cdot\mathrm{poly}(h_{k})\cdot n^{O(\epsilon)}/\phi^{2}=|\pi^{(i)}|\cdot\mathrm{poly}(h)\cdot n^{O(\epsilon)}.

The total reinitialization time summing over all levels
k^{\star}\leq k\leq\bar{k}-1
is

\sum_{k^{\star}\leq k\leq\bar{k}-1}(|H_{k}^{(i)}|+|T_{k}^{(i)}|/\phi)\cdot\mathrm{poly}(h_{k})\cdot n^{O(\epsilon)}/\phi=|\pi^{(i)}|\cdot\mathrm{poly}(h)\cdot n^{O(\epsilon)},

because the reinitialization time is dominated by level

k^{\star}
, and we have
|\pi^{(i)}_{k^{\star}}|\geq\phi\cdot|H_{k^{\star}}^{(i)}|
,
|T_{k^{\star}}^{(i)}|\leq|H^{(i)}_{k^{\star}}|
and
|\pi^{(i)}_{k^{\star}}|\leq n^{O(\epsilon)}\cdot|\pi^{(i)}|
.

∎

13.4
.

Let
G
be an online-batch dynamic graph with a fully dynamic terminal set
T\subseteq V(G)
under
t
batches of updates
\pi^{(1)},...,\pi^{(t)}
of edge insertions/deletions, isolated vertex insertions/deletions, and terminal insertions/deletions. There is an algorithm that maintains an
(\alpha_{\mathrm{low}},\alpha_{\mathrm{up}})
-sparsifier
H
of
T
on
G
with
\alpha_{\mathrm{low}}=2^{\mathrm{poly}(1/\epsilon)}
,
\alpha_{\mathrm{up}}=2^{O(1/\epsilon^{3})}
, and size
|H|\leq O(\lambda_{x}\cdot\kappa_{H,\mathrm{size}}\cdot|T|)=|T|\cdot n^{O(\epsilon^{4})}
.
The initialization time is
|G^{(0)}|\cdot n^{O(\epsilon)}
and the update time for
\pi^{(i)}
is
|\pi^{(i)}|\cdot n^{O(\epsilon)}
.

Proof.

Fix
h=n^{\epsilon}
. We maintain the same chain
{\cal G}
of distance-reduced graphs
G_{1},G_{2},...,G_{\bar{x}}
as that in the proof of
Theorem

12.3
.

(1)

G_{1}=G
is the original graph.

(2)

For each
2\leq x\leq\bar{x}
,
G_{x}
is the distance-reduced graph from applying
Lemma

12.2
on
G_{x-1}
with length parameter
h
.

By setting
\bar{x}=\lambda_{x}=O(1/\epsilon)
to be sufficiently large,
12.4
states that the last graph
G_{\bar{x}}
has
\mathrm{dist}_{G_{\bar{x}}}(u,v)\leq h
for all
u,v\in V(G)
.

For each
1\leq x\leq\bar{x}
, we maintain an
(\alpha^{\prime}_{\mathrm{low}},\alpha^{\prime}_{\mathrm{up}},h)
-sparsifier
H^{\prime}_{x}
of
T
on
G_{x}
by applying
Lemma

13.2
, where
\alpha^{\prime}_{\mathrm{low}}=2^{\mathrm{poly}(1/\epsilon)}
,
\alpha^{\prime}_{\mathrm{up}}=2^{O(1/\epsilon^{2})}
and
|H^{\prime}_{x}|=O(\kappa_{H,\mathrm{size}}\cdot|T|)
. Let
H_{x}
be the graph from scaling up the length of all edges in
H^{\prime}_{x}
by a factor
h^{x-1}
.

We construct the final sparsifier
H
as follows. For each dynamic sparsifier
H_{x}
, we maintain a
fresh graph

H_{x,\mathrm{fresh}}
which is isomorphic to
H_{x}
over all time, but vertices in
H_{x,\mathrm{fresh}}
are
fresh vertices
. In other words,
H_{x,\mathrm{fresh}}
is obtained by assigning each vertex in
H_{x}
